# run-kit UI — Lenses & Surface Layout

## Web Tile (the `web` lens renderer)

`app/frontend/src/components/iframe-window.tsx` — the renderer for the **`web` surface/lens** (§ Window Views (Lens Model)), mounted as a tile by `SurfaceLayout` whenever the layout tree hosts a `web` leaf (§ Surface Layout). `IframeWindow` is the web tile's **chrome** — tab strip, address bar + submit ladder, find bar, zoom control, load progress line, error surface, onboarding panel, the document CustomEvent receivers — rendering each tab's content through one engine per tab behind the `WebFrameEngine` contract in `app/frontend/src/lib/web-frame-engine.ts` (§ Engine seam below); the chrome reads only the state an engine reports and drives it only through its registered handle. TWO engines sit behind that one contract: the **`iframe` engine** (every browser, PWA, and phone) and the **`native` engine** (the desktop shell's guest `WebContentsView`, selected when the shell carries the `web` bridge group and the viewer has not opted out — § The `native` engine below). The `web` surface is ALWAYS available (§ Window Views (Lens Model)) — the tile renders the window's **web-tab family**: `SurfaceLayout` feeds `tabs={webTabs ?? []}` and `active={webActive}` (overlaid by the store's optimistic web override while select/remove/move is in flight — § Optimistic select/remove/move below), and the component derives the active address itself (`webTabs[(webActive || 1) - 1] ?? ""`, clamped — the same value `activeWebUrl(win)` yields). Stored `@rk_win_web_<n>` values may be site-relative paths beyond `/present/` and `/proxy/`; the hash-addressed tutorial companion page (`/tutorial/tutorial.html#chN`) is the first shipped consumer, and each viewer resolves it against its own run-kit origin. A family with declared tabs mounts one engine PER TAB (the selected engine kind renders every tab — § Engine seam below); the strip ALWAYS renders with the tile — at 0 tabs/0 drafts it is just the trailing `+`, keeping the draft entry points reachable from the empty state; an empty family selects the tile's ONBOARDING content state below the strip (§ Onboarding state below); and an out-of-range active pointer clamps to the family, never to onboarding. Bottom bar is NOT rendered for a web tile (no terminal to send keys to). The component takes `tabs`/`active`, an optional `onWriteUrl: (url: string) => Promise<unknown>` address-bar write seam (the caller owns the slot index — `SurfaceLayout` binds it to the active `@rk_win_web_<n>` option write, n = `webActive` or 1 while the pointer is unset), optional strip verb callbacks (`onSelectTab(n)` / `onCloseTab(n)` / `onAddTab(target) → {index, existed}` / `onMoveTab(n, to)` — the caller owns the POSTs; the component re-renders declared state from the next `tabs`/`active` props and never renumbers it locally), and three optional interaction seams — an `onInteract: () => void` tile-focus seam, a `shouldReclaimChord` chord-reclaim predicate, and an `onPageMeta: (meta: { title: string | null }) => void` page-meta seam (all below). It owns ephemeral draft and per-frame chrome state but no persisted view/URL/localStorage state; it mutates no `@rk_win_lens` (§ Window Views → decoupling). View switching is owned by the top-bar surface toggles / mobile switch group and the palette — the tile itself carries no switch-to-terminal affordance. (260714-t97o, 260812-ab5v, 260819-v6y4, 260821-zqlq, iip5, 9kip, s36e, 6uu0, q2xk)

**Layout**: Outer wrapper `flex flex-col flex-1 min-h-0` with up to six children: the web tab strip (`shrink-0`, always rendered — § Web tab strip below), the URL bar (`shrink-0`), the find bar (`shrink-0`, rendered only while open — § Find bar below), the load progress line (rendered only while the ACTIVE frame loads — § Load progress line below), an in-tile error state (rendered in place of the ACTIVE frame's VISIBLE area while active — § Error states below), and the content-zoom wrapper (`flex-1 min-h-0 overflow-hidden`) holding one ENGINE per declared tab — each `key`ed by engine kind + tab URL (`${engineKind}:${tabUrl}` — a preference flip remounts every tab on the other engine; a tab URL change re-keys only that tab) and rendering the tab's content, every non-active tab `hidden` but never unmounted (P3; § Web tab strip below). In the ONBOARDING state (empty family) the wrapper holds the strip (bare `+` plus any open drafts), the reduced URL bar, and the onboarding panel — the frames, find bar, progress line, and error/probe machinery do not mount (§ Onboarding state below).

**Engine seam (`lib/web-frame-engine.ts`)**: a pure, DOM-light contract module (the `window-view.ts`/`find-in-page.ts` module shape; no React runtime import) sits between the chrome and whatever renders a tab's content. It exports `WebFrameEngineKind` (`"iframe" | "native"`), `WebFrameCapabilities` (`history`, `find`, `meta`, `zoomGestures`, `devtools` — five required booleans: what an engine CAN do), `TileError` (the three error-surface kinds — § Error states below), `FrameChromeState` (`loading`, `supports`, `trackedLocation`, `title`, `favicon`, `tileError`, `canGoBack`, `canGoForward`, `find: { active, total } | null` — the chrome-relevant slice one engine instance reports up through `onState`), `FindOptions` (`{ forward, findNext }`), `WebFrameEngineHandle` (`kind`, `reload`, `retry`, `back`, `forward`, `find(query, opts)`, `stopFind`, `openDevTools?`, `destroy?` — the chrome→engine commands, registered per frame URL (the frame's identity) via `registerHandle`/`unregisterHandle`; the handle never exposes the engine's element; `destroy?` is the chrome-initiated immediate teardown for engines whose content survives unmount — the chrome owns the tab family and alone knows when a tab DIES (close, URL-slot rewrite) versus the tile merely going away, and an engine with nothing to retain omits it), `WebFrameEngineProps` (`url`, `active`, `zoom`, `wireGestureListeners`, `onState`, `onLoad`, the handle registrars, the late-bindable `interactRef`/`reclaimRef` seams, the optional `retentionScope?: { server, windowId }` — the tile's tmux scope, joined with the slot `url` into the native engine's guest retention identity (main scopes it with the desktop window + host id from the sender's host view); absent ⇒ the guest never parks and unmount destroys it; the iframe engine ignores it — and two optional props the iframe engine accepts and ignores: `onZoomStep?: (direction: "in" | "out") => void` — the chrome passes its `applyZoom` bucket stepper, and the native engine's `zoom` relay calls it — and `chordTable?: readonly WebChordSpec[]` — computed once from the chrome's `useKeybindings().bindings` read via the memoized `buildWebChordTable` pass-through (`lib/web-chord-table.ts`; a rebind re-derives it)), and `ChordEvent` + `redispatchChord` — the one shared helper re-dispatching a reclaimed chord as a synthetic bubbling `KeyboardEvent("keydown")` on the parent document (bubbling reaches both the document-level palette listener and the window-level dispatcher — § Interaction + reclaim seam below). The module carries no origin awareness: an engine's same-origin posture is engine-internal and surfaces only as capability flags. The chrome's `createEngine(kind)` — the one place the chrome names an engine implementation — has an exhaustive arm per kind (a missing arm is a compile error), and the chrome selects the kind PER RENDER via `selectWebEngineKind(canShellWeb(), nativeEnabled)` (the pure rule in `lib/web-engine-pref.ts`, shared with the palette toggle — [keyboard-and-palette](/run-kit/ui/keyboard-and-palette.md) § Command Palette Actions): `nativeEnabled` comes from `useLocalStorageBoolean(WEB_NATIVE_ENGINE_PREF_KEY, WEB_NATIVE_ENGINE_DEFAULT)` (the per-viewer `runkit-web-native-engine` boolean, default on), so the native engine mounts exactly when the shell carries the `web` bridge group AND the viewer has not opted out. It renders one `<Engine key={`${engineKind}:${tabUrl}`} …/>` per tab inside the zoom wrapper, so a preference flip remounts every tab on the other engine (the outgoing native engines PARK their guests in cleanup — a flip-back adopts them, bounded by the shell's parked-set LRU) while a tab URL change still re-keys only that tab; `handleChromeState`'s equality guard compares the reported field set (`supports` and `find` by value) so an unchanged report re-renders nothing. **The chrome renders per capability, never per origin** (§ Design Decisions → The chrome renders per capability, never per origin): ◀ ▶ render iff `!onboarding && supports.history` and disable on `!canGoBack`/`!canGoForward`; the find bar takes `disabled={!supports.find}` with the verbatim hint `page is cross-origin — find unavailable` while the ⌕ button itself stays rendered and enabled on every non-onboarding tile; header meta reads the active engine's reported `title`; before an engine's first report the chrome seeds `supports` from `DEFAULT_CAPABILITIES[engineKind]` — a per-kind record mapping to each engine module's exported default (`WEB_FRAME_IFRAME_DEFAULT_CAPABILITIES` / `WEB_FRAME_NATIVE_DEFAULT_CAPABILITIES`), keeping engine knowledge out of the chrome. The chrome file holds no `crossOrigin`/`contentDocument`/`contentWindow`/`HTMLIFrameElement`/`checkFrame` reference. The `web-inspect` document CustomEvent (`WEB_INSPECT_EVENT` in `lib/web-url.ts`) is the chrome's single-receiver inspect seam: the palette's `Web: Inspect page` entry dispatches it and the mounted tile calls the ACTIVE handle's `openDevTools?.()` — an engine without the capability no-ops, and the palette entry itself is gated on the native engine upstream ([keyboard-and-palette](/run-kit/ui/keyboard-and-palette.md) § Command Palette Actions). Every `Tip` in the chrome — the tab-strip cluster and the URL-bar cluster — passes `placement="top"` so a tip never renders over the content rect, where the native engine's guest is a native layer composited above the DOM; Floating UI's `flip()` still handles the viewport edge. (260916-fxt1, q2xk, w0k7)

**The `iframe` engine (`components/web-frame-iframe.tsx`)**: `WebFrameIframe(props: WebFrameEngineProps)` owns the `<iframe>` element (`src={toProxySrc(url)}`, `hidden={!active}`, additionally hidden under an active `tileError`, the compensated scale style at `zoom ≠ 1`, `title="Proxied content"`, the sandbox list — § Iframe attributes below), the same-origin attach seam with chord reclaim (§ Interaction + reclaim seam below), EVERY probe (§ Error states below), real reload vs the about:blank bounce (§ URL Bar → Refresh button), history back/forward, and the find machinery (§ Find bar below). It derives `supports` from the same-origin attach probe (the `void iframe.contentWindow?.location.href; doc = iframe.contentDocument` try/catch): `history = find = meta = zoomGestures = !crossOrigin`, `devtools = false`; `crossOrigin` stays file-local — it also selects the reload strategy — and never crosses the engine boundary. It reports `canGoBack = canGoForward = supports.history` (no boundary signal exists; a boundary click is a harmless no-op), clears its find state on every real load and then fires `onLoad(url)` (the chrome resets its find query on the ACTIVE frame's `onLoad` — § Design Decisions → `onLoad` is a callback edge, not a state level), and exports `WEB_FRAME_IFRAME_DEFAULT_CAPABILITIES` for the pre-report first paint. Unit coverage: `web-frame-iframe.test.tsx` renders the engine directly with a recording `onState`/`onLoad`/`registerHandle` harness (render + reporting, the `onInteract` seam, chord reclaim, the error-state probes, the engine handle, the find engine); `iframe-window.stub-engine.test.tsx` drives the chrome over a stub engine (module-mocked `@/components/web-frame-iframe`) to prove the per-capability renders and handle routing; `web-frame-engine.test.ts` covers `redispatchChord`. (260916-fxt1)

**The `native` engine (`components/web-frame-native.tsx`)**: `WebFrameNative(props: WebFrameEngineProps)` implements the same contract over the desktop shell's `web` bridge group — the tab's content renders in a guest `WebContentsView` composited above the SPA's DOM ([desktop-shell](/run-kit/desktop-shell.md) § Web Views), never in an element the SPA can paint over. Its tabKey is `web-<n>` from a module-level monotonic counter assigned once per engine mount (a `useRef` initializer — fresh per remount, so a StrictMode replay or a kind flip never reuses a key), exposed on the placeholder as `data-tab-key`. One mount effect, keyed on `url`, runs in order: subscribe FIRST via `onShellWebEvent` (so no early relay is missed — an adopt's state replay lands immediately after the create resolves) → await `shellWebMode()` (the host's `direct`/`proxy`/`legacy` load mode over the additive `web:mode` bridge invoker — absent on older shells, where it reads `legacy`; [desktop-shell](/run-kit/desktop-shell.md) § Web Views → Per-host proxy modes) → `createShellWebView(tabKey, absoluteUrl, identity)` where `identity` is the STABLE retention identity — `${retentionScope.server}␀${retentionScope.windowId}␀${url}` NUL-joined (the slot URL, never the tracked location — a guest's in-page navigation must not change its identity; no `retentionScope` prop ⇒ no identity ⇒ the guest can never park), main scoping it with the desktop window + host from the sender's host view ([desktop-shell](/run-kit/desktop-shell.md) § Web Views → the registry) — with `absoluteUrl = new URL(toNativeSrc(url, mode), window.location.origin).href` — in `direct`/`proxy` a stored `/proxy/N/rest` slot re-expresses as its literal loopback URL `http://localhost:N/rest` (the guest session resolves it on the rk host in `proxy`, locally in `direct`), in `legacy` exactly the `toProxySrc` host-absolute form (the iframe engine's load target); a `URL`-constructor failure falls back to the raw `url` — → after the create resolves, the initial chord table and zoom factor are sent (`web:chords`/`web:zoom` gate on the guest existing main-side, so a mount-time send would be rejected and those channels never self-heal; the reads take the LATEST values through refs so a prop change landing during the awaits is not lost) → `registerHandle(url, handle)`; the cleanup cancels a still-pending create (an unmount during the mode await must not create a guest — a guest the late create just orphaned is DESTROYED, not parked, because the unmount may have been a chrome-issued tab death), unregisters the handle, then PARKS the guest via `parkShellWebView(tabKey)` — the tile went away; a same-identity remount adopts it — except after a chrome-issued `destroy()` through the handle (a tab that is GONE never parks), and falls back to `destroyShellWebView(tabKey)` on an older shell without `web.park` (`canParkShellWebView()` narrows the additive invoker), calls the subscription's disposer exactly once, and cancels any pending rAF. Relayed events are demuxed by tabKey — an event for another tab is dropped before any state update — and otherwise map: `title` → `title` (empty string ⇒ `null`); `favicon` → `favicon = favicons[0] ?? null`; `loading` → `loading`, a `true` edge clearing any stale `tileError` and the `false` edge resetting `find` to `null` (the iframe engine's per-load reset, mirrored) and firing `onLoad(url)` (the chrome's per-completed-load find-query reset); `url` → `trackedLocation` (root-relative `pathname + search + hash` when same-origin with the host, the absolute URL otherwise, the raw string when unparsable) plus `canGoBack`/`canGoForward`, re-sends the zoom bucket (`setShellWebViewZoom` on EVERY navigation — Chromium's per-host zoom store inside the guest partition fights the SPA's buckets across hosts), and maps its `httpStatus` (present on `did-navigate` only) through `tileErrorForGuestResponse(httpStatus, url)`, a `null` result clearing a stale error on a successful navigation; `failed` → `loading = false` and `tileError = tileErrorForGuestFailure(event, url)`; `focus` → the `interactRef` tile-focus seam (`onInteract` — the iframe engine's in-frame pointerdown/keydown equivalent); `zoom` (the guest's own ctrl+wheel arriving as a direction-only relay) → `onZoomStep?.(direction)`, stepping the chrome's bucket — main never applies it, and the stepped `zoom` prop flows back through the zoom effect; `find` → `find = { active: Math.max(0, active - 1), total }` (Chromium's `activeMatchOrdinal` is 1-based, the FindBar's `matchIndex` 0-based; `total === 0` reads `0/0`); `chord` → `interactRef.current?.()` FIRST (a keydown inside the tile is an interaction — the iframe engine's `onKey` ordering), then `redispatchChord(event)`, the shared helper's synthetic bubbling `keydown` on the document, WITHOUT consulting `reclaimRef` (the uploaded table already IS the predicate, and Escape is not a registry chord). The reported capabilities are `{ history: true, find: true, meta: true, zoomGestures: true, devtools: true }` (`WEB_FRAME_NATIVE_DEFAULT_CAPABILITIES`) — every chrome control works on this engine, and find/devtools pass the iframe engine (cross-origin find, a real detached DevTools window). The handle is `{ kind: "native", reload, retry: reload, back, forward, find, stopFind, openDevTools, destroy }` — `destroy` marks the tab chrome-destroyed (so the unmount cleanup does NOT park) and calls `destroyShellWebView(tabKey)`: the chrome issues it when a tab is closed or its URL slot is rewritten, the two deaths only the chrome can see ([desktop-shell](/run-kit/desktop-shell.md) § Design Decisions → The engine's unmount always parks; the chrome owns explicit destroys): `reload` clears `tileError`, sets `loading = true`, and calls `reloadShellWebView(tabKey)`; the remaining verbs ride the bridge invokers — `goBackShellWebView`/`goForwardShellWebView` (main-side no-ops at the history boundary), `findShellWebView`, `stopFindShellWebView` (also resetting the engine's `find` state to `null`), and `openShellWebViewDevTools`. Two pure modules own the engine's derivations (the `web-url.ts` module contract, colocated Vitest): `lib/web-chord-table.ts` exports `WebChordSpec` and `buildWebChordTable(bindings)` — every `enabled` binding that is neither `ttyOnly` nor `guiOnly` (`webOnly` included), expanded per tier exactly as `matchesCombo` accepts (`cmd` → Ctrl OR Meta; `shifted` → Shift+Ctrl OR Shift+Meta; `ctrl` → Ctrl alone; Alt always `false`), deduped by the five-tuple in registry order with a plain `{code: "Escape"}` appended last; `lib/web-native-errors.ts` exports `tileErrorForGuestFailure`, `tileErrorForGuestResponse`, `reasonFromChromiumDescription`, and the `WEB_ERR_CONNECTION_REFUSED` (−102) / `WEB_ERR_CONNECTION_RESET` (−101) constants — a proxy-kind tab answering the rk reverse proxy's 502 (`did-navigate`'s `httpResponseCode`) or failing with a refused/reset code maps to `{ kind: "dead-port", port }`; every other main-frame failure maps to `{ kind: "unreachable", host, reason }` with the host from the failed URL (the raw string when unparsable) and the reason derived from Chromium's description (`ERR_NAME_NOT_RESOLVED` → `name not resolved`; empty ⇒ `load failed`); the `refused` kind is never produced on this engine — Chromium renders sites that refuse embedding. The engine renders ONLY a placeholder — `<div data-testid="web-native-placeholder" data-tab-key={tabKey} hidden={!active} className="w-full h-full bg-bg-primary" />` filling the zoom wrapper, no transform — painting the tile background so a hidden or not-yet-painted guest leaves the card, never a hole; the `zoom` prop reaches the guest's renderer via `web:zoom`, sent after create, on every `zoom` prop change, and on every `url` relay. **Bounds**: `measure()` reads the placeholder's `getBoundingClientRect()`, `Math.round`s each field, dedupes against the last SENT rect, and calls `setShellWebViewBounds`; a zero-size rect (hidden tab, collapsed tile) sends no bounds and feeds the visibility rule instead. Triggers, all funnelled through `measure()`: a `ResizeObserver` on the placeholder, a dependency-less `useLayoutEffect` (a position-only shift fires no observer entry), a window `resize` listener, the drag rAF loop, and the `active`/`wantVisible` true edges. Bounds are sent while hidden — main parks them and applies them on show — and they are viewport coordinates with no offset added (the host view fills the window content area). **Visibility**: `wantVisible = active && !overlayOpen && rectNonZero && tileError === null && posture !== "move"` — while the error surface is up the guest must not paint over it (the chrome keeps the engine wrapper mounted beside the surface, so hiding is the only way the copy is visible; the iframe engine hides its frame the same way) — with `overlayOpen` from `useSyncExternalStore(subscribe, isOccludingOpen, () => false)` over `lib/overlay-presence` — the full hide signal, modal-class surfaces AND click-opened menus/popovers (`transient`); tooltips and hover-opened flyout cards register nothing and never hide the guest ([dialogs-and-state](/run-kit/ui/dialogs-and-state.md) § Overlay Presence); `web:visible` is sent only when `wantVisible` changes, and the `false → true` edge measures first so bounds precede the show. **Drag posture**: the `TileDragContext` posture (`useTileDragPosture()` — § Surface Layout → Tile renderer) selects the mid-drag behavior: the `resize` posture (a sash or intersection drag) runs a rAF loop of deduped `measure()` calls for the drag's duration, with one final measure on the resize → idle edge, and never hides the guest; the `move` posture (a tile header drag) hides the guest — the drop overlay previews the result tree and the composited guest cannot be painted over — and the move → idle show edge re-measures first (§ Design Decisions → Sash drags live-resize the native guest; tile moves hide it). `wireGestureListeners` is never called — the engine owns no content document. Unit coverage: `web-frame-native.test.tsx` over a mocked `window.runkitShell.web` (recording invokers, a captured relay handler, a controllable `ResizeObserver` and `getBoundingClientRect`, a manual rAF pump) proves the mount order and URL shapes, the teardown-once discipline, the tabKey demux and event mapping (the find ordinal map, the zoom re-apply on every navigation, the chord re-dispatch ordering), the capability/handle shape, the `tileError` set/clear edges and the error-hidden guest, the bounds rounding/dedupe/zero-rect rules, the modal+transient/`active` visibility edges, and the drag loop. (q2xk, w0k7, ckpm)

**Per-engine parity** — the chrome renders per capability, so the control is identical while each engine's mechanism differs:

| | iframe engine | native engine |
|---|---|---|
| history | `contentWindow.history.back()/forward()` on same-origin frames; no boundary signal (both flags report as `supports.history`, a boundary click a harmless no-op); cross-origin reports no history | `web:back`/`web:forward` → `navigationHistory.goBack/goForward()` (boundary no-op main-side); the `url` relay's `canGoBack`/`canGoForward` give a real boundary signal |
| find | Highlight-API walk of the same-origin `contentDocument` (`lib/find-in-page.ts`); a cross-origin frame reports `supports.find: false` and the bar disables | Chromium `findInPage` behind the same FindBar — works cross-origin; 1-based ordinals relayed on `found-in-page`, mapped to the bar's 0-based index |
| zoom | scale wrapper on the frame element (`transform: scale(s)` over a `100%/s` box) | `web:zoom` → `setZoomFactor` in the guest renderer, re-applied on every navigation; the guest's ctrl+wheel steps the SPA bucket through the `zoom` relay |
| chord reclaim | capture-phase `keydown` on the same-origin `contentDocument`, predicate consulted at event time | main-side `before-input-event` matched against the SPA-enumerated chord table → focus hop → `chord` relay → document re-dispatch |
| error surface | engine-owned probes: `/api/frame-check` for external refusals, a same-origin fetch reading the proxy's 502 for dead ports | Chromium `did-fail-load` codes plus `did-navigate`'s `httpStatus` mapped SPA-side (`lib/web-native-errors.ts`); the `refused` kind is unreachable |
| devtools | none (`supports.devtools: false`) — no Inspect button | detached Chromium DevTools via `web:devtools`, opened by the URL bar's `Inspect page` button (`<>` code-xml glyph, `data-icon="inspect"`, between the zoom control and ↗; rendered only when `supports.devtools`) or the `Web: Inspect page` palette entry |
| page meta | same-origin `contentDocument` reads (title, favicon, location) | relayed `title`/`favicon`/`url` events, cross-origin included |

**Web tab strip**: a `role="tablist"` strip (`data-testid="web-tab-strip"`, `shrink-0`, horizontally scrollable, 11px monospace, the tile-header idiom — on the tile's own `bg-bg-primary` surface with a `border-b border-border` rule) always renders above the URL bar — at 0 tabs/0 drafts it holds only the trailing `+` (a tablist with zero `role="tab"` children is the accepted resting shape), so the empty family keeps its draft entry points. Each declared tab (`role="tab"`, `aria-selected`, `data-testid="web-tab"`, `data-index={n}`, `title={displayForm(url)}`) labels itself with its URL-keyed tracked document title and falls back to `webTabTitle(url)`/`#n`. Its icon slot shows a per-frame loading spinner, static under `prefers-reduced-motion`; after load it shows the same-origin document's icon link or frame-origin `/favicon.ico`, an external host's `https://{host}/favicon.ico`, or the `classifyAddress` kind dot (green present / amber proxy / blue external) when no icon applies or an image errors. A cross-origin navigation clears the tracked title, location and icon derived from the prior readable document so the stored URL drives the fallbacks. The close `×` is always rendered on the active tab and on coarse pointers, hover/focus-within-revealed otherwise; `auxclick` button 1 closes through the same `onCloseTab` path. The strip is a ROVING-FOCUS tablist: only the selected entry is in the tab order; ←/→/Home/End move focus without writing; Enter/Space select; Delete/Backspace close; component-local ⌥⇧←/⌥⇧→ moves the active declared tab one slot through `onMoveTab`, with boundary presses as silent no-ops. Pointer drag crosses a 6px threshold before becoming reorder, paints a green drop-side indicator on the hovered tab edge, and commits one `onMoveTab(from, to)` on release; drafts are neither drag sources nor targets. The trailing `+`, a double-click on empty strip space, and the palette's `Web: New tab` event append a dashed viewer-local draft after every declared tab and focus the address bar. Multiple drafts may coexist. Enter materializes the selected draft through `onAddTab`, selects the returned server-assigned slot through `onSelectTab(index)`, and removes the draft after add resolves; add idempotence may return an existing slot. Escape or the draft's `×` discards only that draft without a POST. Drafts live for one mounted window tile and disappear on window switch/unmount. **A SELECTED draft deactivates every frame** — each Engine renders `active={selectedDraft === null && …}` while a draft is selected, so the native guest hides (visibility rule) and iframe frames hide, and a blank new-tab panel (`data-testid="web-draft-panel"` — the `://` glyph, "New tab", and the *"type an address above — Enter opens it here, Esc discards"* guidance, the empty-tile onboarding visual language kept minimal) paints over the frames wrapper; the previous page stays MOUNTED underneath, hidden (P3), and selecting a real tab again (click, roving Enter/Space, or a submit landing in an existing slot) deselects the draft and re-activates that tab's frame WITHOUT a reload. The `+` disables at the declared-family cap (`tabs.length >= 16`) with a `web tabs full (16)` Tip; add errors surface inline in the address bar's `role="alert"` submit-error slot. **Frames**: the zoom wrapper mounts one `<Engine key={`${engineKind}:${tabUrl}`}>` per declared tab — each tab's content `hidden` (`display:none`) when non-active (the iframe engine's `<iframe>`; the native engine's placeholder, its guest view parked visible-state by the shell), never unmounted by selection or reorder and never re-`src`d, so in-page state survives tab switches; the chrome also owns the native guest's DEATH — a URL present in the previous `tabs` and absent from the next while the tile stays on the SAME window (`server:windowId` unchanged) is a tab close / URL-slot rewrite / external removal, so its engine handle's `destroy()` is called RENDER-PHASE (the destroy must precede the outgoing engine's commit-phase unmount park, and the handle suppresses its own park once destroy fired — the pair is order-proof), while a window switch destroys nothing: the departing tabs' engines park on unmount and their identities adopt on return (§ The `native` engine above; [desktop-shell](/run-kit/desktop-shell.md) § Design Decisions → The engine's unmount always parks; the chrome owns explicit destroys); the URL half of the key keeps close and move renumbering from remounting frames, and the kind half remounts every tab on the other engine when the viewer flips the engine preference. Frame-scoped state (loading, capabilities, tracked location, title, favicon, probe/error, find) lives in the per-tab engine and reports into the chrome's URL-keyed `FrameChromeState` map; the URL bar, back/forward, refresh, find bar, progress line, error surface, `onPageMeta`, and zoom bind to the ACTIVE frame's entry, while every same-origin frame contributes its title/icon to its own tab. All frames mount EAGERLY (the family cap of 16 bounds the count; no lazy mount, no LRU) and inactive frames run no dead-port probe timer — a frame probes on first mount and on activation. The document CustomEvents (`web-find:open`, `web-address:focus`, `web-tab:open-draft`, `web-open-external`, `web-zoom`) keep their single listeners on the ONE `IframeWindow` per layout. e2e: `web-tabs.spec.ts` seeds real tmux families and covers the one-tab strip, drafts and materialization/discard, drag/keyboard/palette reorder, middle-click close, shared-state convergence, and URL-keyed mounted frames. (9kip, s36e)

**Optimistic select/remove/move**: the strip verbs are wired in `SurfaceLayout` (`components/surface-layout.tsx`) through `useOptimisticAction` over the window store's per-entry `webOverride { webTabs?, webActive? }` (display only). Select sets `{ webActive: n }`; remove derives the local family with `webFamilyAfterRemove(tabs, active, n)`; move derives it with `webFamilyAfterMove(tabs, active, n, to)`, including the active-pointer repoint. Synchronous override refs make consecutive gestures compound on the family already displayed instead of stale SSE payload. Move POSTs are serialized in gesture order. A rejected move rolls back to the SSE-confirmed family, cancels every queued dependent move whose indices came from the rejected optimistic family, fires no dependent POST, and emits one error toast; dependents are dropped rather than reinterpreted against different indices. Successful verb POSTs wake the SSE hub, and a reconcile effect clears the override once the payload matches; a window switch drops any in-flight override (the override cleanup effect re-runs on its `[server, sessionName, windowId]` dep change — the grid is keyed by server, not remounted). Add is not optimistic because the slot index is server-assigned; `addWebTab` receives `toWebAddTarget(target)` so a root-relative `/proxy/…` draft reaches the backend's `rk present`-shaped target resolver. The component types verbs as promise-returning; optimistic executors are fire-and-forget, so the `SurfaceLayout` wrappers resolve at once. (9kip, s36e)

**URL Bar**: Thin toolbar above the tile's content area (`border-b border-border bg-bg-primary`), one warm-tip `TipGroup` cluster, button order per the approved design study (`fab/changes/260819-v6y4-web-tile-browser-chrome/web-tile-chrome-design-study.html`):
- **Back / Forward buttons** (`WebBackGlyph`/`WebForwardGlyph` — `data-icon="web-back"`/`"web-forward"`, register arrow SVGs) — drive the active engine handle's `back()`/`forward()` (the iframe engine runs `contentWindow.history.back()/forward()`; cross-origin history is unreachable to a parent by design, so that engine reports no history capability there). Both render iff the engine reports `supports.history` and disable on `!canGoBack`/`!canGoForward` — the iframe engine has no boundary signal, so it reports both flags equal to `supports.history` and a boundary click is a harmless no-op. The navigation is per-viewer view state and never touches the stored web tab (spec window-views R7). (260819-v6y4)
- **Refresh button** (`RefreshGlyph` — `data-icon="refresh"`, the same register export the top-bar RefreshButton renders) — routes to the active engine handle's `reload()`: on the iframe engine a REAL reload on same-origin frames, where `contentWindow.location.reload()` preserves the frame's CURRENT location and in-page state (no reset to the stored tab address); the `about:blank` bounce (clear `src`, re-set via `setTimeout(0)`) remains ONLY as the cross-origin fallback. Styled: `w-7 h-7 rounded hover:bg-bg-card text-text-secondary hover:text-text-primary` — every URL-bar button carries the tile-verb hover brighten. (260819-v6y4, 76uz)
- **Address input (display/edit split)** — at rest it SHOWS the kind-specific DISPLAY form (`displayForm(rawAddress)`, § Address model below — a proxied `/proxy/3000/…` reads `localhost:3000/…`, a presented file reads its basename with the `server`/`v`/`raw` plumbing params hidden, an external URL reads host+path with the scheme omitted). Focusing it reveals the RAW editable value (the tracked frame location when known, else the active tab's address) with select-all. On Enter the input routes through the five-lane submit ladder (`routeAddressSubmit`, § Address model below). URL-shaped input (absolute http(s), root-relative, bare loopback `host:port` → `/proxy/{port}/…`) takes the WRITE lane: with a selected draft Enter calls `onAddTab(url)`, selects the returned index, and removes that draft after success; otherwise it calls `onWriteUrl(url)` and a same-URL submit is a no-op. PATH/PORT-shaped input — a relative path (`docs/foo.html`, `./x`), bare `:NNNN`, or a dotted single name — takes the ADD lane regardless of draft state: submitted raw to the add verb, the backend stats it under the window's first-pane worktree cwd, and the add's target-identity match makes the paste APPEND-OR-FOCUS (an existing tab for the target focuses, a new target appends; the current tab is never navigated in place). A dotted single name (`README.md`, `example.com`) is stat-decided: it exists → present tab; `ApiError` status 400 → one silent retry as `https://{name}` through the write lane (so a pasted `README.md` never lands on Moldova's TLD). Bare words and explicit non-http(s) schemes — with or without `//` (`ftp://x`, `file:/etc/passwd`, `mailto:a/b`); only the dotted `host.tld:port[/path]` bare-domain shape is exempt from the scheme reject — surface an inline `role="alert"` error and fire NO POST; an add-lane 400 without a fallback surfaces the backend body text (`target "…" does not exist`) in the same slot with the input kept editable; blank Enter is a silent no-op. `SurfaceLayout` binds the write seam to `setWindowOptions(server, windowId, { "@rk_win_web_<n>": url })` → `POST /api/windows/{windowId}/options` (partial-merge — n = `webActive`, or slot 1 while the pointer is unset). Escape reverts a declared tab's edit without a POST or discards the selected draft; an ordinary blur restores the rest display. The address bar REPLACES the active declared slot for URL-shaped input and APPENDS-OR-FOCUSES through add for path/port-shaped input; the strip `+`, empty-space double-click, and palette `Web: New tab` open drafts that materialize through add — § Design Decisions → Address bar replaces, `+` adds. On a replace failure the input reverts to the SSE-confirmed address; on a draft-add failure the draft remains selected with its submitted value and inline error for retry. Styled: `bg-bg-card text-text-primary text-sm px-2 py-1 rounded border border-border`. Editing a declared content address is *substrate* state (shared, POSTed, every viewer sees it); editing an unmaterialized draft is viewer-local and never POSTed. (260529-jad6, 260819-v6y4, iip5, 9kip, s36e)
- **Find button** (`FindGlyph` — `data-icon="find"`, the same register export as the tty tile's find toggle: one find identity across surfaces; `aria-label="Find in page"`, `aria-pressed` while open — lit `text-accent-green` when the bar is open) — the pointer/touch entry point to the find bar; toggles it locally (the chord and palette entries share one open seam instead, § Find bar)
- **Open-in-browser button** (`OpenExternalGlyph` — `data-icon="open-external"`, register arrow-up-right; `aria-label="Open in browser"`, rightmost) — opens the CURRENT address (the tracked frame location when known, else the stored tab address) in a new tab via `window.open(…, "_blank", "noopener")`; relative addresses resolve naturally against the viewer's origin (the stored address stays relative per the display contract `docs/site/skill/display.md`). The universal escape hatch for anything the tile can't render; the `Web: Open in browser` palette action mirrors it (§ Address model below — the document-CustomEvent seams). (260819-v6y4)

**SSE Sync**: a `useEffect` on the derived active address syncs the URL bar text (`setInputUrl(url)`) and clears the submit error on every active-address change — an SSE push, a tab select, or a same-slot replace. The frame side needs NO sync effect: a changed tab URL re-keys (remounts) exactly that frame, and a selection change only flips `hidden` — no frame's `src` is ever rewritten by the parent, so identical SSE ticks and tab switches reload nothing. When the active web tab changes externally (an agent or another viewer runs `tmux set-option -w @rk_win_web_<n>` or a web verb), the next payload repaint flips the visible frame and the URL bar follows automatically. (9kip)

**Interaction + reclaim seam (`onInteract`, `shouldReclaimChord`)**: in-frame clicks and keydowns stay in the frame's own document, and focus entering a frame fires NO focusin in the parent — parent-document listeners are blind to them. The seam is engine-internal: each `WebFrameIframe` attaches capture-phase `pointerdown`/`keydown` listeners to the same-origin `contentDocument` after every iframe `load` (each navigation replaces the document; cross-origin access throws and is skipped silently — the `CodeSurface` attach pattern) plus a parent-window `blur` fallback that reports when `document.activeElement` is the iframe, covering cross-origin embeds; the native engine's interaction sources are the relayed guest `focus` event (`web:event` kind `focus` → `interactRef.current()`) and the relayed `chord` event (reporting `interactRef` before re-dispatch, matching the iframe engine's keydown ordering — § The `native` engine above). The chrome hands both seams down as late-bindable refs (`interactRef`/`reclaimRef` on `WebFrameEngineProps`), so a prop arriving after mount (a hidden tile handed slot -1 becoming visible) still binds — the attach never gates on them and the engine reads the ref per event. Every in-frame keydown reports `onInteract` FIRST, then — when a `shouldReclaimChord` predicate prop is injected — a keydown matching it is `preventDefault` + `stopImmediatePropagation`ed inside the frame and re-dispatched through the contract module's shared `redispatchChord` helper as a synthetic bubbling `KeyboardEvent` on the parent **document** (the CodeSurface `onKey` mechanism, byte-for-byte — § Code Surface → Chord reclaim): run-kit's registry chords (⌘K, ⌘;, ⌘F, …) keep working with focus inside the frame while every non-claimed key reaches the framed page untouched (typing into an in-frame form is unchanged). The native engine's chord path is the third reclaim mechanism beside this listener and the code seam: a guest's keydowns never reach the SPA document, so the chrome enumerates the predicate into a per-guest chord table (`chordTable`, built by `buildWebChordTable` from the keybinding registry), main matches `before-input-event` against it with exact modifier equality, `preventDefault`s the matched input and hops OS focus to the host view, and relays the chord for the engine to re-dispatch through the same `redispatchChord` after reporting `onInteract` first — the engine never re-consults `reclaimRef` (the table already IS the predicate, and Escape rides it as an always-present spec — § Design Decisions → The chord table is the reclaim predicate, enumerated; [desktop-shell](/run-kit/desktop-shell.md) § Web Views). `SurfaceLayout` injects the predicate bound to the `web` kind (`shouldReclaimChord("web")`, the kind-aware factory in `app.tsx` — § Surface Layout → Tile renderer), so `ttyOnly` chords pass through to the framed page and `webOnly` chords (⌘F, ⌘L) reclaim only here (§ Keyboard Shortcuts → the `ttyOnly`/`webOnly` flags). Cross-origin frames get no reclaim — the `blur` fallback stays the only interaction signal, and once a cross-origin frame holds focus, later in-frame clicks report nothing — accepted, the tile is already focused by then. `SurfaceLayout` wires the seam to tile focus (§ Surface Layout → Focused tile); both props are optional and other mount contexts omit them unchanged. (260815-41t9, 260819-ie2i)

**Find bar (⌘F find-in-page)**: the web tile ships in-frame page search for same-origin content — a `shrink-0` row rendered between the URL bar and the frame area while open (`data-testid="web-find-bar"`, the URL bar's border/background vocabulary; layout per the approved design study `fab/changes/260819-v6y4-web-tile-browser-chrome/web-tile-chrome-design-study.html` state 03). The row is the shared presentational **`FindBar`** component (`components/find-bar.tsx`, colocated `find-bar.test.tsx`) — props-driven (`query`/`matchIndex`/`matchCount`/`onQueryChange`/`onNext`/`onPrev`/`onClose`, plus optional `toggles`, `disabled`, `statusText` (replaces the counter), and `scopeNote`), the consumer owning the open state — also mounted by the tty tile (§ Tile renderer → tty find bar); `IframeWindow` owns only `findOpen` + `findQuery` and passes no toggles — the ACTIVE engine owns matches, the active index, and highlights behind its handle: a query change or an active-tab switch with the bar open calls `find(query, { forward: true, findNext: false })` (an empty query calls `stopFind()`), Enter/Shift+Enter call `find(query, { forward, findNext: true })` (stepping with wrap), and closing the bar calls `stopFind()`; the engine reports `find: { active, total } | null` back through its state and the bar renders it as the `n/N` counter (§ Design Decisions → Find is an engine command with a chrome-owned query). Row contents in order: an autofocused text input, the `n/N` match counter with the active ordinal in accent green (`0/0` when the query matches nothing), ∧/∨ previous/next buttons, a ✕ close button, and a right-aligned key hint `Enter next · ⇧Enter prev · Esc close` suppressed on coarse pointers (`useCoarsePointer()` — keyboard-chord copy never renders on touch). Enter advances to the next match, Shift+Enter to the previous (both wrap), Escape closes the bar and clears all highlights. Three entry points share ONE open seam: the ⌘F/Ctrl+F `web-find` chord (reclaimed inside the frame or handled at tile level while the web tile owns focus — § Keyboard Shortcuts → the `ttyOnly`/`webOnly` flags) and the `Web: Find in page` palette action both dispatch the `web-find:open` document CustomEvent (`WEB_FIND_OPEN_EVENT`), which the mounted web tile listens for (the `window-heading:rename` / `theme-selector:open` precedent — one `IframeWindow` per layout hosting N frames, so the receiver is unambiguous; the listener acts on the ACTIVE frame); the ⌕ URL-bar button (the pointer/touch entry) toggles the bar locally. An engine reporting `supports.find: false` (the iframe engine on a cross-origin frame) renders the bar DISABLED — input and navigation buttons disabled, the inline hint `page is cross-origin — find unavailable` in place of the counter — and its `find` command is a no-op reporting `find: null` (⌘F pressed while a cross-origin frame holds focus never reaches the parent at all, so the ⌕ button and the palette are the bar's entry points there). Every frame `load` resets the find state — the engine clears its matches and highlights (they die with the document they were collected from) and fires `onLoad`, on which the chrome resets its query for the ACTIVE frame (no term persistence across navigations); the bar's open/closed state itself survives navigation. (260819-ie2i, 260819-zqf9)

**Find engine (`lib/find-in-page.ts`)**: a pure module with colocated tests (the `window-view.ts` / `surface-layout.ts` module contract), driven by the iframe engine over its own frame's same-origin `contentDocument` — no script injected into the framed page. `collectMatches` walks the frame document's text nodes with a case-insensitive TreeWalker into DOM `Range`s (skipping non-rendered containers like `script`/`style`); `stepMatch` is the wrap-around active-index transition; `applyHighlights` registers two CSS Custom Highlights (`rk-find` all matches, `rk-find-active` the active match with its accent-green outline) on the FRAME window's `CSS.highlights` and installs one `<style>` element of `::highlight()` rules into the frame's `<head>` (inert DOM — `clearHighlights` removes both on reset); the active match scrolls into view (`block: "nearest"`). Where the Highlight API is unavailable, `contentWindow.find()` is the best-effort fallback — navigation works, the counter may read the zero form. All frame-DOM access carries the attach seam's try/catch posture (pre-load and cross-origin frames no-op silently). Unit-provable in jsdom without the Highlight API. e2e: `web-tile-find.spec.ts` on the real-tmux port-3020 rig (the `web-view-lens.spec.ts` `_tmux.ts` pattern) proves ⌘K opening the palette with focus inside a same-origin web-tile frame, the ⌘F open → match count → Enter navigation → Escape close flow on a presented HTML file, and the cross-origin disabled bar + hint via the ⌕ button. (260819-ie2i)

**Address model (`lib/web-url.ts`)**: a pure, DOM-free module with colocated tests (the `window-view.ts` / `surface-layout.ts` module contract) owning every derivation over a stored `@rk_win_web_<n>` address or an address-bar keystroke. `classifyAddress(url)` returns one of four kinds — `present` (any root-relative `/present/…`, covering both `/present/{server}/{roothash}/{path}` and the legacy `/present/{windowId}/{n}/{path}?server=` arm), `proxy` (root-relative `/proxy/{port}/…` OR an absolute loopback `http(s)://localhost|127.0.0.1|[::1]:{port}…`), `external` (any other absolute http(s) URL), `relative` (any other root-relative path) — driving the header badge and error posture; unrecognized input degrades to `relative`, never throws. `displayForm(url)` is the kind-specific resting form: a presented file shows its basename, with content-keyed `server`/`roothash` path segments and the query plumbing excluded (the legacy `server` identity param, the shared `v` cache-buster, and the viewer shell's `raw` escape hatch — `PRESENT_PLUMBING_PARAMS`); a presented directory or degraded shape retains the raw-address fallback; proxy shows `localhost:{port}{path}`; external shows `host{path}{?query}` with the scheme omitted; relative stays raw. `normalizeAddressInput(input)` maps bare loopback `host:port[{path}]` to `/proxy/{port}{path or /}`, maps a bare domain to `https://…`, and otherwise passes through for `isAllowedUrl` to validate. `isAllowedUrl(input)` mirrors the backend allowlist (absolute http/https with a host or root-relative). `routeAddressSubmit(input)` is the address bar's submit router (`AddressSubmitRoute`, a three-arm union): `write` — URL-shaped input, the slot-write/draft lanes, composing `normalizeAddressInput`; `add` — path/port-shaped input handed raw to the backend add verb, with a `fallback` `https://{input}` form carried for dotted single segments (the caller retries it on `ApiError` 400); `reject` — bare words and explicit non-http(s) schemes. Lane order: absolute http(s) + root-relative pass-through → bare loopback rewrite + bare `:NNNN` as a port target → scheme reject (exempting only the full dotted `host.tld:port[/path]` bare-domain shape) → slash-bearing/`./`-leading paths → dotted single segments with fallback → reject. (ewud) `proxyPortOf(url)` returns the proxy-kind port; `toProxySrc(url)` owns iframe-src mapping. `WebNativeMode` (`"direct" | "proxy" | "legacy"`) is the native engine's host-reported load mode, and `toNativeSrc(url, mode)` its load target: in `direct`/`proxy` a stored `/proxy/{port}/rest` slot maps back to the literal loopback URL `http://localhost:{port}/rest` (the `toWebAddTarget` inverse mapping — the same stored slot stays portable to a browser viewer's iframe engine) while absolute URLs and every other address kind pass through unchanged; `legacy` is exactly `toProxySrc(url)`. `webTabTitle(url)` is the shorter tab label: presented files show the basename with both address shapes' plumbing excluded, proxy shows `localhost:{port}{path}`, external shows the host, and relative stays raw; directory/degraded present shapes retain the raw-address fallback, and empty input returns `""` for the caller's `#n` fallback. `toWebAddTarget(url)` passes both present shapes through unchanged and re-expresses only a root-relative `/proxy/{port}…` address as `http://localhost:{port}…` for the backend's `rk present`-shaped add parser. The module exports `WEB_ADDRESS_FOCUS_EVENT` (`web-address:focus`, the plain ⌘L/palette focus seam), `WEB_TAB_DRAFT_EVENT` (`web-tab:open-draft`, the palette's draft-opening seam), and `WEB_OPEN_EXTERNAL_EVENT` (`web-open-external`, the palette seam). Each follows the `WEB_FIND_OPEN_EVENT` single-receiver pattern: one `IframeWindow` per layout hosts N frames, so one listener can act on the active frame or tile-local draft state. Display derivation never rewrites the stored relative address. (260819-v6y4, 9kip, 260901-ei4t-present-url-server-root-hash, s36e)

**What a present tab renders**: a present-kind address for a gated extension (`.md`/`.markdown`/`.excalidraw`) loads the backend-served viewer shell inside the frame — rendered markdown (mermaid diagrams on fenced blocks) or a static excalidraw SVG — at the document's own URL, so the frame content is the rendered document, not raw source; the raw form stays one `?raw=1` away. The shell's contract (extension gate, `X-Present-Format`, dev-proxy sourcing) lives in [api-and-sockets](/run-kit/api-and-sockets.md) § API Layer → `/present/{server}/{roothash}/*`; its build shape in [build-and-release](/run-kit/build-and-release.md) § Build Pipeline. (260908-krov-present-viewer-shell) **Diagrams are zoomable figures** (`app/frontend/src/viewer/zoomable-figure.ts` over the pure `viewer/pan-zoom.ts`): every excalidraw scene (`.viewer-scene`) and every mermaid diagram (`.viewer-diagram`) is wrapped as a `.viewer-figure` (`role="group"`, `tabindex=0`, `data-testid="viewer-figure"`, `data-mode`, `data-zoomed`) holding a clipping `.viewer-figure-viewport` and a hover/focus-revealed toolbar (Zoom out · percent readout · Zoom in · Fit to view · Expand on inline figures). The SVG keeps its NATURAL layout box (explicit px `width`/`height` from its `viewBox`, `max-width: none`) and the only magnification is `transform: translate() scale()` with origin `0 0` — vector text stays crisp, nothing re-renders. Rest = the fit state (`fitScale`: `"contain"` for an excalidraw canvas, `"width"` for an inline diagram, never upscaled past natural); bounds `[fit, 8×]` (`FIGURE_ZOOM_MAX`); ctrl/⌘-wheel zooms continuously about the cursor (the tile's `applyWheelZoom` mapping from `lib/zoom-gesture.ts`), two-pointer pinch about the midpoint, Safari `gesture*` likewise; button/plain-key zoom steps a local extended ladder (`FIGURE_ZOOM_LEVELS`, 0.1…8) to the next level strictly beyond the current scale (no snap-then-step); drag pans with pointer capture once `isPannable`; plain keys `+`/`=` `-`/`_` `0` and arrows (`PAN_STEP_PX`, ×5 with Shift) act while focus is inside the figure — never ⌘/Ctrl variants. `touch-action` is `pan-x pan-y` at fit (page scroll survives a finger over an inline diagram) and `none` once zoomed. A `ResizeObserver` refits at fit and only re-clamps when zoomed. Layouts: `body[data-format="excalidraw"]` drops the reading column and the canvas figure is `100vh`; an inline mermaid figure keeps its fitted height and its **Expand** toggle (`aria-pressed`) grows the box to `calc(100vh − 2rem)` and back. Figure gestures are claimed by one viewer-wide **`window`-capture** wheel/gesture arm (`installFigureGestureArm`, installed once in `viewer/main.ts`) scoped to targets inside a `.viewer-figure`; every other wheel — unmodified, or outside a figure — passes untouched to the tile's frame-document arm, so tile zoom and figure zoom compose. Zoom is ephemeral per figure (nothing persisted, nothing POSTed); no `focus()` fires on mount. e2e: `present-viewer.spec.ts` (wide excalidraw + mermaid fixtures: fit at rest, ctrl-wheel escape, `+`/`0`, drag clamp, Expand, tile passthrough). (pdow)

**Current-path tracking + page meta**: every engine instance handles its own frame's `load` events through the attach seam's try/catch posture. A same-origin frame's engine stores its location root-relative (viewer origin stripped), document title, and favicon link or frame-origin `/favicon.ico`, reporting them into the chrome's URL-keyed state map via `onState`; every tab can therefore show metadata from its own frame. An unreadable cross-origin load clears that frame's tracked location/title/favicon so stored-URL fallbacks apply. The address bar, navigation controls and optional `onPageMeta` header seam bind only to the ACTIVE map entry — the chrome fires `onPageMeta` from the ACTIVE engine's reported `title`; `SurfaceLayout` holds the active title for the tile header. All metadata and in-frame navigation are display-only and never POSTed; only explicit Enter on a declared tab's address writes its slot. (260819-v6y4, 9kip, s36e)

**Onboarding state (empty web-tab family)**: with no tab to render (`tabs.length === 0`), the tile mounts the ONBOARDING state in place of the frames and their probe machinery — the branch is `onboarding = tabs.length === 0`, and NO frame-check/dead-port probing runs (the probes key off an address; § Error states below). With ≥1 tab every frame mounts regardless of the active pointer's value (an out-of-range pointer clamps, never shows onboarding). The **reduced URL bar** keeps only the REFRESH button and the ADDRESS INPUT — back/forward, the find ⌕ button, and the ↗ open-in-browser button are hidden (nothing to navigate, search, or open) — and the input is FULLY LIVE with placeholder `localhost:3000 · /present/… · https://…`: typing an address + Enter runs the existing submit pipeline (`normalizeAddressInput` → `isAllowedUrl` → `onWriteUrl` → the slot-1 `@rk_win_web_1` POST, § URL Bar above) and boots the tile for real — the bar is itself an init path. The **body panel** (replacing the frames; centered, monospace, on `bg-bg-primary`) carries a large dimmed `://` glyph, the heading **"Nothing to show yet"**, the subhead *"this tile follows the window's active web tab (@rk_win_web_N) — three ways to fill it:"*, three instruction rows (accent-green lead glyph, bold lead-in, `bg-bg-inset` bordered code chips): `❯` **Ask your agent to show something** — "Present this as a page" — the agent runs `rk present ./report.html` and it appears here (HTML files, diagrams, mocks, directories); `⇄` **Preview a dev server** — type `localhost:3000` in the address bar above (proxied through run-kit, works from any device), or have the agent run `rk present :3000`; `↗` **Open any URL** — external sites embed when they allow it, and find-in-page (⌘F) + back/forward work on same-origin pages. A dimmed footer reads *"the tile goes live automatically when an address lands ● no reload needed"*. The tile header renders the plain `://  Web` label — no kind badge, no page title, no meta chip (§ Surface Layout → Tile chrome). The flip is automatic in BOTH directions — `onboarding` derives from the tab family's emptiness (§ SSE Sync), so an empty→non-empty transition swaps the panel for the live frames with no user action, and non-empty→empty returns to onboarding; `present-auto-expand` is untouched — an already-open onboarding tile flips in place via the `withAutoWeb` identity arm (§ Surface Layout → Present auto-expand). The find entry points no-op here: the `Web: Find in page` palette entry is content-gated on `hasWebUrl` and the tile's own `web-find:open` listener double-guards ([keyboard-and-palette](/run-kit/ui/keyboard-and-palette.md) § `web-find`). (260821-zqlq, 9kip)

**Error states (fail loudly)**: a silent blank iframe is not a reachable state for a probed-blocked external URL or a dead proxied port — the tile renders an explicit error surface (`data-testid="web-tile-error"`) IN PLACE of the ACTIVE frame's visible area, copy per the design study (states 05/06). Three kinds: **frame refusal** — for `external`-kind addresses the engine calls `GET /api/frame-check` (`checkFrame`, [api-and-sockets](/run-kit/api-and-sockets.md) § API Layer) on address change; `embeddable: false` renders "`{host}` refuses embedding" + the reason line + an Open in browser ↗ button; **unreachable external** — `reachable: false` renders "`{host}` can't be reached" + the probe reason + the same hatch; **dead proxied port** — for `proxy`-kind addresses a same-origin `fetch` of the proxied path reads the reverse proxy's 502 (nothing listening) and renders "nothing listening on :{port}" + "connection refused — the dev server may have stopped" + a ↻ Retry button that re-runs detection and reloads (a nonce re-fires the probe effect). Present/relative kinds never probe; a same-origin fetch FAILURE (the app server itself down) is not a dead upstream and leaves the iframe alone. The probes live in the per-tab iframe engine and nowhere else (§ Design Decisions → Probes are engine-owned) and run on first mount and on ACTIVATION only — an inactive frame runs no probe timer and renders no error surface; only the active frame's reported `tileError` feeds the tile's error render. The error state clears on the next address change/retry. The errored frame's content stays MOUNTED but hidden beneath the error surface — the iframe engine hides its `<iframe>`, the native engine keeps its wrapper mounted and hides the guest (§ The `native` engine above; § Design Decisions → Error states render over a hidden, still-mounted iframe). (260819-v6y4, 9kip)

**Load progress line**: a 2px indeterminate accent-green sweep (`.rk-web-progress` in `globals.css`, `data-testid="web-load-progress"`) rendered at the content's top edge while the ACTIVE frame loads — the engine reports `loading` (set on the frame's src change/reload/history navigation, cleared on that frame's `load` event) and the chrome renders the line from the active entry (the element's presence IS the state; inactive frames carry no progress line). Under `prefers-reduced-motion` the sweep zeroes to a static dim line — load feedback is never motion-only. (260819-v6y4, 9kip)

**Content zoom**: the web tile magnifies its guest content by a per-viewer zoom level `s` applied as a SCALE WRAPPER on the ACTIVE frame — the chrome owns the level (the bucket, the triggers, the persistence) and hands it down as the engine's `zoom` prop; the iframe engine applies it to its own element, which renders inside the `overflow-hidden` wrapper (`data-testid="web-zoom-frame-wrapper"`) at `width/height: 100%/s` with `transform: scale(s); transform-origin: 0 0`, so zooming in shrinks the frame's CSS viewport and responsive guest layouts adapt exactly as under real browser zoom; at `s = 1` no transform is applied and the layout is identical to an unzoomed tile. The mechanism never reaches into the guest document, so it covers all four address kinds including cross-origin `external` (§ Design Decisions → Scale wrapper over guest-document CSS zoom). Zoom has TWO trigger families with different quantization (the Chrome/macOS convention): CLICK/SHORTCUT zoom steps the discrete ladder; GESTURES are CONTINUOUS — the factor tracks the fingers through arbitrary values, clamped to `[WEB_ZOOM_MIN, WEB_ZOOM_MAX]` (0.5–3, the ladder's ends). The pure, DOM-free `lib/web-zoom.ts` (colocated tests, the `web-url.ts` module contract) owns the derivations: the browser-standard ladder `WEB_ZOOM_LEVELS` (50%–300%, default/reset 1) + `stepWebZoom` (clamps at the ends, snaps an off-ladder value to the nearest level FIRST — the load-bearing bridge from a gesture-set float to the button/palette ladder: a `+` click from 137% snaps to 125% and lands on 150%); `webZoomKeyFor(url)` — the persistence bucket derived via `classifyAddress`: `external` → the URL's origin, `proxy`/loopback → `proxy:{port}`, `present`/`relative` → the single viewer-origin bucket `self` (presented files rotate paths, so per-path buckets would fragment state); and try/catch-noop `readWebZoom`/`writeWebZoom` over ONE localStorage key `runkit-web-zoom` holding a `{[bucket]: level}` map — `readWebZoom` returns the stored FLOAT clamped to the bounds, never snapped (a gesture-set 1.37 reloads as 1.37; the ladder snap lives solely in `stepWebZoom`), `writeWebZoom` rounds to 2 decimals and a round-to-1 value removes the entry (100% is the absent state, robust to gesture float noise), per-viewer state only, never POSTed (Constitution IV; spec window-views R7). `IframeWindow` seeds its zoom state from the ACTIVE address's bucket and re-seeds when the bucket changes — an active-tab change switches buckets (per-viewer, per-bucket — not per tab). Persistence timing differs by family: click-path writes are immediate; gesture writes ride a trailing debounce (`ZOOM_PERSIST_DEBOUNCE_MS = 250` — a pinch emits dozens of events) whose pending write FLUSHES, never drops, on unmount and on bucket change (the flush targets the OLD bucket before re-seeding). **Triggers**: (1) a compact control in the URL-bar cluster between the find ⌕ and open-external ↗ buttons (`data-testid="web-zoom-control"`; text-glyph `−`/`+` steppers with the percent readout between them doubling as the reset affordance — disabled at 100% — in the `w-7 h-7 rounded hover:bg-bg-card` + `Tip` URL-bar button vocabulary) — the universal floor trigger, and the only one that works over an `external` frame; NOT rendered in the onboarding state (the reduced URL bar keeps only refresh + address input). (2) The palette's `Web: Zoom in`/`Web: Zoom out`/`Web: Reset zoom` actions ([keyboard-and-palette](/run-kit/ui/keyboard-and-palette.md) § Command Palette Actions) dispatch the `web-zoom` document CustomEvent (`WEB_ZOOM_EVENT` in `lib/web-zoom.ts`, `detail.direction: "in" | "out" | "reset"` — the `web-find:open` precedent: the mounted tile is the single receiver, double-guarded to no-op on a contentless tile). (3) Ctrl/Cmd+wheel and touchpad-pinch gestures — CONTINUOUS, applied per event: each ctrl/meta wheel event maps `s → clamp(s * exp(-deltaY * WHEEL_ZOOM_SENSITIVITY))` (`applyWheelZoom` in the shared `lib/zoom-gesture.ts` — exponential, so equal deltas multiply equally and a reversed gesture returns to its start), and Safari's `gesturechange` sets `clamp(base * scale)` from a base captured at `gesturestart` (its scale is cumulative — no accumulator state); the stepped accumulator family in the same module remains the TERMINAL's ([terminal](/run-kit/ui/terminal.md) § Terminal Font Size → Gesture triggers — integer font steps are inherently discrete). Both families arrive through TWO non-passive capture arms of ONE chrome-owned `wireGestureListeners` installer (handed to the engine as a prop): the chrome attaches it on the tile's own wrapper (covering the URL bar, find bar, and error surface, all parent-document DOM), and the engine attaches it to each same-origin frame's document on every `load` (the chord-reclaim attach posture: try/catch, cross-origin/pre-load skips silently, re-attaches per navigation) — a wheel event over an iframe never reaches the parent document, so both arms are needed. Over a cross-origin (`external`) frame gestures are unreachable — accepted platform limit, no fallback attach; the chrome control and palette remain the triggers. Only ctrl/meta-modified wheel and Safari `gesture*` events are intercepted (`preventDefault` — the gesture never becomes browser page zoom); everything else passes through untouched. e2e: `web-tile-zoom.spec.ts` on the real-tmux rig (the `web-view-lens` pattern) proves control stepping, reset to 100%, persistence across reload, onboarding absence, palette stepping, and the continuous gesture semantics (an in-frame ctrl-wheel lands on the off-ladder `182%`, a small tick compounds with no threshold, and a `+` click snaps back onto the ladder) on a same-origin page. (260823-cwvv, 260824-iafo, 9kip)

**Proxy URL conversion**: `toProxySrc(url)` (`lib/web-url.ts`, § Address model above) maps an absolute loopback URL to its same-origin proxy path: `http://localhost:8080/docs` → `/proxy/8080/docs`. Portless loopback and every other address pass through unchanged (relative addresses are already same-origin). The native engine does NOT use it in `direct`/`proxy` mode — `toNativeSrc(url, mode)` (same module) re-expresses stored `/proxy/{port}` slots as literal loopback URLs there; `toProxySrc` remains the `legacy`-mode and iframe-engine mapping.

**Iframe attributes**: the engine renders its element with `sandbox="allow-same-origin allow-scripts allow-forms allow-popups allow-modals allow-downloads"` (without `allow-downloads`, VS Code file downloads break — the same sandbox `CodeSurface` reuses, § Code Surface), `title="Proxied content"`, `border-0`.

**Window creation**: "Tab: New Iframe Tab" command palette action (id `create-iframe-window`) opens a `Dialog` with two inputs: window name (autofocused, Enter focuses URL input) and URL (Enter creates window). Create button disabled until both fields non-empty. Calls `createWindow(server, session, name, undefined, url)` — the API client maps a given URL onto the `{ rkType: "iframe", rkUrl: <url> }` POST body (the backend keeps those body field names this release — Change 1 R10 — and stamps `@rk_win_layout=web` + `@rk_win_web_1` + `@rk_win_web_active=1`). Backend uses `CreateWindowWithOptions` for atomic `\;`-chained tmux command. Only shown when a session is active. Synthetic iframe windows are created this way. (260714-t97o, 260820-lfla, iip5)

### e2e — `web-native.spec.ts` (desktop lane)

`app/desktop/tests/e2e/web-native.spec.ts` — the native engine's end-to-end proof, run by `just test-desktop-e2e` against the worktree's derived rig with a fresh Electron shell per test (the launch/fixture contract lives in [architecture/testing](/run-kit/architecture/testing.md) § Desktop Electron E2E; the shell-side claims in [desktop-shell](/run-kit/desktop-shell.md) § Web Views → Testing). Seven independent tests, each seeding a tmux window stamped with a spec-owned `node:http` loopback stub as its web tab — the seeded e2e-a host's origin is what `rk url` resolves under the harness env, so its web mode is `direct` and guests load the stub's LITERAL loopback URL (no `/proxy/<port>/` hop; guest classification keys on that literal URL): (a) opening a web tab creates exactly one guest, visible, whose bounds match the placeholder rect within 1 px — the guest paints exactly over its tile; (b) the palette hides the guest and Escape shows it again — nothing the SPA draws can paint over the native layer; (c) the guest's document title reaches the tab strip through the `title` relay (guest → main → `web:event` → engine state → chrome); (d) ⌘K pressed inside the guest opens the SPA's palette — the chord-table upload, `before-input-event` match, focus hop, and `chord` relay + document re-dispatch end to end; (e) a host switch hides the outgoing host's guest, and switching back re-raises it above its host view in `contentView.children` order and shows it — the detach hide and the attach plan's re-add, read from Electron's tree; (f) closing the window destroys the guest's webContents — guests die with their window; (g) a `web:bounds` call while hidden parks the rect and leaves the guest hidden with unchanged live bounds. The sibling `web-retention.spec.ts` proves the retention and overlay rules end to end — a window switch parks the guest and returning adopts it (same webContents id, same page-side load token), a click-opened menu hides the guest and Escape restores it, and selecting `+` hides the previous guest behind the blank new-tab panel ([desktop-shell](/run-kit/desktop-shell.md) § Web Views → Testing). The `iframe` engine's coverage stays with the web specs — `web-tabs`, `web-tile-chrome`, `web-tile-find`, `web-tile-zoom`, `web-view-lens`, `present-*` — which drive it on the same rig. (260917-m3a9, ckpm)

## Code Surface (the `code` lens renderer)

`app/frontend/src/components/code-surface.tsx` — the renderer for the **`code` surface/lens** (§ Window Views (Lens Model)) — mounted as a tile by `SurfaceLayout` whenever the layout includes `code` (§ Surface Layout): via the top-bar surface toggles, the `Tile: Show Code` palette entry, or the switcher's `View: Code` row (which collapses the layout to the bare `code` leaf through `switchView`). It embeds **code-server** at the window's code root — a full editor (tabs, syntax highlighting, SCM view, side-by-side diffs) beside the live agent terminal with no editor functionality built in run-kit. It is a lean component, NOT an `IframeWindow` reuse: the code-server URL is fully DERIVED, so the URL bar (`@rk_win_web_<n>` substrate chrome) is meaningless here — the component is exactly an iframe plus the not-running empty state and the `code-surface-pending` state (§ Design Decisions → The code surface is a lean `CodeSurface`). (260811-k3vp)

- **src** — the primary form is `codeServerWorkspaceSrc(workspacePath)` = `/code/?workspace=<encoded path>` (relative, never a composed origin or port — the `toProxySrc` discipline, riding the same origin through any reverse proxy), where `<path>` is the derived per-tab workspace file whose `settings` block carries `rk.tab`/`rk.server` — the identity the bridge extension reads ([code-bridge](/run-kit/code-bridge.md) § Tab identity). The path is STABLE: code-server keys browser-side workspace state by the proxy pathname and the workspace identity, and `/code/` is fixed by the backend route (the port behind it is a private implementation detail, resolved server-side). The sibling `codeServerSrc(folder)` = `/code/?folder=<folder>` survives only as the DEGRADE form (a failed workspace GET, or a refused code-root seed — each degrades with exactly one console warning so the editor still opens, folder-keyed, the run-kit menu items hidden) and as the editor's own File > Open Folder navigation form, which the follow rule re-derives (§ The code root → Follow rule). The computed `src` is held in a **ref keyed to the iframe's MOUNT GENERATION** (the `reachable` gate): a code-root update never rewrites a live frame's `src`, while a genuine remount (a reachability `false→true` flip, or a frame eviction + re-creation under the retention LRU — § Frame retention below) boots the CURRENT workspace URL — with exactly two sanctioned re-navigation MECHANISMS of a live frame, the nonce-keyed `followSrc` re-navigation and `contentWindow.location.reload()` (§ Design Decisions → Two sanctioned re-navigation mechanisms, four triggers). A window switch alone is NOT a generation boundary: a retained frame's `reachable`/`src` props don't change on show/hide, so no effect re-arms.
- **Mount gating** — the iframe mounts only once the workspace path is known. `app.tsx` runs `useCodeWorkspace(server, windowId, win, codeTileOpen, seedRejected, { windowsById, liveWindowIds })` (`hooks/use-code-workspace.ts`), which fetches `GET /api/windows/{windowId}/code-workspace` (via `fetchCodeWorkspace` in `api/client.ts` over `deduplicatedFetch`; a `409` maps to a typed `{status:"no-root"}` result, never a throw) when the payload's substrate `win.codeRoot` is non-empty and the code tile is open. Resolved srcs live in an in-memory `Map` keyed `${server}:${windowId}:${root}` (plus the `seed-rejected:` namespace below) that survives window switches for the hook's lifetime: the derivation effect fires only for the ACTIVE window whose key has no entry, so a revisit renders its src synchronously (no `opening…` flash, no refetch) and a retained frame's src outlives its window's active period (nothing is persisted — in-memory viewer state, Constitution IV). Entries whose window left the threaded `liveWindowIds` set (killed/closed) are pruned alongside their frame's eviction; the map is otherwise unbounded (short-string values, small window counts). The per-window lookup `codeSrcFor(windowId)` — substrate entry winning over the seed-refusal fallback, resolving the window's current root through the threaded `windowsById` — is what `app.tsx` passes to `SurfaceLayout`; the active window's src and every retained frame's mount src read through it, and `app.tsx` additionally consumes the hook's `codeSrc` (the active window's resolved src) for the `Code: Reload Editor` palette gate. Until the path resolves, a reachable tile renders the pending state `opening…` (`data-testid="code-surface-pending"`, the `code-surface-empty` styling — an unreachable host keeps the not-running state, never pending); a `409` keeps pending and re-fetches on the next payload change (the first-ever open costs one SSE tick while the seed POST lands). A refused seed POST (e.g. a root outside `$HOME`) is recorded per (server, window, seed) so a deterministic `400` never becomes per-tick POST spam, and the hook's `seed-rejected:` key degrades the tile to `?folder=` instead of pending forever — a later successful seed re-arms the `?workspace=` path.
- **Availability vs reachability** (spec right-panel.md § Surface Registry): AVAILABILITY = `codeRootFor(win)` is non-empty — the shared `@rk_win_code_root` OR a `gitRoot` derives non-empty — the STABLE capability signal (`hasCode`, computed over the payload), so the surface toggle / switcher segment never strobe when the active pane leaves the repo (the port is always resolvable by convention, so the "configured" leg is always true). REACHABILITY (the host's TTL-cached probe, the state socket's `code-server` event) selects the surface's CONTENT only: reachable renders the iframe once the workspace path resolves (the `code-surface-pending` state until then); unreachable renders the terse monospace empty state `code-server not running — check rk doctor` (`data-testid="code-surface-empty"`) instead of a dead iframe.
- **Sandbox** — the same token set as `IframeWindow`, including `allow-downloads` (without it VS Code file downloads break).
- **Chord reclaim** — focus inside the iframe swallows run-kit's chords (`⌘K` collides with VS Code's own), and same-origin makes an escape hatch possible: a capture-phase `keydown` listener attached to the iframe's `contentDocument` after every load intercepts events matching the INJECTED `shouldReclaimChord` predicate (built in `app.tsx` from the keybinding registry's pure, kind-aware `hasReclaimableMatch(e, bindings, kind)` — `findMatches(...).some(...)` over the surface-restriction flags, here bound to the `"code"` kind), `preventDefault` + `stopImmediatePropagation`s them before the embedded app's keybinding service, and re-dispatches a synthetic bubbling `KeyboardEvent` on the parent **document** (reaching both document-level listeners — the palette chord — and window-level ones — the dispatcher). The reclaim carve-outs: a keydown whose ONLY registry matches are `ttyOnly` bindings (the tmux split pair — a keydown arriving inside the iframe means the code tile owns focus) is NOT reclaimed, so ⌘D reaches code-server's own keybinding service, and a `webOnly` match (⌘F web find) is NOT reclaimed in the code iframe either — code-server keeps its own find — while unrestricted registry chords (⌘K, ⌘;, ⌘2, ⌃`, …) are reclaimed — ⌃` deliberately preempts code-server's own integrated-terminal toggle, the hop back to tty winning over the embedded app's binding (260812-wfic, 260819-ie2i). The effect is keyed on `reachable` (the iframe only mounts when reachable, so a reachability flip re-runs it against the fresh iframe), re-attaches on `load` (each navigation replaces the document), guards `contentDocument` access in try/catch (a cross-origin or pre-load frame silently skips the attach — click-out remains the escape), and removes the listener from the attached document on cleanup. Proven by the `code-surface.spec.ts` e2e "keyboard spike: a registry chord pressed INSIDE the iframe reaches the parent" (§ Design Decisions → Chord-reclaim via a capture-phase iframe listener).
- **Load-time focus grab — guarded** — the code-server workbench grabs DOM focus once per iframe load (a script `focus()` at editor-restore time that no VS Code / code-server setting suppresses); a switch back to a RETAINED frame fires no load, so no grab — the grabs that need answering are the fresh-boot ones (first visit, post-eviction re-show, reachability recovery), which would otherwise win the window's focus and flip `focusedTileKind` to `code`, killing all `ttyOnly` keybindings. Per-window focus memory plus a steal guard answer it: `CodeSurface` reports the grab through the `onProgrammaticFocus?: () => boolean` prop, fed by a capture-phase `focusin` listener on the frame's contentDocument attached by the same load/attach effect as chord reclaim (the ONLY observable — a script `focus()` re-points the focus chain but fires no parent-side iframe event); `app.tsx` reverts an armed grab whose remembered kind is not `code`. The tile wrapper's `onFocus` consults the guard before `focusLeaf`, so a grab never settles the focused kind on `code` (§ Surface Layout → Tile renderer). Full model: [focus-ownership](/run-kit/ui/focus-ownership.md). (260815-ltz7)
- **First-boot rescue (positive empty-boot signal, one-shot reload)** — a never-cached `?workspace=` boot can load zero folders and never recover in place: VS Code web boots a `vscode-remote` `.code-workspace` from its server-side cached configuration first (`~/.rk/code-server-profile/User/caches/CachedConfigurations/workspaces/<id>/workspace.json`), so a never-cached workspace renders `NO FOLDER OPENED`, the late workspace-file load does not repopulate the live workbench, but it DOES write the cache — the next boot works. The oracle is the bridge extension's OWN empty-boot marker, never the absence of a host record: a good boot's record lands only after the remote extension host activates, which under load outlasts any fixed window (7 s measured with three concurrent workbenches), so absence cannot tell a slow boot from a broken one — while the extension provably runs `activate()` on the broken boot and reports it by writing `cb/boots/<hostId>.json`, whose stamp the status GET reports as `emptyBootAt` ([code-bridge](/run-kit/code-bridge.md) § Socket and registry contract, § Host resolution and liveness).
