The rule lives in the pure, DOM-free `lib/code-boot-rescue.ts` module (colocated `code-boot-rescue.test.ts`, the `code-folder-latch.ts` contract); `CodeSurface` only feeds it events and executes its one verb, driven by the optional injected **`fetchBridgeStatus` prop** — supplied PER FRAME from `app.tsx`'s `fetchBridgeStatusFor(windowId)` factory (`() => fetchCodeBridge(server, windowId)` bound to the FRAME's window, threaded through `SurfaceLayout` like the other code-surface seams: a retained frame's verdict can fire after its window stopped being active, and it must read its OWN window's bridge status — absent ⇒ the generation is inert: no fetches, no timer). Per `?workspace=` mount generation (the effect's `[reachable, src]` keying re-runs on every generation boundary — a reachability flip, an eviction + re-creation, or a `followSrc` nonce adoption — never a window switch alone, which keeps the retained frame's props unchanged — resetting baselines, timers, and budget): the status GET is issued ONCE at src adoption, capturing BOTH baselines (`startedAt` and `emptyBootAt`; an `unavailable` result leaves both `null`, and a `null` baseline is never confirmable — `newerThanBaseline` fails closed on it); the iframe's first `load` arms a `CODE_BOOT_RESCUE_WAIT_MS` (10 s) timer; at expiry a SECOND GET is the first verdict — `installed !== true` (false, or null when the verdict GET is unavailable — an old backend's 404 included) ⇒ `skip-not-installed`: no reload, exactly one `console.warn` per generation naming `rk code-server install`; a host record strictly newer than ITS baseline is the good-boot confirmation and settles the generation EARLY (no re-check, no reload — and it outranks a newer marker at the same read: a live tab-keyed record proves the folder loaded); a marker strictly newer than ITS baseline ⇒ `"reload"` fires ONE `iframe.contentWindow.location.reload()` inside the component's try/catch posture — never a `src` write, so the `?workspace=` URL and tab identity survive; NEITHER stamp newer ⇒ exactly ONE re-check timer arms for `CODE_BOOT_RESCUE_RECHECK_MS` (10 s) later, whose read applies the same decision and then settles regardless. `newerThanBaseline(baseline, current)` — the one stamp-compare helper both stamps ride, replacing the retired `bridgeConfirmed` — is true iff `current` is a parseable non-empty stamp AND (`baseline === ""` OR `Date.parse(current) > Date.parse(baseline)`): the `""` baseline (read OK, nothing existed at adoption) accepts any parseable marker, a `null` baseline never confirms, and the browser clock is never consulted. At most THREE reads and ONE reload per generation, no interval loop; a settled generation ignores later `load` events (`settled` is set before each verdict fetch); unmount or a generation change clears pending timers and discards in-flight reads. `?folder=` mounts are never rescued (a folder-opened host writes no tab identity, so neither signal can exist). Until the updated extension is installed (`rk code-server update` / `rk update`) the backend reports `emptyBootAt: ""` and the rescue never fires — fail-closed. The host-record stamp can never produce `"reload"`. Unit coverage: `code-boot-rescue.test.ts` (the `newerThanBaseline` baseline/parse table and the full `decideRescue` decision table) and `code-surface.test.tsx` fake-timer scenarios over a mocked fetcher (newer marker at the re-check ⇒ three reads, one reload; newer record at the first verdict ⇒ two reads, no re-check, no reload; nothing newer at either verdict ⇒ three reads, no reload, a later `load` re-arms nothing; newer marker at the first verdict ⇒ one reload, no re-check; unavailable ⇒ no reload + one warn; remount ⇒ fresh baselines; `?folder=` / prop absent ⇒ inert). e2e `code-surface.spec.ts` carries both arms against the per-run `XDG_STATE_HOME` the harness forwards to the spec and the backend alike: the NEGATIVE arm ("a confirming bridge host record suppresses the rescue reload (exactly one iframe load)") writes a fake pid-alive host record after the baseline read and asserts exactly one `load`; the POSITIVE arm ("a newer empty-boot marker reloads the frame exactly once (positive rescue arm)") writes a fake pid-alive marker under `${XDG_STATE_HOME}/run-kit/cb/boots/` after the baseline read and asserts exactly two `load`s across the verdict + re-check window. `scripts/test-e2e.sh` makes the backend's `installed` answer deterministic: it plants a fixture bridge-extension manifest (`run-kit.rk-code-bridge-0.0.0-e2e/package.json`) under a per-run `XDG_DATA_HOME` and forwards that variable ONLY to the dev-server launch line, never to the Playwright run — the rig's only `XDG_DATA_HOME` reader is the backend's `codeserver.ExtensionsDir` derivation (260910-74q0-code-tile-first-boot-rescue-reload, 260910-oa3c-code-rescue-positive-signal-record-ownership).
- **User-initiated reload (the `reloadNonce` seam)** — the header's Reload editor verb (§ Surface Layout → Tile chrome) reboots the ACTIVE window's frame in place through the rescue's own mechanism: `CodeSurface` takes an optional `reloadNonce?: number`, with a `reloadNonceRef` initialized to the mount-time prop value so the value present at mount is pre-seen (a mount IS a fresh boot) and an already-seen nonce (every ordinary re-render) is inert; an effect keyed on `[reloadNonce]` runs exactly one `iframeRef.current?.contentWindow?.location.reload()` in the rescue's try/catch posture when a number not seen before by that instance arrives — and only while the iframe is mounted (`reachable && src !== null`; a nonce arriving while the tile pends or is unreachable is recorded as seen and no-ops, the next mount booting fresh anyway). Never a `src` write, never an unmount. `SurfaceLayout` holds `codeReload: { windowId, nonce } | null` state and passes the nonce ONLY to the frame whose `windowId` matches — every other frame receives `undefined`, and a frame created later pre-sees whatever value is current at its mount, so a reload can never replay across windows or remounts. The reload is transparent to the first-boot rescue: it fires no new mount generation (the `src` ref is untouched), a settled generation ignores the extra `load`, an unsettled one at most re-arms its existing wait timer, and the chord-reclaim / folder-report / focus-grab listeners re-attach on that `load` exactly as after the rescue's reload. e2e: `code-surface.spec.ts` proves the verb reloads the live frame in place (one extra `load`, same element, unchanged `src`), hides while the tile pends, reloads only the active window's frame with a retained frame untouched, and renders no verbs on the not-running empty state. (c5w2)
- **Frame retention — a bounded per-server LRU of live frames** — a code frame survives a same-server window switch: the tile wrapper's React key is the frame's identity, `code:<windowId>:<creation src>` (`code:pending` while the src is unresolved — no iframe exists to lose), so a switch re-renders the mounted grid instead of unmounting the iframe (an iframe unmount is a page unload — VS Code web disconnects gracefully and code-server kills that workbench's extension host; a display-hidden frame re-shows in ~16 ms vs a measured 1.4 s chrome / ~2 s explorer / ~2.9 s SCM warm boot, 7 s extension host under load). `SurfaceLayout` holds the retention state as an ordered list of `{windowId, src, root}` frame records, most-recently-shown LAST — cross-window component state the `[server, windowId]` reset effect never touches (the grid is keyed by `server`, so the list is per-server by construction). A record is created only for the ACTIVE window once its src has resolved and its code tile is open — a pending tile has no frame and never counts — and an existing record bumps to most-recently-shown on every show (a retained frame never bumps itself: it cannot become visible without being the active window). The list caps at `CODE_FRAME_CAP_DESKTOP = 3` / `CODE_FRAME_CAP_MOBILE = 1` (the existing `isMobile` prop selects; the cap counts the active window's frame — desktop = active + up to 2 retained), because each live frame is one ~250–320 MB extension-host process on the server plus a browser renderer. Eviction IS unmount (the ordinary graceful-disconnect path) and is payload-driven, no timers: (a) LRU overflow when a new frame is created — the least-recently-shown record (list head) goes, never the active frame; (b) the record's window leaves the threaded `liveWindowIds` set (killed/closed); (c) the window's live `codeRootFor` diverges from the record's `root` baseline by anything other than a follow (a transient empty read never evicts; a follow moves the baseline in place — § The code root → Follow rule); (d) `codeReachable` flips `true→false` — every record drops, and the next `false→true` starts from an empty LRU. A runtime cap decrease (an `isMobile` flip) evicts down immediately, oldest NON-ACTIVE records first — the active window's record is protected even while its code tile is closed (a closed tile can't bump its record to the tail through the show effect); closing the code tile in a window keeps its frame retained and counted, and the active window's record renders through the same hidden-tile slot when its layout omits `code` (the per-window `everOpened` reset reseeds from the new window's layout on a switch, so the record — not the set — carries the retention). Every non-active record renders as a display-hidden tile wrapper (`hidden` class, slot `-1`, never `visibility`/off-screen) through the same flat `allTiles` list on BOTH the desktop and mobile branches, so the visible↔hidden transition never remounts; its testid is `surface-tile-code-retained` with a `data-window-id` attribute — `surface-tile-code` stays unique to the active window's tile for locators. **Per-frame seams**: each frame's `CodeSurface` receives props bound to ITS window — `gitRoot` = the record's baseline root, `workspaceSrc` = the record's creation src (fixed; the mount-generation rule pins the iframe's src), `followSrc` only for the active window's frame, `fetchBridgeStatus` from the per-window `fetchBridgeStatusFor(windowId)` factory. A retained frame carries NO `onInteract`/`onProgrammaticFocus`/`onFolderNavigated` bindings (a display-hidden frame can neither receive focus nor navigate, so the seams can never fire — omitting them removes the stale-closure class: nothing can record focus or a follow against the ACTIVE window's key from a hidden frame), and it regains them on becoming active WITHOUT a remount — the stable key means the same instance re-renders with the seams bound. The eviction baseline's follow handling and the rejected alternatives live in § Design Decisions → Code frames are retained across window switches by an LRU inside `SurfaceLayout`. e2e: `code-surface.spec.ts` proves a switch away and back re-shows the retained frame with exactly ONE iframe `load` and the same element, and that no second focus grab fires on the return. (260916-az44-persistent-code-frames-lru)

### The code root — `@rk_win_code_root` seed + follow

The folder the editor opens is the shared, per-window tmux option **`@rk_win_code_root`** (surfaced on the payload as `codeRoot`), never the live SSE derivation. `gitRoot` is re-derived per tick from the ACTIVE pane's cwd (backend `deriveGitRoot` — the git toplevel when found, else the pane's raw cwd), so a pane switch or a `cd` would otherwise retarget the embedded editor to another workspace, taking in-flight editor state with it (open tabs, dirty buffers, undo stacks). Derivation's only job is to SEED. Because the state is a window option, every viewer of the tab renders the same folder and a snapshot restore carries it with the window. (260813-if5d, iip5)

- **Module** — `lib/code-folder-latch.ts` is pure and DOM-free with colocated tests (the `window-view.ts` / `right-panel.ts` module contract): `codeRootFor(win)` = `win?.codeRoot || win?.gitRoot || ""` — the folder the code surface opens and the availability predicate's input — and `codeRootSeed(win, layout)` = `leaves(layout).includes("code") && !win?.codeRoot && win?.gitRoot ? win.gitRoot : null` — the seed decision. `codeRootFollowTarget(win)` is the drift predicate behind the Follow terminal verb (the drift bullet below): the live `gitRoot` when the latched `codeRoot` and the derivation are both non-empty and DIFFER, else `null` — the same "null means no write" return shape as `codeRootSeed`.
- **Seed rule — derivation runs exactly once** — the seed POSTs `setWindowOptions({ "@rk_win_code_root": gitRoot })` the first time the code surface actually RENDERS for a window whose `codeRoot` is empty, keyed on `leaves(layout).includes("code")`, the choke point every entry path resolves through (the surface toggles, `View: Code`, the mobile switch group). Availability alone seeds nothing: an offered-but-never-opened code lens is not an open editor. An empty derivation seeds nothing either — but the derivation is empty only when no cwd is resolvable at all (a window with no panes and no worktree path); a window outside every repo derives its pane's raw cwd and offers the code surface on that folder. A per-window in-flight ref suppresses a second POST while the payload still reads empty (the SSE tick arrives after the POST); after seeding, derivation never moves the editor — not on a pane switch, not on tile close/reopen, not on reload.
- **Follow rule — the editor, an explicit verb, and the shell move it** — `CodeSurface`'s `load`-event seam (the same one the chord reclaim re-attaches on) also reads the same-origin `contentWindow.location`, parses `folder`, and reports it via `onFolderNavigated` when present, non-empty, and different (decoded comparison) from the current folder — a frame at `?workspace=…` carries no `folder` param, so the seam stays silent on the primary form; `SurfaceLayout` passes it through as `onCodeFolderNavigated` and `app.tsx`'s handler (a no-op when the folder equals the current root): (1) POSTs `setWindowOptions({ "@rk_win_code_root": folder })` as before, (2) re-fetches the workspace path for the new root, and (3) re-navigates the LIVE frame to the new `?workspace=` URL via the nonce-keyed `followSrc` prop (`{src, nonce, root}` — the `root` rides along so the frame record's eviction baseline can move with the follow) — one of the two sanctioned re-navigation mechanisms (§ Design Decisions → Two sanctioned re-navigation mechanisms, four triggers); a failed follow fetch leaves the editor at its own working `?folder=` navigation (the shell-initiated verb instead degrades onto it — below). **The follow is never an eviction**: the frame's follow wrapper — `requestCodeFollow(frameWindowId, folder, report)`, the ONE wrapper both follow triggers ride (the editor-initiated `onFolderNavigated` report and the header's Follow terminal verb) — records a pending-follow target (`{windowId, root}`) SYNCHRONOUSLY at report time, BEFORE the latch POST — the option write wakes the SSE hub, so the payload tick carrying the new `codeRoot` can land before the POST response and the re-derivation GET produce the nonce, and the root-divergence eviction must not read the follow's own write as an external change. Both tick orderings are covered: payload-first — the eviction effect sees a divergence TOWARD the pending target, moves the record's baseline in place, and consumes the target; nonce-first — the nonce-adoption effect moves the baseline and the target STAYS set until the payload's current root observes it (an interim tick matches target == moved baseline and never evicts), consumed on arrival. Pending targets are keyed PER WINDOW (a `Map<windowId, root>`), so overlapping follows across a window switch inside one SSE round trip keep both. A REJECTED latch POST clears the window's target — `app.tsx`'s handler re-throws after its error toast and the wrapper's `.catch` deletes it — so nothing diverges and nothing evicts, and a later external update to the same folder reads as a non-follow divergence instead of inheriting the failed follow; targets whose record left the list are dropped as stale. So File > Open Folder inside code-server moves the shared code root AND restores the workspace identity the bare navigation dropped, while the terminal never moves the editor on its own. The header's **Follow terminal** verb is the second writer beside it: the verb rides the same `requestCodeFollow` wrapper (the pending-target record covers it by construction) with its own parent callback `onCodeFollowTerminal` — `app.tsx`'s `handleCodeFollowTerminal` POSTs the latch then calls `followFolder(folder, { degradeToFolder: true })`, so a FAILED workspace re-derivation lands the live frame on `/code/?folder=<gitRoot>` with exactly one console warning (the verb's frame still sits on the OLD folder, unlike the editor-initiated follow whose frame already navigated itself, so leave-in-place would leave the option and the editor disagreeing); a `codeFollowInFlight` guard disables the verb from click until the follow promise settles, the payload still reading the OLD root until the option tick. The shell twin is `rk tab code set <folder>` — the same option write (`SetWindowOptions`; it never writes the workspace file — the GET is the single writer), so a CLI-set root repaints every viewer on the next option tick. The location read carries the attach's try/catch posture — a cross-origin or pre-load frame skips silently.
- **The render feed** — the payload record carries `codeRoot` directly, so `hasCode`, `availableTiles`, `degradeLayout`, the tile render guard, `tileMeta`, and the seed/fetch gating all read it with no substitution layer: `SurfaceLayout` computes `gitRoot={codeRootFor(win)}` for `CodeSurface` (the latch comparison baseline — a retained frame's from its record) and mounts each frame at its record's creation src, resolved through the hook's per-window `codeSrcFor` lookup (§ Code Surface → Mount gating, Frame retention). A tab whose active pane left the repo keeps offering `code` (`hasCode` reads `codeRoot` as availability) and the editor stays on the shared root.
- **Drift is visible, not silent** — when the latched root differs from the live derivation, the code tile header renders the **Follow terminal** verb exactly while `codeRootFollowTarget(win)` is non-null (§ Module above): the verb's presence IS the drift indicator, and clicking it re-seeds `@rk_win_code_root` from the live `gitRoot` through the follow path, the verb disappearing once the payload tick lands and the roots agree again. Empty inputs never count as drift — a pre-seed window (`codeRoot` empty) is about to be seeded from `gitRoot` anyway, and an empty derivation (no cwd resolvable) has no folder to follow. A stale root that STILL equals the derivation (a deleted worktree both point at) shows no verb and keeps rendering code-server's own "folder does not exist" state; the escapes are File > Open Folder and `rk tab code set`. No liveness probe. (c5w2)
- **One-shot latch migration (one release)** — the route-entry translation effect (§ Surface Layout → `app.tsx` integration) migrates a surviving per-browser `runkit-code-folder:{server}:{@N}` localStorage key: when `win.codeRoot` is empty and the key holds a non-empty path, one POST seeds `@rk_win_code_root` from it; the key is deleted either way. (iip5)
- **e2e — `code-folder-latch.spec.ts`** — real tmux on the port-3020 rig with the `code-surface.spec.ts` code-server stub: a window with one pane inside a repo and one outside proves the first code-tile render seeds `@rk_win_code_root` (asserted via `_tmux.ts` `windowOption`), that the code tile survives the active pane leaving the repo and reopens at the seeded root across a reload (persistence is tmux's), and that the option is substrate state shared across viewers. The follow half is e2e-covered through the verb ("the Follow terminal verb re-seeds the code root from the drifted terminal cwd"): splitting the pane into a fresh `mkdtemp` dir under `$HOME` (a non-repo cwd, so the derivation returns the raw cwd — and inside `$HOME` because the code-root write path's validation refuses anything outside it) leaves `@rk_win_code_root` on the seeded root while `gitRoot` tracks the split, the `Follow terminal` verb appears, clicking it re-seeds the option to the derived root and re-navigates the SAME iframe element to the new `?workspace=` URL (a follow re-navigates, never remounts), and the verb disappears once the roots agree. Live File > Open Folder navigation itself is unit-tested only (no live code-server in the harness).

## GUI Surface (the `gui` lens renderer)

`app/frontend/src/components/gui-surface.tsx` — the renderer for the **`gui` surface/lens** (§ Window Views (Lens Model); spec `docs/specs/gui.md`), mounted as a tile by `SurfaceLayout`'s `case "gui"` and **lazy-loaded** (`export default` + `React.lazy` with a `Suspense` fallback styled like `code-surface-pending`) so tabs that never open a gui tile never pay for noVNC's ~150 KB core. The component embeds the host desktop through **noVNC's `RFB` class on a bare host `<div>`** (no `vnc.html` iframe — the library gives rk the focus/chord seams without a cross-document boundary), connecting through a `WebSocket` the component constructs itself at the **absolute** `ws(s)://<location.host>/ws/gui/host` URL built from `location` (never a relative path — the embed must work behind any proxy) and hands to noVNC 1.7's raw-channel constructor form `new RFB(hostEl, socket, { shared: true })` — `types/novnc.d.ts` types the constructor's second parameter `string | WebSocket`, and owning the socket object is what lets the stats seam count bytes on it (§ The stats overlay below). The backend contract (the switch, supervisor, relay, payload) lives in [gui](/run-kit/gui.md). (o2sp) (5psw)

**The `gui` signal — `useGui()`**: `contexts/session-context.tsx` handles the host-global `event: gui` socket slot in its own context (the `CodeServerContext` precedent): the payload is a LIST (`[]gui.StreamEntry`), narrowed to the `id === "host"` entry (fallback `[0]`) field-by-field with type guards (`enabled`/`reachable` booleans, `backend`/`display`/`wm`/`geometry` strings, `width`/`height`/`viewers` numbers — a malformed payload narrows to safe `false`/`""`/`0` defaults, never throws; a missing or non-string `geometry` reads `""`, the `wm` narrowing's shape (zuci)), deduped on the raw payload string so the every-tick broadcast does not re-render consumers. `useGui(): GuiSignal | null` returns `null` before the first event, which reads as **not available** — the opposite of `useCodeServer`'s null semantics, because the gui surface is never on by default and a late replay must not flash a button (D3).

**Availability — the 4-kind registry**: `lib/window-view.ts` exports `GuiHost = { enabled: boolean } | null | undefined` and `hasGui(host)` (`host?.enabled === true`); `availableViews(win, host?)` pushes `gui` when it holds, and `HINT_ORDER` is `[code, gui, web, tty]`. `lib/surface-layout.ts`'s `availableTiles(win, host?)` pushes `gui` **last** (the ⌘4 position) iff `hasGui(host)`; `degradeLayout`/`effectiveLayout(win, host?)` and `lib/right-panel.ts`'s `availableSurfaces(win, host?)` thread the same optional arg. Only `enabled` gates availability — `reachable`, `backend`, and `viewers` select the tile's CONTENT, never its existence (the code split). The host signal arrives as an **argument, not a store read**, so the pure modules stay DOM-free and colocated-testable (§ Design Decisions → Host signal is an argument, not a store read). `SURFACE_LABEL.gui = "GUI"`; `SURFACE_GLYPH.gui = "[]"` (the 2-char ASCII window frame, matching the `>_` / `://` / `{}` grammar); `SURFACE_KINDS` gains `"gui"`, so `parseLayoutTree("h(tty,gui)")` parses (as does the legacy `split-h:tty,gui`) and the backend `layoutspec` registry accepts gui-bearing `@rk_win_layout` writes ([backend-packages](/run-kit/architecture/backend-packages.md) § Backend Libraries). **Off degrades, on restores, the option is never written**: with the switch off, `h(tty,gui)` degrades to the bare `tty` leaf — the gui leaf drops out, the remaining structure kept — while `@rk_win_layout` stays as written; when the payload flips `enabled: true` the same option renders the gui tile again — no `/options` write on either transition. Every toggle/palette/chord path keys off the same `availableTiles` result, so the 4th top-bar button, the overflow Tiles row, and the mobile switch group appear and disappear on the stream alone, with no gui-specific branch in `SurfaceToggleGroup`.

**Content states**:

| State | Render |
|-------|--------|
| `enabled && reachable` | the canvas host `<div>` (`data-testid="gui-surface-canvas"`) with the live `RFB` |
| `enabled && reachable && wm === "" && backend !== "screen-sharing"` (and the viewer has not dismissed it) | the canvas PLUS the bare-WM strip (`data-testid="gui-wm-strip"`) above it — below |
| `enabled && !reachable` | the empty state (`data-testid="gui-surface-empty"`): `GUI is on but not running` + the `reason` from `GET /api/gui/host` (fetched ONCE per unreachable transition — never polled; the stream drives re-fetch; a failed GET leaves the state without a reason line) + two boxed controls: **Restart supervisor** (`POST /api/gui/host/restart` via the `onRestart` seam — a 409 arrives as `{ ok: false, disabled: true }` and renders `gui turned off`) and **Open supervisor logs** (`onOpenLogs` navigates to the `rk-gui` session's `host` window on the `rk-daemon` server) |
| connected, then RFB `disconnect` | while `reachable` stays true the canvas stays mounted under a `disconnected — reconnecting…` overlay (`data-testid="gui-surface-reconnecting"`), re-dialing on a 1 s → 2 s → 4 s → 8 s capped backoff (reset on `connect`); `reachable` flipping false swaps in the empty state and clears the timer |
| `!enabled` | never mounted — degradation removed the tile |

**The bare-WM strip**: on a reachable host whose supervisor resolved no WM rung (`wm === ""`; never the screen-sharing mirror — § Design Decisions → The bare-WM strip and launch rows hide on the screen-sharing backend) a one-line strip renders as the FIRST child of the `gui-surface-canvas` wrapper (already `flex flex-col`), BEFORE the noVNC host `<div ref={hostRef} className="flex-1 min-h-0" />` — a flex sibling, never an absolute overlay, so the host div (the `flex-1` child noVNC observes for `scaleViewport`/`resizeSession`) shrinks by the strip's height and the fit plus SetDesktopSize see the reduced canvas with no measurement code. The host div carries a stable `key="novnc-host"` pinning its DOM identity: without it a canvas⇄empty-state branch switch could let React reuse the node for a same-position sibling, and the connect effect's `hostEl.replaceChildren()` cleanup would wipe THAT element's content. The strip is monospace (`font-mono text-xs`, `role="status"`, `border-b`), wrapping to two lines at phone width, content in order: the text `No window manager on the GUI host`; when the install line is known, ` — <install line>`; ` · then ` followed by an inline text button **Restart supervisor** firing the existing `onRestart` seam (its `ok:false` is ignored — a 409 means the switch flipped off and the stream unmounts the tile); when the line is known, a **Copy** button (`aria-label="Copy install line"`, the shared `Control` chip shape, so coarse-pointer minimums ride the shared token) that copies ONLY the install line via `copyToClipboard` (`src/lib/clipboard.ts`) and reads `Copied` for 1.5 s on success (a `false` result leaves the strip unchanged); and **×** (`aria-label="Dismiss"`, same `Control` shape). The install line is the status document's `wm_hint` — package-manager-aware wording built server-side, never hardcoded in the frontend — fetched via `fetchGuiStatus()` ONCE per bare transition, mirroring the reason fetch's grammar (a `hintFetchedRef` guard re-armed by the StrictMode-replay cleanup, a `cancelled` flag on late resolution, never polled); a failed GET leaves the line unknown and the strip still renders, without the install segment and without Copy; `wm` non-empty or `reachable` false re-arms the transition and clears the stored line. The dismissal is the per-viewer localStorage key `runkit-gui-wm-strip-dismissed` (`readGuiWmStripDismissed`/`writeGuiWmStripDismissed` in `lib/gui-posture.ts`), read on mount; × writes `"1"` and hides the strip; an effect observing `gui.wm` clears state and storage whenever `wm !== ""` (so a LATER bare state shows the strip again); a `reachable` flip never clears it. (vu4p)

**The R6 dot**: `onConnectionChange(connected)` fires on RFB `connect`/`disconnect`; `app.tsx` folds it into the per-surface `showDot` predicate — for `gui` the dot is the live connection state while a tile is open, falling back to the signal's `reachable` when none is (VNC health, like web's dot is "has content").

**The resize policy** — `rfb.resizeSession = !coarsePointer && focused && !resizeLocked && !hostLocked && geometry === "auto"`, recomputed on every prop change through `propsRef` (noVNC applies the setter live, sending SetDesktopSize on the next size change), with `geometry = gui?.geometry ?? ""` riding `propsRef` like `hostLocked`. The host's `gui.geometry` setting owns the desktop size ([gui](/run-kit/gui.md) § The `gui.geometry` key): its `auto` value is the follow-the-tile policy — exactly the D7 behavior — under which only the **focused, fine-pointer, unlocked** viewer drives the shared desktop's resolution, the last-focused fine viewer winning by construction; any fixed `WxH` (or the `""` of a disabled host) forces `resizeSession` false for EVERY viewer (V-D4), so the desktop keeps its configured size and the tiles letterbox to its aspect — fit mode's `scaleViewport` preserves aspect by construction (V-D5), no other rendering change needed. A coarse-pointer viewer NEVER sets it (a phone connecting cannot change the payload's `width`/`height`). Under `auto` the two pins keep their meaning: the viewer-local lock (`rk-gui-lock`) pins a fine viewer out, and the host pin — `rk gui lock`, streamed as the entry's `locked` and read as `hostLocked = gui?.locked ?? false`, re-applied on every stream entry change — pins EVERY viewer out (see [gui](/run-kit/gui.md) § Agent verbs); under a fixed geometry both pins are inert and the palette's Lock rows render disabled with the fixed-size description ([keyboard-and-palette](/run-kit/ui/keyboard-and-palette.md) § The `GUI:` palette family). (d0za) (zuci) Quality rides the per-viewer `rk-gui-quality` posture (5psw): the `quality` prop names one of `GUI_QUALITY_PRESETS`'s presets — `sharp` (8,1), `balanced` (6,2), `smooth` (3,7) — and `applyRfbProps` sets `rfb.qualityLevel`/`rfb.compressionLevel` from that tuple, applied live through the every-render prop effect with no re-dial; `readGuiQuality(coarsePointer)` resolves an absent or invalid stored value to the pointer-class default (`balanced` on fine, `smooth` on coarse), and app.tsx owns the `guiQuality` state beside `guiZoom`/`guiPointerMode` (the palette's `GUI: Quality →` rows write it — [keyboard-and-palette](/run-kit/ui/keyboard-and-palette.md) § The `GUI:` palette family; the named-presets contract: [gui](/run-kit/gui.md) § Design Decisions → Quality presets are names; the tuple table is internal). `showDotCursor = true`, `focusOnClick = true`, `background = ""`. No `devicePixelRatio` scaling — noVNC sizes the canvas in CSS px.

**Zoom — the `rk-gui-zoom` ladder and the sized host**: zoom is a per-viewer posture (localStorage `rk-gui-zoom`, owned as `app.tsx` state and threaded through `SurfaceLayout`): `fit` (the default — the uniform letterboxed fit, `scaleViewport`) or a percentage from `GUI_ZOOM_STEPS` (50/75/100/125/150/200). The pure `stepGuiZoom` (`lib/gui-posture.ts`) steps the ladder asymmetrically: zooming IN from `fit` lands on 100 (1:1, never 50/75); zooming OUT below 100 runs 75 → 50 → `fit`; both ends saturate. A legacy `rk-gui-view: "1:1"` reads as `100` once (the one-time migration in `readGuiZoom`) and `writeGuiZoom` removes the legacy key; any out-of-table value reads as `fit`. The rendering mechanism is the **sized host** (§ Design Decisions → The sized host drives gui zoom): `scaleViewport` stays true at EVERY zoom and `clipViewport` stays false (public API only) — at a percentage `z` the noVNC host div (`gui-novnc-host`, `shrink-0`) is sized by the pure `zoomedHostSize(fbW, fbH, zoom, dpr)` (`lib/gui-posture.ts`) to `fb.w·z/(100·dpr)` × `fb.h·z/(100·dpr)` CSS px (the framebuffer from the gui stream entry's `width`/`height`; `undefined` at `fit` or a zero framebuffer dimension — the host stays tile-sized), so `Display.autoscale` (`min(w/fb.w, h/fb.h)`, no 1.0 cap) yields exactly `z/100` and `_display.scale` equals the canvas's visual scale by construction, keeping `absX`/`absY` pointer mapping exact on every step; noVNC's own ResizeObserver recomputes the same value, so mid-zoom browser/tile resizes are self-healing with zero re-application code. The `dpr` argument is `hidpi ? window.devicePixelRatio : 1` — with the per-viewer `rk-gui-hidpi` posture on, a 100% zoom maps one framebuffer pixel to one device pixel (crisp 1:1 on a Retina display); HiDPI is client-side rendering only — `resizeSession`, `gui.geometry`, and every server-facing value are untouched, and `fit` is unaffected (the fit scale is tile-bound) (t2lv). While zoomed `resizeSession` is held false (the formula gains a `zoom === "fit"` clause): noVNC's ResizeObserver watches that screen div, and `_requestRemoteResize` would request the deliberately-larger scaled size as the remote desktop size — the gui-signal echo would then grow the framebuffer that feeds the host size, a runaway feedback loop under `auto`; the five-clause formula resumes verbatim at `fit`. Every zoom change (palette, chord, wheel, pinch) shows the monospace corner badge `gui-zoom-badge` (`150%` or `fit`) for `ZOOM_BADGE_MS = 1500` — a change restarts the timer, never on mount, cleared on unmount. (0aur)

**The stats overlay** (`components/gui-stats-overlay.tsx`; the per-viewer `rk-gui-stats-visible` posture in the `rk-gui-lock` `"1"`/absent shape, default off — the ergonomics plan's V-D11): while the `statsVisible` prop is true the canvas branch mounts `GuiStatsOverlay` (`data-testid="gui-stats-overlay"`) absolutely at the canvas wrapper's top-right in the zoom badge's exact classes, rendering `formatGuiStats`'s line `{fps} fps · {mbit} Mbit/s · {rtt} ms · {W}×{H} · {zoom}` — integer fps, Mbit/s to one decimal below 10 and an integer at or above, integer ms, each unsampled value `—` (so a fresh overlay reads `— fps · — Mbit/s · — ms · 1920×1080 · fit`), zoom `fit` or `N%` — and the zoom badge is suppressed while the overlay is up (they share the corner, and the overlay's zoom segment is live). Only while `statsVisible && connected` does the collector run (§ Design Decisions → Stats collection is opt-in and idle when hidden): fps counts canvas-source `drawImage` calls (`image instanceof HTMLCanvasElement` — noVNC's `Display.flip`, the same criterion `gui-perf.spec.ts` probes) through a wrap installed on the tile canvas's own 2D context INSTANCE (obtained via `hostEl.querySelector("canvas").getContext("2d")` after connect and restored on cleanup — never the prototype, never `_display`); Mbit/s comes from binary bytes (`ArrayBuffer.byteLength` / `Blob.size`) counted by an `addEventListener("message")` on the self-constructed socket, which rides the socket's whole life as a cheap increment (noVNC's `Websock.attach` sets `binaryType`/`onmessage` and coexists with it); a `STATS_SAMPLE_MS` (1000) interval folds the counters into the snapshot via `sampleRates`; and every `STATS_PING_MS` (5000) a `pingGui()` round trip is timed with `performance.now()` into the RTT segment — `null` until the first resolves and after any failure, the interval carrying the `review-ignore` carve-out (a measurement probe, not state polling; the endpoint: [gui](/run-kit/gui.md) § HTTP routes). Hiding the overlay, a disconnect, or an unmount clears both intervals and restores `drawImage`. The pure math — the constants, `sampleRates`, `formatGuiStats`, the `GuiStats` type — lives in `lib/gui-stats.ts` with colocated tests. app.tsx owns the `guiStatsVisible` state (seeded from `readGuiStatsVisible()`, its handler writing `writeGuiStatsVisible`) and threads it with `guiQuality` through `SurfaceLayout`'s optional `guiQuality` (default `"balanced"`) / `guiStatsVisible` (default `false`) props into `GuiSurface`'s `quality`/`statsVisible`. (5psw)

**Pan, and the zoom chords**: when the scaled desktop exceeds the tile the wrapper (`overflow: hidden`) pans by clamped `scrollLeft`/`scrollTop` so the canvas always covers the tile (no overscroll gap): a FINE pointer left-drags on the canvas — the press is swallowed in the capture phase so it never reaches noVNC as a guest button, and a release under `PAN_DRAG_THRESHOLD_PX` (10) replays to the canvas as a plain click; a COARSE pointer in `touch` mode one-finger drags — an observing (passive, capture-phase) touch tracker scrolls the wrapper while `dragViewport` stays on so noVNC swallows the drag as a viewport gesture instead of sending the guest a left-drag (noVNC's own pan moves only a CLIPPING display — `_updateClip` force-disables clipping under `scaleViewport`, so the native drag pan is a visual no-op here and the tile drives its own scroll); in `trackpad` mode the viewport follows the virtual cursor (cursor-edge follow via the tile's `ensureCursorVisible` seam the layer calls). No pan gesture exists at `fit`. The zoom chords are three ctrl-tier `guiOnly` registry rows — Ctrl+= / Ctrl+- / Ctrl+0 (`gui-zoom-in` / `gui-zoom-out` / `gui-zoom-fit`, labels `Zoom GUI in/out/to fit`; [keyboard-and-palette](/run-kit/ui/keyboard-and-palette.md) § Keyboard Shortcuts) — whose handlers mount only while the gui tile owns focus and whose reclaim rides the capture-phase chord gate below; the palette mirrors them with destination-only rows. Separately, a capture-phase non-passive `wheel` listener on the wrapper steps the ladder when Ctrl is held, feeding `deltaY` through the shared `createWheelAccumulator` (`lib/zoom-gesture.ts`, `WHEEL_STEP_THRESHOLD = 50` — a mac trackpad pinch arrives as ctrl+wheel, and sub-threshold deltas accumulate into single steps rather than stepping once per event) with `preventDefault` + `stopPropagation` so neither the browser's page zoom nor noVNC's `_handleWheel` sees it; a wheel without Ctrl passes to noVNC untouched. (0aur)

**The trackpad pointer mode** (`components/gui-pointer.ts`): the `rk-gui-pointer` posture is `touch` | `trackpad`, defaulting to `trackpad` on a coarse pointer and `touch` on fine (a stored choice wins over the pointer-class default). `touch` is the verbatim noVNC passthrough — tap where you touch, native pinch sends Ctrl+wheel to the guest, native two-finger drag scrolls. While `pointerMode === "trackpad"` on a coarse pointer with a live RFB, `attachGuiPointer(wrapper, opts)` (returns a detach) OWNS every touch on the wrapper in the capture phase (`stopPropagation` + `preventDefault` — noVNC's `GestureHandler` and its `touchstart→focusCanvas` listener never see them; the layer calls `rfb.focus()` itself on the first touch) and re-emits synthetic DOM events on noVNC's canvas — `mousemove`/`mousedown`/`mouseup` `MouseEvent`s (with `buttons` and `clientX/clientY` at the virtual cursor's screen position, `bubbles: true`) and px `WheelEvent`s (`deltaMode: 0`, accumulated by noVNC into 50 px wheel steps at the cursor) — so no RFB private is ever called and the translated input is indistinguishable from a mouse (§ Design Decisions → Trackpad input reaches noVNC as synthetic DOM events). The gesture set, over named constants: one-finger drag = relative cursor move at `TRACKPAD_GAIN` 1.25; a tap (lift within `TAP_MAX_MS` 180 and under `TAP_MAX_PX` 10 of movement) = left click at the cursor, a tap over either threshold reclassifies as a drag start and never clicks; two-finger tap = right click; two-finger drag = wheel deltas 1:1 with the centroid, no momentum; a two-finger gesture classifies once it leaves the dead zone and stays locked — pinch when the inter-finger distance change exceeds the centroid movement (stepping the zoom ladder one rung per `PINCH_STEP_PX` 40 of distance change BEYOND the dead zone), scroll otherwise; long-press (`LONG_PRESS_MS` 500, movement under the dead zone) = a mousedown held until the finger lifts (drag-and-drop), and `touchcancel`/detach release any held button and clear timers. The virtual cursor seeds at the tile centre, clamps to the canvas, and is drawn by the rk-owned `gui-trackpad-cursor` indicator (absolutely positioned over the wrapper, `pointer-events: none`) because noVNC's local cursor is a CSS `cursor` invisible under touch; a relative move that would leave the visible window calls `ensureCursorVisible` (the cursor-follow pan). Touches targeted at the wrapper's own chrome — the key bar, the bare-WM strip, the credentials overlay (`CHROME_SELECTOR`) — pass through untouched: swallowing their touchstart would suppress the compatibility mouse events and leave the controls untappable, and a touch keeps its start target for its whole life, so a canvas drag passing over the bar stays owned while a bar tap never enters gesture state; the layer additionally detaches while the credentials prompt is up. `dragViewport` is forced false while the layer is attached (the two gesture systems would fight over the same touches) and follows the touch-mode pan rule only in `touch` mode; attach/detach rides mode flips, re-dials, disconnect, and unmount. (0aur)

**The key bar** (`components/gui-keybar.tsx`): on coarse pointers a `GuiKeyBar` strip (`data-testid="gui-keybar"`) docks UNDER the canvas as a flex sibling of the noVNC host div (the bare-WM strip is the above-precedent; the fit subtracts its height), hidden in the empty/credentials states, on fine pointers, and while the per-viewer `rk-gui-keybar` posture hides it (`"0"` = hidden, absent = shown — the `keyBarVisible` prop, toggled by the palette's `GUI: Hide/Show key bar` pair and the toolbar's `⚙` panel ⌨ row) (t2lv): `Esc Tab Ctrl Alt ⇧ ← ↑ ↓ → ⌨` as `Control variant="chip"` buttons. The modifiers latch through the pure `latchModifier` reducer (`off → armed → locked → off`): one tap ARMS the modifier for the next non-modifier key (rendered pressed), a second tap LOCKS it (rendered `Ctrl ●`, held across multiple keys), a third releases; sending a non-modifier key composes a chord via `composeChord` — the latched modifiers' `sendKey` downs (MODIFIERS order), the key's press+release, the modifiers' ups (reverse) — an armed modifier consumed back to `off`, a locked one persisting. `⌨` focuses a visually-hidden `<input>` (the noVNC-UI trick; `autocapitalize="off"`, `autocomplete="off"`, `spellcheck={false}`) to raise the platform's on-screen keyboard, forwarding printable characters via `beforeinput`/`input` (keysym = the Latin-1 code point, else `0x01000000 | codePoint`; the input's value resets after each forward) and Enter/Backspace/Tab/Escape/arrows via `keydown` (`preventDefault`), each composed with the latched modifiers; a second ⌨ tap or blur releases the input. Keys ride `rfb.sendKey` through a ref-bound callback — a no-op without a live RFB (noVNC's own `sendKey` no-ops unless connected and not viewOnly), and the keysyms come from `lib/gui-keysyms.ts`, the local X11 table (noVNC's package exports hide `KeyTable`/`keysyms`). (0aur)

**The header fold cluster** (`components/gui-toolbar.tsx`, `data-testid="gui-toolbar"`): the session toolbar lives INSIDE the tile header's `flex-1` spring — `SurfaceLayout`'s gui branch mirrors the tty branch, and the cluster's root (`flex-1 min-w-0`) owns the whole spring, which is the fold's measured budget. Nothing overlays the framebuffer; there is no hide timer and no reveal gesture. The cluster is a **measured priority fold**: the pure, dependency-free `lib/gui-toolbar-fold.ts` (`computeGuiToolbarFold` — the third instance of the measured-overflow idiom beside `lib/top-bar-overflow.ts` and `lib/crumb-collapse.ts`) owns the decision and the component owns the DOM measurement — ONE `ResizeObserver` plus a hidden probe row (`data-testid="gui-toolbar-probe"`) rendering every fit candidate's real width (BOTH label forms of the degradable items, one divider, and the pinned block), observing the spring, the probe, AND the pinned block, with a serialized `candidateKey` re-running the measure effect when the probed SET changes (the gui action list changes with connection state, lock, and pointer kind). Collapse-first: the fold state starts `null` and is set in a pre-paint `useLayoutEffect`; unmeasured environments (jsdom, zero-width probes) keep the previous state with the FULLY-EXPANDED form as the cold default, so unit tests exercise the full inventory. The priority ladder: **1 screen size · 2 zoom (`− fit +`) · 3 quality · 4 input (`⎘ ⌥`) · 5 launch (`▣ ◍`) · 6 health (`∿ ↻`)**, folding tail-first; the two degradable items (quality, then screen size — least-important first) spend their short labels (`◐ Balanced` → `◐`; `1920×1080 ▾` → `1920 ▾`) before ANY item folds. The pinned block at the cluster's tail holds the keyboard-capture verb as a PERMANENT member whenever its palette row exists (fine pointers only — touch input never reaches the chord gate the verb toggles), plus the `⚙` toggle conditionally (the two-pass reserve): the capture block's probed width (divider + verb) leaves the fit budget UP FRONT in both layout modes (subtracted from the right-aligned spring budget and from the centred symmetric budget alike, and counted into the centred mode's right block), so the verb can never fold or clip at any width; the `⚙` keeps its conditional reserve — fit once with NO `⚙` reserve (if every item fits, no `⚙` renders and nothing is reserved), otherwise re-fit with the `⚙` block's measured width (`pinnedWidth` — covering the conditional `⚙` alone) reserved before any ladder item, so that reserve never causes the fold that justifies it (§ Design Decisions → A permanent pinned member reserves up front; the `⚙` keeps the two-pass reserve). One-sided EXPAND-edge hysteresis (`GUI_TOOLBAR_FOLD_HYSTERESIS_PX = 24`, the module's own constant matching `CRUMB_COLLAPSE_HYSTERESIS_PX`) keeps a drag hovering the boundary from flapping. Grouping: FOUR groups (size ┆ zoom ┆ quality ┆ the six action glyphs) with exactly THREE hairline dividers at the shipped `<span aria-hidden="true" className="mx-0.5 h-3.5 w-px bg-border" />` spec, items FLUSH at `gap: 0`, no divider between the peer action glyphs; verb boxes stay 24×24 (26×26 coarse), so WCAG 2.2 SC 2.5.8 is untouched. Chrome is the header's vocabulary: borderless, `hover:bg-bg-inset`, secondary ink at rest → primary on hover, latched = green ink + inset ring (`controlClass({ variant: "toggle", ringed: true })`, the ⌕ find-toggle precedent), text chips borderless/content-width with 6px side padding; every glyph-only control renders a 14px `ControlGlyph` SVG from the `top-bar-icons.tsx` register (`PasteGlyph`, `SendKeyGlyph`, `TerminalGlyph`, `BrowserGlyph`, `StatsGlyph`, `ReconnectGlyph`, `GearGlyph`, `FullscreenGlyph`, `KeyboardGlyph`, and the zoom trio) — no Unicode glyphs — while the text chips (resolution, quality) stay text. Every control carries a `Tip` inside ONE `TipGroup` (the warm cluster — a sweep opens each tip at 0 ms); `Tip` REPLACES the native `title=` and adds no wrapper DOM node, so the width probe is unaffected; `aria-label` stays as the coarse-pointer path. The three zoom tips pull their keycaps from the LIVE `gui-zoom-in`/`-out`/`-fit` registry bindings (`useKeybindings()` → `byAction.get(id)` → `formatCombo` — the source `withShortcutHints` reads), so a remap updates the tooltip for free; the size chip carries the dim note `menu`, the quality chip `cycles`, and the `⚙` toggle the folded-item count. The capture verb (`data-testid="gui-capture-toggle"`, `aria-pressed` mirroring the latch, latching with the ringed-toggle arm) carries a `Tip` labelled `Keyboard capture` whose keycap reads the live `gui-capture-toggle` binding the same way (`kbdFor`), and its `onClick` IS the `gui-capture-toggle` palette row's `onSelect`; on coarse pointers the verb is omitted, never disabled. (31eg) The resolution chip (`data-testid="gui-toolbar-resolution"`, `aria-haspopup="menu"`, present iff at least one `gui-res-*` row exists) labels itself from the stream entry: `auto ▾` when `geometry === "auto"`, else `W×H ▾` from the entry's `width`×`height` (falling back to `presetLabel(geometry)` with no `(portrait)` suffix when either is 0), prefixed `🔒 ` while `locked`, degrading to `1920 ▾` while the `aria-label` keeps the full form. Presence follows the palette's omit-not-disable rule EXCEPT the three zoom chips and the quality chip, which always render and disable when the destination row is absent, so the fold never reflows on a zoom or preset change; the coarse posture pair (`⌖`/`⌨`) lives in the `⚙` panel only. `gui-turn-off`, `gui-desktop`, `gui-logs`, `gui-hidpi-*`, and `gui-view-1to1` never appear in the cluster or its menus. Both menu chips open the shared **`GuiToolbarMenu`** (`components/gui-toolbar-menu.tsx`, `data-testid="gui-toolbar-menu"` with `data-menu="resolution"|"overflow"` — one test id, two menus): an `absolute`, `role="menu"` one-column monospace menu rendered inside the anchor's positioned ancestor, anchored under its chip with a `MENU_GAP_PX` (4) gap and horizontally clamped to that ancestor's span, using `POPOVER_SHELL` and `Control variant="menu-row"` (`role="menuitem"`) with the palette's idioms (`label — description`, disabled rows dimmed and inert, a `current` description rendered as the trailing ✓ `MENU_ROW_CHECK_MARK`); a row may also be a SEPARATOR (an `aria-hidden` hairline with no `menuitem` role — the fold panel's group seams). Focus moves to the first enabled row on open; `↑`/`↓` rove with wraparound, `Enter`/`Space` select, `Escape`/`Tab` close and return focus to the anchor chip, the menu's own `onKeyDown` stops propagation so no key reaches the canvas's chord gate, and a document-level capture `pointerdown` outside both the menu and its anchor chip closes it. The `⚙` panel lists the folded rungs as FLAT by-id palette rows in ladder order with group separators — a folded screen-size rung inlines the resolution menu rows, a folded quality rung contributes the cycle row labelled `Quality → <current preset>` — never nested submenus; on coarse pointers it appends the `⌖`/`⌨` rows after a separator. The panel's open state is the per-viewer `rk-gui-toolbar` posture (absent/invalid = closed), owned by app.tsx (`guiToolbarVisible` + `handleGuiToolbarVisibleChange`, seeded from `readGuiToolbarVisible` with write-through), threaded through `SurfaceLayout`'s `guiToolbarVisible`/`onGuiToolbarVisibleChange` props, and mirrored by the palette's destination-only `GUI: Show toolbar` / `GUI: Hide toolbar` pair. A persisted-open panel whose items have all unfolded renders nothing (no `⚙`, no anchor) without rewriting the posture — the state goes latent, not false. Every chip, verb, and menu row fires the `onSelect` of the palette row picked by stable id via `pickGuiActions` — the same memoized `buildGuiActions` output app.tsx feeds the palette, threaded through `SurfaceLayout` as `guiActions`; the resolution menu's rows are the palette's Resolution and Lock rows in `RESOLUTION_MENU_IDS` order, labels stripped of the `GUI: Resolution → ` / `GUI: ` prefixes via `stripGuiLabel`, descriptions verbatim (`current`, `locked` while the host pin is set) (§ Design Decisions → The header fold cluster is presentational; app.tsx owns every action). **Fullscreen targets the TILE**: app.tsx's `guiFullscreen` resolves the canvas/empty node then `.closest('[data-testid^="surface-tile-gui"]')` (the duplicate-suffix form still matches), so the header travels into fullscreen and serves it like every other case; `GuiSurface` keeps its `fullscreenchange` listener as a CONTAINMENT check (`document.fullscreenElement?.contains(root)` against a root ref attached to both the canvas and the empty-state roots) and reports flips up through the `onFullscreenChange` prop, and `SurfaceLayout`'s `guiTileFullscreen` latches the header's `⤢` rail verb green (ringed toggle, firing the `gui-fullscreen` row by id, rendered at ANY tile count — the tty find-button precedent, separated from the fold cluster by one hairline) and suppresses the gui tile's layout verbs (`⛶ ↰ ⇄ ✕`) while it lasts — the zoomed-tile precedent. The fit math and the `keyboardLock()` chaining are untouched. **Mobile is the bottom rung of the same ladder**: with no tile header, `GuiToolbarMobileOverflow` renders the pinned `⚙` block (`data-testid="gui-toolbar-overflow"`, `aria-label="More controls"`) into the TOP BAR beside the pinned mobile switch group, gated on switch mode with `active === "gui"` ([top-bar](/run-kit/ui/top-bar.md) § Surface toggles); its panel is the same `GuiToolbarMenu` carrying EVERY ladder rung's rows plus the coarse `⌖`/`⌨` rows and the `gui-fullscreen` row (grouped with screen size — desktop never folds `⤢`, a header rail verb). The toolbar lives outside the canvas wrapper, so it needs no entry in the trackpad layer's `CHROME_SELECTOR`. (t2lv) (abna) (lut4)

**The resize palette rows' mechanics** — the `GUI: Resolution → …` rows ([keyboard-and-palette](/run-kit/ui/keyboard-and-palette.md) § The `GUI:` palette family) all post `POST /api/gui/host/resize` through the client's `resizeGui(geometry)`; no toast on success (the desktop visibly reflows), a thrown non-2xx toasts the server's message. `Match this tile` (tile-open only) measures the noVNC host div inside the gui tile's canvas wrapper (`[data-testid="gui-surface-canvas"]`'s `:scope > div.flex-1` child, falling back to the wrapper) and posts `closestAspectPreset(width, height)` — a one-shot resize to the preset whose aspect ratio is nearest the tile's, not a continuous follow (V-D5). The pure, DOM-free `src/lib/gui-geometry.ts` (colocated tests, the `web-url.ts` module contract) owns the preset list `GUI_GEOMETRY_PRESETS` (`1280x720, 1600x900, 1920x1080, 2560x1440, 1080x1920` — 16:9 covers laptops and monitors; the portrait preset is the phone-first session), `presetLabel` (the display `×` form — `1280×720`, `1080×1920 (portrait)`), `parseGeometryInput` (accepts `1440x900`, `1440×900`, `1440 900` — `x`, `×`, or whitespace as the separator — and enforces 320–7680 per side client-side, the single error copy `Width×Height, 320–7680 per side`; the backend's `ParseGeometry` stays authoritative), and `closestAspectPreset` (exact aspect-distance comparison by cross-multiplication; ties prefer a preset that covers the tile in both dimensions, then the smaller area among covers / the larger area when neither covers). `Custom…` opens the **`GuiGeometryPrompt`** (`components/gui-geometry-prompt.tsx`, lazy-loaded like `SessionNamePrompt`, the same shape): a `Dialog` titled `Desktop size` with one input (`aria-label="Width×Height"`, placeholder `1440x900`), live `parseGeometryInput` validation showing the inline red error once there is input, Enter or the `Resize` button submitting the normalized `WxH` (the button disabled while invalid), Escape/backdrop closing. (zuci)

**Visibility gates the connection, focus never does**: when the tile is not visible (zoomed away, or `document.visibilityState === "hidden"`) for 15 s, the component disconnects — noVNC has no pause API, and disconnecting stops framebuffer traffic and releases the relay's `viewers` count so the backend probe resumes — and reconnects on the next visible true. Focus loss alone never disconnects. Unmount disconnects and clears every timer. The gui tile preserves hide-never-unmount: `visible` is a prop, not a mount gate (§ Surface Layout → Hide-never-unmount).

**Chord gate — same-document capture, no steal guard**: the canvas wrapper's capture-phase `keydown` intercepts events matching the injected `shouldReclaimChord` predicate (`hasReclaimableMatch(e, bindings, "gui")` bound in `app.tsx`) BEFORE noVNC's canvas-attached handler sees them, then re-dispatches a synthetic bubbling `KeyboardEvent` on the parent document — rk's dispatcher listens at window in the bubble phase, so a plain `stopPropagation` would eat the chord with it. ⌘K, ⌘1–⌘4, ⌘;, the zen chord, and the `guiOnly` ctrl-tier zoom chords (Ctrl+=/−/0 — reclaimable for kind `"gui"`, § Zoom above) stay rk's; every other key reaches the guest. **Capture mode** (31eg): while the per-viewer `rk-gui-capture` latch is set, the predicate is called with its fourth `captured` argument — app.tsx's `reclaimChordForKind` passes `kind === "gui" && guiCapture`, so the code/web iframe reclaim paths never see the flag — and narrows to the single `gui-capture-toggle` actionId: every other chord (34 registry entries survive the released gate; the five `ttyOnly`/`webOnly` bindings were never reclaimed here) returns `false` and falls through to noVNC and onto the wire, ⌘K and the zoom trio included. The release binding (`gui-capture-toggle`, ⇧⌘G / Ctrl+Shift+G — [keyboard-and-palette](/run-kit/ui/keyboard-and-palette.md) § The default binding set) is the one chord still reclaimed, and the latch also releases through the pinned header verb and the pointer-reachable palette row (`GUI: Release keyboard`); while captured there is no keyboard way to switch tiles. The mouse is NEVER captured — the gate is a keydown interceptor only, the wrapper's pointer handling untouched — so the icon click always works. ⌘W/⌘T/⌘N/⌘Q cannot reach the guest from a browser tab regardless of the latch (the browser takes them above the page); gui fullscreen's `keyboard.lock()` delivers them. Pointerdown/keydown fire `onInteract` (the tile-focus seam — canvas interaction is genuine focus and records `gui`, [focus-ownership](/run-kit/ui/focus-ownership.md)). No steal guard is wired: noVNC grabs focus only on click (`focusOnClick`), never programmatically, so there is no load-time grab to revert — unlike the code tile (§ Code Surface → Load-time focus grab — guarded).

**Clipboard**: RFB `clipboard` events write to `navigator.clipboard` best-effort (silently skipped without permission); paste is the palette's `GUI: Paste clipboard` (a user gesture — `readText` needs one) driving the `commandsRef` imperative seam's `clipboardPasteFrom`.

**Send key (`GUI: Send key…`)**: the palette row (tile open AND connected — the paste gate) opens the lazy **`GuiSendKeyPrompt`** (`components/gui-send-key-prompt.tsx`, the `GuiGeometryPrompt` shape — a `Dialog` titled `Send key` with one autofocused, live-validated input (placeholder `Ctrl+Alt+F4`), Enter or the `Send` button submitting, an inline error plus a disabled `Send` on an unparseable non-empty value, Escape/backdrop closing) plus a quick-pick row of the five `SUGGESTED_CHORDS` — `Ctrl+Alt+Del`, `Ctrl+Alt+T`, `Alt+F4`, `Super`, `Print` — as `Control` chips that submit immediately. Chord parsing and sending live in the pure `lib/gui-send-key.ts`: `parseKeyChord` accepts `+`-joined, case-insensitive tokens — modifiers (`Ctrl`/`Control`, `Alt`, `Shift`, `Super`/`Win`/`Meta`) then a final key (a named key — `Del`/`Delete`, `F1`–`F12`, `Print`/`PrtSc`, `Esc`/`Escape`, `Tab`, `Enter`/`Return`, `Space`, `Backspace`, the arrows — a single printable character via `keysymForChar`, or a modifier: a modifiers-only chord like `Super` presses and releases the modifier); an empty input, a trailing or doubled `+`, or an unknown token returns null. `sendKeyChord` reuses the key bar's `composeChord` ordering — modifier downs in order, the key's press+release (`sendKey(keysym, code)` with `down` undefined), modifier ups in reverse — over the keysyms in `lib/gui-keysyms.ts` (`KEYSYM_SUPER_L`, `KEYSYM_DELETE`, `KEYSYM_PRINT`, `KEYSYM_SPACE`, `KEYSYM_F1`–`KEYSYM_F12`). The send is viewer-side through noVNC's `sendKey` — no server round trip; app.tsx's submit handler first checks the host signal's `backend`: on the `screen-sharing` mirror it toasts the frontend constant `GUI_SEND_KEY_MIRROR_REFUSAL` (`gui send key is not supported on macOS in v1 — the GUI mirrors your live session view-only` — the backend's `gui <verb> is not supported…` template with verb `send key`) as an error and sends nothing (§ Design Decisions → Send key refuses on the mirror in the frontend, with the backend's wording, in [gui](/run-kit/gui.md)); every other backend runs `sendKeyChord` over the `commandsRef` seam's `sendKey`, a no-op without a live RFB. (t2lv)

**macOS (`backend === "screen-sharing"`)**: the tile additionally sets `viewOnly = true` (the relay already drops input — the flag stops the pretend cursor). On `credentialsrequired` (the ARD type-30 negotiation) an inline password field overlays the canvas (`data-testid="gui-surface-credentials"`: label `Screen Sharing password`, a `type="password"` input, Enter or **Connect** ⇒ `rfb.sendCredentials({ password })`); the password lives ONLY in component state — never localStorage/sessionStorage, never a POST — and is re-asked on every reconnect. A `securityfailure` after submit re-shows the field with `Wrong password — try again`; Escape disconnects into the empty state with reason `Screen Sharing password required`.

**The palette's imperative seams** — `GuiSurfaceCommands` (`paste`, `reconnect`, `sendKey(keysym, code, down?)` — each a no-op without a live RFB), filled into the caller's `commandsRef` while an RFB is live — back the `GUI: Paste clipboard` / `GUI: Reconnect` / `GUI: Send key…` rows (t2lv) ([keyboard-and-palette](/run-kit/ui/keyboard-and-palette.md) § The `GUI:` palette family). The fullscreen verb (`GUI: Fullscreen` — element fullscreen + `navigator.keyboard.lock`, zen fallback where `requestFullscreen` is absent) lives in `app.tsx` and is documented there too.

**e2e — `gui-surface.spec.ts`**: two halves. (a) **Ungated**, over the `_state-socket-mock.ts` `gui` slot (delivered on hello, flipped mid-test via the exported `emitGui`): switch off ⇒ no 4th button, no Tiles row, ⌘4 inert, and a `split-h:tty,gui` window (a legacy value, still parsed) renders only the tty tile; flipping the slot to `enabled:true, reachable:false` ⇒ the button appears without reload and the tile opens to `gui-surface-empty` — at 375 px and 1280 px. The bare-WM strip cases drive the slot between a bare fixture (`GUI_ON_BARE`, reachable, `wm: ""`) and a WM-stamped one (`GUI_ON_ICEWM`, `wm: "icewm-session"`), with `mockGuiBackend` taking the `GET /api/gui/host` document as a parameter (the bare one carries `wm_hint`): desktop — the strip shows the exact copy with the install line, Copy (context-granted `clipboard-read`/`clipboard-write`) puts exactly the install line on the clipboard (read back via `navigator.clipboard.readText()`), and a WM-stamped flip removes the strip; desktop — × hides the strip, `page.reload()` keeps it hidden (the localStorage dismissal survives), and an icewm-then-bare flip pair brings it back (the non-empty `wm` cleared the dismissal); mobile 375 px — the strip renders (wrapping allowed) with both buttons visible inside the viewport and × dismisses; desktop palette — the `GUI: Open terminal` / `GUI: Open browser` rows follow reachability (absent on the unreachable fixture), and selecting `GUI: Open browser` against a stubbed `POST /api/gui/host/launch` answering the `ok:false` ladder-miss document sends body `{"app":"browser"}` and toasts the hint verbatim. The geometry cases drive the slot between the fixed-geometry fixture (`GUI_ON_FIXED`, `geometry: "1600x900"` — every reachable fixture carries the setting, `GUI_ON_BARE`/`GUI_ON_ICEWM` reading `1920x1080`) and `GUI_ON_AUTO` (zuci): with `GUI_ON_FIXED` and the gui tile open, the palette's `GUI: Lock resolution` renders aria-disabled with the exact text `GUI: Lock resolution — resolution is fixed (1600×900) — pick Auto to follow the tile`, and an `emitGui` flip to `auto` re-enables the row while the destination-only `GUI: Resolution → Auto (follow this tile)` row drops out of the list; and selecting `GUI: Resolution → 1280×720` against a stubbed `POST /api/gui/host/resize` sends exactly `{"geometry":"1280x720"}` (the `×` glyph is display-only — the wire value is the lowercase-x form). The stats/quality cases (5psw) drive the 1080p fixture (`GUI_ON_1080P`) on @2 — its persisted `split-h:tty,gui` layout keeps the tile open across the reload — and `mockGuiBackend` additionally stubs `POST /api/windows/*/select` (the mount-time tmux alignment POSTs select when the URL window is not the payload's active one; unstubbed, the failure bounces the route back to @1): `GUI: Show stats` mounts `gui-stats-overlay` reading the dash placeholders plus `1920×1080 · fit` (the mocked `/ws/gui/` socket never handshakes, so the collector never connects and nothing samples), the overlay survives a `page.reload()` (the `rk-gui-stats-visible` posture), and `GUI: Hide stats` removes it and unsets the key; and the three `GUI: Quality →` rows render with their fixed descriptions and the ` · current` marker on Balanced, selecting `GUI: Quality → Smooth` writing `rk-gui-quality=smooth`. A resize case POSTs `/api/gui/host/resize {"geometry":"1280x720"}` through `page.request`, asserts the status document's `width`/`height` follow within 5 s and the open tile's fit-mode canvas letterboxes to the desktop's 16:9 aspect (contained in the noVNC host div's box, strictly narrower or shorter), then resizes back to `1920x1080`; a stats case (5psw) shows the overlay on the live rig, asserts its size segment against the status document's `width×height` and polls the RTT segment for `\d+ ms` (the 5 s ping probe), then launches the terminal role through `page.request.post("/api/gui/host/launch", { data: { app: "terminal" } })` (a window opening paints frames) and polls the overlay for a sample with fps ≥ 1 and Mbit/s > 0 within 10 s; the rig `afterAll` POSTs `{"gui.geometry": null}` before the settings restore so the run's geometry writes never outlive it. ## Window Views (Lens Model)

The model (`docs/specs/window-views.md`, rules R1–R7; arrangement generalized by `docs/specs/surface-layout.md`): **a window ROW is a substrate** (a supervised tmux pane); **a VIEW is a lens** over one derivable output of that pane (the tty is a lens; an iframe of the pane's HTTP service is a lens; later: `gui`, the host desktop — `docs/specs/gui.md`). **Which lenses a window offers is a derived capability set; which surfaces the tab shows is shared tab state** — the surface layout (§ Surface Layout) carried in the `@rk_win_layout` window option, never part of the window's identity; only a viewer's zoom/single-tile choice is per-viewer client state. A single-tile layout IS the exclusive-lens view: the bare `web` leaf is "the web lens", the bare `tty` leaf the default terminal. The frontend is a renderer of the payload — the `@rk_win_layout`/`@rk_win_web_<n>`/`@rk_win_code_root` options and `POST /options` carry the layout and content addresses; the SSE payload carries `layout`/`webTabs`/`webActive`/`codeRoot`/`agentProvider`. (260714-t97o, 260714-r7rq, iip5, 260904-bf1l-agent-session-identity-rename)

Four lenses are implemented: `tty` (always available, `TerminalClient`), `web` (ALWAYS available, like `tty` — `hasWebUrl` selects its CONTENT: the onboarding state when the web-tab family is empty, the live iframe when the active tab carries an address; `IframeWindow`, § Web Tile → Onboarding state), `code` (available when `codeRootFor(win)` is non-empty — the shared `@rk_win_code_root` or a derived `gitRoot`; the stable capability signal; `CodeSurface`, § Code Surface), and `gui` (available exactly when the host-global `event: gui` signal's `enabled` is true — a per-HOST capability threaded into the helpers as an optional arg; `GuiSurface`, § GUI Surface). Capabilities are orthogonal and STACK (spec R5): one window can offer `tty`+`web`+`code`+`gui` at once, and the layout can tile up to three of them at once (§ Surface Layout). The lens set IS the layout's tileable surface set (`SurfaceKind = ViewName`). There is no default-view hint: a web-first window carries an explicit `web` `@rk_win_layout` value (stamped at creation by the `rkType:"iframe"` arm, or by the retired-`@rk_win_lens` migration/dual-read). (iip5)

### Pure view-model helper — `lib/window-view.ts`

The single source of lens CAPABILITY logic — pure, DOM-free, colocated `window-view.test.ts` (the same pattern as `window-transition.ts`/`navigation.ts`). `surface-layout.ts` builds its availability set on these predicates, so the toggles, the layout, and the palette share one capability source (no drift). Helpers take a minimal structural `{ layout?, webTabs?, webActive?, codeRoot?, gitRoot? }` shape (`ViewWindow`, assignable from `WindowInfo`):

- **`ViewName = "tty" | "web" | "code" | "gui"`** — the lens union; aliased as the layout's `SurfaceKind`.
- **`GuiHost` / `hasGui(host)`** — the host-global gui signal the availability helpers consult (`{ enabled: boolean } | null | undefined`): absent/null reads as NOT available (never on by default — D3). Only `enabled` gates; `reachable`/`backend`/`viewers` select content, never presence.
- **`hasWebUrl(win)` / `hasCode(win)`** — web's CONTENT selector plus the code capability predicate: `hasWebUrl` is `(win?.webTabs?.length ?? 0) > 0` and gates NOTHING about availability — web is always available (below); it selects the web tile's content (onboarding vs live iframe — an empty web-tab family renders onboarding, never a blank-src iframe) and drives the web toggle's corner dot (§ Surface Toggles); for `code`, `(win?.codeRoot || win?.gitRoot)` non-empty — the shared `@rk_win_code_root` OR the live derivation, so a window whose code root is seeded keeps offering `code` after its active pane leaves the repo (the port is always resolvable by convention, so there is no configured-port leg); code-server REACHABILITY is deliberately NOT in this gate (it fluctuates and would strobe the toggles/palette; it selects the surface's content instead, § Code Surface).
- **`activeWebUrl(win): string`** — `webTabs[(webActive >= 1 ? webActive : 1) - 1] ?? ""`: the address the single web tile shows as its ACTIVE tab (empty family or out-of-range pointer → slot 1 / `""`). The tile header's display form reads it; `IframeWindow` derives the same value internally from its `tabs`/`active` props. (iip5, 9kip)
- **`availableViews(win, host?): ViewName[]`** (spec R1/R3) — `["tty"]`, plus `"web"` UNCONDITIONALLY (always available; `hasWebUrl` selects content, never availability), plus `"code"` when `hasCode`, plus `"gui"` when `hasGui(host)`. **Availability is decoupled from `@rk_win_lens`**: an iframe-created window with no URL offers `tty` + `web` (the web tile renders its onboarding state). Returned in the registry's fixed order via a `HINT_ORDER` (`[code, gui, web, tty]`) filter — so a fully-stacked window yields `["code","gui","web","tty"]` (`HINT_ORDER` is purely the capability ordering; there is no default-view hint). The layout module's `availableTiles` re-derives the same set tty-first (§ Surface Layout).
- Input gating for the lens chord is **not** a `window-view.ts` concern: the shared `shouldSuppressChord` predicate in `lib/keybindings.ts` gates every app chord (§ Keyboard Shortcuts → Dispatch seams).
- **localStorage helpers** — **value-bearing** key `runkit-window-view:{server}:{windowId}` storing the view NAME (absent = default). `windowViewStorageKey`/`readStoredView` (READ-only — nothing writes this key), try/catch-noop per `chrome-context.tsx`. The key is a LEGACY input to the one-shot route-entry translation (§ Surface Layout → `app.tsx` integration) and survives only for that one consumer; the follow-up cleanup change removes it. (260814-6b0j, iip5)

### Legacy layout search params — `lib/router-url.ts`

The terminal route is the BARE route: nothing in the frontend writes search params anymore, and every history entry is `/{server}/{N}` alone. The legacy `layout`/`view`/`panel` params remain ACCEPTED inbound for one release so old deep links never break — they are translation-only inputs to the route-entry one-shot effect (§ Surface Layout → `app.tsx` integration), which folds them into `@rk_win_layout` (rendered at once through the `pendingLayout` optimistic overlay — a carried deep link never waits for the option tick; a rejected write reverts) and replaces the URL with the bare route. The exported pure `validateTerminalSearch(search): { view?: "web" | "code"; panel?: "web" | "code"; layout?: string }` passes `view` ∈ {`web`, `code`} and `panel` ∈ {`web`, `code`} through, forwards any non-empty string `layout` (tree validation happens in `parseLayoutTree`, § Surface Layout), and **drops any other/unknown/non-string value** (treated as absent, NEVER errored — `chat` is a dropped legacy value, never translated: a legacy `?view=chat` deep link degrades this way, landing on the window's stored/default layout). It is wired as the route's `validateSearch` and unit-tested directly in `router-url.test.ts`. The `declare module "@tanstack/react-router"` `Register` block types the params, so `useSearch({ strict: false })` in `app.tsx` reads them **with no `as` cast** (code-quality type-narrowing — `tsc --noEmit` clean). `translateLegacyParams(view, panel)` maps `?view=X` → `single:X` and `?view=X&panel=Y` → `split-h:X,Y` (X in slot A) as the pure param→layout-string mapper the one-shot effect consumes. (iip5)

### `app.tsx` integration — derivation, `switchView`, the layout render

`app.tsx` (terminal route) owns ONE layout-state block (§ Surface Layout → `app.tsx` integration for the full derivation/optimistic/translation machinery): `layout = effectiveLayout(effectiveWindow)` — the payload's `@rk_win_layout` parsed and degraded — overlaid by the optimistic `pendingLayout` while a verb's POST is in flight. The lens model's consumers key off **slot A** — `resolvedView: ViewName = slotOrder(layout)[0]` (spec R12's shim: a multi-tile layout reads as its slot-A surface — the template's main tile, the first leaf in reading order for a custom tree — to the palette `View:` actions). One stable `switchView(view)` callback collapses to a single-tile layout: `applyLayout({ leaf: view })` — `applyLayout` POSTs `@rk_win_layout` through the unified `/options` seam, so a lens pick is a shared tab-state mutation every viewer of the window renders (never an `@rk_win_lens` mutation, spec R7). The **render** is `<SurfaceLayout layout={layout} …/>` keyed by `server` — a same-server window switch re-renders the mounted grid with a new `windowId` prop (per-window transient state resets by effect inside the component, § Surface Layout → Tile renderer); only a server change remounts it — one tile per surface in the rendered order (§ Surface Layout → tile renderer). There is no `@rk_win_lens`-mutating "switch" path anywhere: switching lenses/surfaces is the surface toggles' and the palette's job, neither of which mutates `@rk_win_lens` (spec R7).

### Lens-switch surfaces — palette, top-bar toggles

There is no lens-switcher component: the lens-switch surfaces are the command palette's `View: …` actions (§ Palette parity below — DESKTOP-only; on mobile the top-bar switch group and its `Tile: Switch to <Surface>` palette twin take their place, § Surface Layout → Mobile slot-A + the top-bar switch group) and the top bar's open-tile toggles (§ Surface Toggles — switch mode on mobile). The top-bar chevron menu carries no lens rows — its VIEW section holds the ▦ layout chip's shape rows plus the `menuOnly` Fixed width and Terminal font rows (§ Chrome (Top Bar) → Right cluster). (260812-0c6o, 260816-ox16)

### Palette parity + keyboard shortcuts (Constitution V)

- **`View: Terminal` / `View: Web` / `View: Code` / `View: GUI` palette actions** (AppShell `viewActions` in `app.tsx` — DESKTOP-only; on mobile the `Tile: Switch to <Surface>` entries take their place, § Surface Layout → Mobile slot-A + the top-bar switch group), each visible only when its view is **available AND not the current** (slot-A) view — the `View: GUI` row falls out of the registry's `VIEW_ACTION_LABEL` map with no gui-specific code — each applies `single:<view>` through `applyLayout` via `switchView` (§ app.tsx integration). The gating + label composition live in a pure builder `buildViewActions(available, resolved, onSwitch)` in `lib/palette/view.ts` (mirrors `lib/palette/move.ts`/`palette/update.ts`, unit-tested in `palette/view.test.ts`). Every entry's `shortcut` is `""` — no chord reaches a single-tile lens switch, so no hint is advertised (§ Design Decisions → No chord reaches a lens switch). One `buildViewActions` call feeds them; there is no `@rk_win_lens`-mutating `toggle-iframe-terminal` action. The multi-tile affordances (add/close/promote/swap/zoom/shape) carry their own `Layout:` palette entries (§ Surface Layout → palette + chords).

### Heading is a static `Tab:` in every lens

The top-bar center `PageType: name` heading on the terminal route reads a **static `Tab: <name>` in every lens** (tty / web / code) — it does NOT follow the active view (§ Window Heading). A single `WINDOW_PREFIX = "Tab:"` constant (`top-bar.tsx`) is passed as a constant `prefix` into `WindowHeading` at the terminal-mode center cell. The heading identifies the WINDOW (the substrate); which lens you look through is carried by the view content itself (a live iframe vs. terminal) and the top bar's lit tile toggles, not the heading (§ Design Decisions → Heading identifies the window, not the lens). The name's anchor does not jump on a lens switch, since the prefix width does not change with the lens (§ Center-heading stable anchor + nav affordances). The click-to-rename affordance and boot-sweep treatment apply. (260714-uco1, 260717-6anu, 260820-lfla)

### Internal navigation targets the bare route (spec R6 / surface-layout L4)

Navigating to a DIFFERENT window carries NO layout/view/panel param — each destination window renders its own shared `@rk_win_layout` (§ Surface Layout → `app.tsx` integration). Client-side navigations (sidebar/palette/breadcrumb via `navigateToWindow`, the waiting-badge click and `Agent: Next waiting` deep links, plus the window-death redirect and SSE-writeback switch) omit/clear `search`, and TanStack Router's `final` search middleware returns `{}` when `dest.search` is absent, so a carried legacy param does not leak onto the target. (The web-view-lens e2e persistence case exercises this through a real sidebar-click navigation to guard against a future `retainSearchParams`/router-upgrade regression.) History entries are bare routes — layout mutations never create or rewrite entries, so back/forward always lands on `/{server}/{N}` and each window renders its current shared layout.

### Window-switch transition classification (one rule)

The window-switch slide transition's `switchTransitionRef` carries a SINGLE `ungatedIds` set: window ids whose shared layout is **not tty-led** — i.e. `effectiveLayout(fw.window).order[0] !== "tty"` — slot A is a non-`tty` surface, which renders `<IframeWindow>` (web), `<CodeSurface>` (code), or `<GuiSurface>` (gui), none of which has the terminal's xterm first-write receipt seam, so they use the transition's **ungated capture** (a first-write gate would never release for a non-xterm surface → stuck transition / blank pane). Computed at render time from the payload directly — no localStorage reads, no legacy-key translation — the same "keyed on the shared layout, not `@rk_win_lens`" invariant as the render path (§ Window-Switch Slide Transition). (iip5)

### e2e — `web-view-lens.spec.ts`
