The suite reaches non-tty lenses through the command palette's `View: …` actions on desktop (a shared `switchLensViaPalette` helper — on desktop the palette is the only lens-switch surface; on MOBILE the top-bar switch group and its `Tile: Switch to <Surface>` palette twin stand in for the `View:` entries) or inbound `?view=` deep links, and asserts the `view-toggle` testid appears NOWHERE in the DOM at any width and the chevron menu carries no `View:` rows. It drives real tmux (own session, `window-heading.spec.ts` pattern), stamping web-tab options (`@rk_win_web_1`, or the retired `@rk_win_lens` for the dual-read case) via `tmux set-option -w` and reading shared state back through the `_tmux.ts` `windowOption` helper — never the URL or localStorage. Its `describe` defaults to a wide **1440px** desktop width used to clear the whole cluster — chosen so the palette-only case is provable with the full cluster in-bar. Covers: the palette's `View: Web` action present on every window (web is always available — no `view-toggle` anywhere, no `View:` menu rows); ⌘3 on a URL-less window opens the ONBOARDING tile, typing `localhost:{port}` in its live address bar boots the iframe (the write lands on the active web slot via `onWriteUrl`), and an agent-side `set-option` on the web slot flips the tile live with no user action; a `View: Web`/`View: Terminal` flip is a SHARED layout write — one `POST /options` on `@rk_win_layout` per pick, the URL stays the bare route, and no `rk-layout:` key is written; the deep link `?view=web` cold-loads through the one-shot translation (one `@rk_win_layout=web` write, the URL replaced with the bare route — the heading stays the static `Tab:` prefix); `?view=web` on a URL-less window keeps its web tile (onboarding — never degrades to the terminal); the retired `@rk_win_lens=iframe` dual-reads server-side as the bare `web` leaf; per-window SHARED persistence — a lens flip lands in `@rk_win_layout` and a real client-side window switch away and back restores the arrangement. The 375px test proves the mobile switch surface at phone width — the switch group + `Tile: Switch to <Surface>` palette entries in place of `View:` (a one-tap tty→web persists the shared layout through the switch verb's grow arm; a `Tile: Switch to Terminal` palette pick likewise) with the retained single-line/no-horizontal-overflow contract holding WITH the group present (long window name → heading room) — AND re-asserts no `view-toggle` and the desktop palette's `View:`-only/no-`Tile: Switch` shape at desktop width. `connection-budget.spec.ts` uses the any-route ≤2 WS + 0 SSE form (architecture/testing § Testing). (260714-uco1, 260717-6anu, 260722-n2n4, 260816-ox16, 260821-zqlq, iip5)

## Surface Layout (the center is a layout of tiles)

The terminal route's center is a **layout manager** (`docs/specs/surface-layout.md`): a resolved **layout** — a canonical split tree over surface leaves — renders as one or more **tiles** (growth offers bounded by the per-viewport 150×100 px size floor, never a tile count), each mounting an existing surface renderer unchanged. `docs/specs/right-panel.md`'s P6 slot mechanics do not apply to the layout manager; its availability derivation and P4 attention do (§ Surface Toggles). A leaf is a **bare surface kind** of the ROUTE window or a **foreign address** `@N/<kind>` — another tab's surface on the same server (§ Surface Layout → Tiles from other tabs; foreign `gui` is never permitted); the tileable kind set is `tty | web | code | gui` (`SurfaceKind = ViewName` — § Window Views (Lens Model)); `tty` is `(current window, tty)`, always available, listed first. (260812-ab5v) (260925-jbz0-surface-cross-tab-tiles) Tiles are content-area state; the toggles that open and close them live in the top bar (§ Surface Toggles).

### The model — a canonical split tree, templates, per-viewer sizes

- **Tree** — the layout stored in `@rk_win_layout` is a canonical split tree: `node = leaf | split`, `leaf = <kind> | @<digits>/<kind>[/<n>]`, `split = dir(node, node [, node…])` with `dir ∈ {h, v}` (`h` lays children left→right, `v` top→bottom), no whitespace. Canonical form: ≥2 children per split, a child split never has its parent's direction (depth alternates), no repeated bare non-tty kind (duplicate bare `tty` leaves are legal — the muxed `/ws/terminals` relay supports N clients per pane; only the FIRST BARE tty tile holds the shared `wsRef`/`focusRef`), no foreign `gui` (one desktop per host), no repeated foreign address, no `/<n>` suffix on any leaf (grammar-only — the tokenizer accepts it so the grammar stays forward-compatible), and — enforced on write paths with the owner known — no foreign leaf naming the layout's own window. A bare kind and a foreign leaf of the same kind coexist (`h(web,@12/web)`). There is no leaf-count cap: `MAX_LAYOUT_LEN = 512` bounds the input bytes BEFORE parsing (the recursion-depth guard), and the per-viewport size floor gates offers (§ Tiles from other tabs, § addSurface). Every writer emits the tree form (`h(tty,v(code,web))`); the legacy `<shape>:<a>,<b>[,<c>]` preset strings (`single`/`split-h`/`split-v`/`row`/`col`/`main-left`/`main-right`/`main-top` — bare kinds only) parse into their trees permanently and losslessly (`main-left:a,b,c` ≡ `h(a,v(b,c))`, `main-right` ≡ `h(v(b,c),a)`, `main-top` ≡ `v(a,h(b,c))`, the flat shapes ≡ flat splits), so old stored values and the `rk tab layout main-left:tty,code,web` shorthand keep working. A leaf is identified by a stable **leaf id**: a foreign leaf's id is its address string (`@12/tty`); a bare leaf's is the kind for a unique bare kind, `tty`, `tty#2`, … by occurrence among the bare leaves for duplicates. (260925-ww92-surface-layout-tree, 260925-jbz0-surface-cross-tab-tiles)
- **Templates** — presets survive as GENERATORS, not the model: `TEMPLATES` builds a tree for any N from a slot order (`row`, `col`, `main-left`, `main-right`, `main-top`, `main-bottom` — registry order; no `grid` template while the cap holds, since at N ≤ 3 it is structurally `main-bottom`; the `main-*` templates put explicit 0.58/0.42 sizes on the root split), `templateOf(tree)` returns `{ name, slots }` (the first template in registry order whose structure matches, `single` at one leaf, `custom` otherwise), `templatesFor(n)` lists the structurally distinct templates at N (`[]` at 1, `[row, col]` at 2, all six at 3), and `slotOrder(tree)` = `templateOf(tree).slots` — slot A stays the template's main tile for every consumer that read slot A (mobile slot A, the zoom fallback, palette ordering), falling back to reading order for a custom tree. `TEMPLATE_LABEL` carries the display names (`Row`, `Column`, `Main Left`, `Main Right`, `Main Top`, `Main Bottom`). (260925-ww92-surface-layout-tree)
- **Sizes** — divider positions, per-viewer localStorage ONLY, keyed `rk-layout-sizes:{server}:{windowId}:{structureSig}` where `structureSig` is the tree's shape with leaves replaced by reading-order indices (e.g. `h(0,v(1,2))`); keyed by structure, not leaves, so a swap keeps sizes with positions. The value is JSON — one fraction array per split in pre-order, each matching that split's child count and summing to 1 (1e-6 tolerance). Reads are validated (finite positive numbers, matching counts; any mismatch falls back to the matched template's own sizes, else equal splits) with try/catch-noop; writes happen on divider drag RELEASE only; never in the URL. The retired `rk-layout-ratios:*` keys are ignored — never read, never deleted. (ek6j, 260925-ww92-surface-layout-tree)
- **Zoom** — the one zoomed tile, per-viewer localStorage ONLY, keyed `rk-layout-zoom:{server}:{windowId}` storing a **leaf id** — a bare unique kind (`tty`, `web`, …), a duplicate-tty occurrence id (`tty#2`), or a foreign leaf's address (`@12/tty`) (`readStoredZoom`/`writeStoredZoom`, try/catch-noop like sizes; leaf-id-validated reads — `zoomLeafKind` derives the kind from any id form — and a stored id the layout no longer hosts resolves to its kind's first leaf in reading order, else reads/clears as unset). Desktop zoom targets the leaf; the mobile single-tile switch reads the same key — a bare kind doubles as its first leaf's id, so the switch group's kind writes stay valid under this shape (§ Mobile slot-A + the top-bar switch group). Zen-initiated zoom rides the same seam (zen is a viewer posture). (iip5, 260925-jbz0-surface-cross-tab-tiles)

### Pure layout model — `lib/layout-tree.ts` + `lib/surface-layout.ts` + `lib/layout-drop.ts`

Two pure, DOM-free modules with colocated tests (the `window-view.ts` pattern — § Design Decisions → Layout helpers live in pure modules). Untrusted URL/localStorage strings are validated by guards on the parse path (no `as` casts):

- **`lib/layout-tree.ts`** — the tree model itself, ported from the reference resolver in the design study `docs/wiki/surface-drop-zone-studies.html`: `parseLayoutTree(raw)` accepts the tree grammar — bare kinds and foreign `@<digits>/<kind>` leaves — AND the legacy preset grammar (bare kinds only, routed through the legacy conversion table, arity-checked per shape) and returns `null` for anything malformed — unknown kind, a non-canonical tree, a `/<n>` suffix, a repeated foreign address, or a repeated bare non-tty kind — never normalising input into validity (the self-window rule needs the owner, so write paths enforce it via `isCanonicalTree(node, owner)`); `serializeLayoutTree(node)` always emits the tree form, a foreign leaf emitting its address (`parse ∘ serialize` is the identity on canonical trees); the pure operations `normalise` (merges same-direction nesting, fractions multiplying through, and lifts single-child splits), `removeLeaf` (siblings absorb the removed share proportionally; `null` when nothing remains), `insertBeside` (wraps the target in a split on the side's axis, the pair splitting the target's share 50/50, then normalises), `swapLeaves` (an involution — sizes stay with positions; leaf identity moves whole, a foreign leaf carrying its home), `layoutRects(node, box, sizes?, gap)` → per-leaf rects keyed by leaf id, and `layoutDividers` → one divider per adjacent sibling pair of every split plus the intersection points where a divider's end meets a perpendicular divider; `structureSig`, `pathOf`, `leaves`, `bareLeaves` (the bare leaves' kinds — a bare kind and a foreign leaf of it coexist), `leafIds`; the foreign-leaf helpers `isForeignLeaf`, `leafAddress` (`@12/tty` for a foreign leaf, the bare kind otherwise), and `parseLeafAddress` (grammar-level address split; the foreign-`gui` rejection lives in validation, not here); `pruneDeadLeaves(tree, liveWindowIds)` (drops every foreign leaf whose home window is not in the live set, removeLeaf + normalise per pass, bare-`tty` fallback when the tree empties, identity when nothing is dead); the `TEMPLATES`/`templateOf`/`templatesFor`/`slotOrder`/`templateSizes` registry above; and the named constants `SPLIT_GAP_PX = 6`, `TEMPLATE_MAIN_FRACTION = 0.58`, `NOMINAL_BOX = 1600×1000`, `MAX_LAYOUT_LEN = 512`. The operations are N-generic — the tests run exhaustive invariants to N = 4 over the 4/36/528-placement enumeration, with no parse/render cap — and the shared fixture tables pin TS↔Go parity (`internal/layoutspec`'s tests read the same files: `layout-tree.fixtures.json` for the tree operations, `layout-grammar.fixtures.json` for the accept/reject grammar corpus). The drop-resolver-facing exports: `attachSizes`/`extractSizes`/`stripSizes` (pre-order `LayoutSizes` ↔ explicit per-split sizes — `attachSizes` shares leaf references, so a leaf located before the attach stays identical (`===`) through it) and the path-addressed `getAt`/`removeNodeAt` (the identity-tracked half of `removeLeaf` — a kind-derived id is ambiguous mid-edit). (260925-ww92-surface-layout-tree, 260925-c1ql-surface-drag-snap, 260925-jbz0-surface-cross-tab-tiles)
- **`lib/surface-layout.ts`** — the app-facing half: `Layout` IS `LayoutNode`. **`effectiveLayout(win, host?)`** — THE resolution: `degradeLayout(parseLayoutTree(win?.layout), win, host) ?? { leaf: "tty" }`. The payload's raw `@rk_win_layout` string is the single source; an unset, malformed, or fully-unavailable value renders the bare `tty` leaf, and the option itself is NEVER rewritten by degradation (the author sees their mistake with `show-options`). Because kind validation is parse-time, a stored value carrying a kind that has left the registry fails the parse wholesale and heals to the bare `tty` leaf — the stale option value is overwritten on the next layout write; no option migration sweep exists. (iip5)
- **Availability degradation (`degradeLayout`)** — an unavailable surface drops out TILE-BY-TILE via `removeLeaf` + `normalise`, the remaining structure kept (`v(tty,h(gui,web))` without gui renders `v(tty,web)`). No broken iframe ever mounts. `tty` and `web` never degrade — both are always available, so a `…web…` layout on a URL-less window keeps its web tile (rendering the onboarding state, § Web Tile → Onboarding state). `gui` degrades with the threaded host signal's `enabled` — off ⇒ the gui leaf drops out of the rendered tree while `@rk_win_layout` keeps its value, on ⇒ the same option renders the tile again, with no write on either transition (§ GUI Surface).
- **`availableTiles(win, host?)`** — the shared registry rail in shortcut order (`tty`, `code`, `web`, then `gui` iff `hasGui(host)`); `SURFACE_LABEL`/`SURFACE_GLYPH` carry the gui rows (`GUI` / `[]`).
- **The inbound translation mapper** — `translateLegacyParams(view, panel)`: `?view=X` → `single:X`; `?view=X&panel=Y` → `split-h:X,Y`; bare `?panel=Y` → `split-h:tty,Y` (legacy-grammar strings, which `parseLayoutTree` accepts downstream). A pure param→layout-string mapper whose only consumer is the one-shot route-entry translation (§ `app.tsx` integration) — inbound-only for one release, removed by the follow-up cleanup change. Beside it, the pure `legacyTranslationDecision({carried, storedLayout, storedLegacy, winLayout})` folds the four candidate sources into `{ write?: string; dropParams: boolean }` — the translation's decision table, unit-tested without React; its `write` is always re-serialized in the tree form. (iip5)
- **Verbs** (all pure, over trees): `addSurface(tree, added, rects?, focusedId?, sizes?)` — `added` is a kind or a full `LayoutLeaf` (a foreign `{leaf, home}` tiles another window's surface); splits the FOCUSED tile along its longer axis (ties → `h`, `focusedId` absent ⇒ the last leaf in reading order), the new leaf placed after it (right or bottom), and when that result breaks the 150×100 px floor in the caller's box it retries the remaining tiles largest-first, refusing (`null`) only when no split fits — using the caller's measured rects when given (desktop — the floor is checked against their bounding box) and the nominal box otherwise (mobile, the CLI), with the viewer's stored `sizes` switching the floor check to rects estimated from the current fractions (the split tile halves on the split axis); also refused: a repeated bare non-tty kind and a foreign address already in the tree — at landscape this reproduces `h(tty,web)` then `h(tty,v(web,code))`; `fitsFloor(tree, sizes, box)` — the floor as an offer gate over a finished tree (template rows); `toggleSurface(tree, surface, rects?, focusedId?, sizes?)` — the open-tile toggle: a kind with a BARE leaf closes its first bare leaf (a foreign leaf is never the close target), a kind open only as foreign leaves (or absent) grows by the add rule; `openTileKinds(tree)` — the toggle group's open state: the BARE kinds in slot order (a kind present only as foreign leaves reads as not open — the away marker is the separate signal); `closeSurface(tree, leafId)` — `removeLeaf` + `normalise`, the neighbours absorbing the size, `null` for the last leaf; `promote(tree, leafId)` — swaps the leaf with slot A (`slotOrder(tree)[0]`), a no-op when absent or already slot A; `swapDirectional(tree, leafId, dir, rects?)` — swaps with the nearest leaf across that edge whose rect overlaps on the perpendicular axis (ties: larger overlap, then reading order), a no-op without a neighbour; `applyTemplate(tree, name)` — rebuilds `TEMPLATES[name]` over the current slot order (lossy for a custom tree; `null` when the name is not in `templatesFor(n)`); `cycleTemplate(tree)` — the next entry of `templatesFor(n)` after the current template, wrapping (a `custom` tree cycles to the first template; one tile is a no-op). The `rk tab layout` CLI verbs are the shell twins of these verbs: `internal/layoutspec` ports them case-for-case to Go (named sentinel errors where the TS returns `null`, plus `RemoveOrTTY` — the remove-with-tty-fallback the borrow/return endpoints use — and `ValidateFor(n, owner)` for the write-path self-window rule), so the frontend's `applyLayout` POST path and the shell verbs share one tree model (260829-c143, 260925-ww92-surface-layout-tree, 260925-jbz0-surface-cross-tab-tiles).
- **Storage** — per-viewer only: the sizes key above + `rk-layout-zoom:{server}:{windowId}` (the zoomed LEAF ID — `zoomStorageKey`/`readStoredZoom`/`writeStoredZoom`, leaf-id-validated reads, `null` clears; § The model → Zoom) + `rk-layout-popped:{server}:{@N}` (the popped leaf ids — § Surface popout; `lib/popout.ts`). All reads/writes try/catch-noop. There is NO layout persistence in localStorage — the layout itself is `@rk_win_layout`. (iip5, 260925-3v9l-surface-popout)
- **`lib/layout-drop.ts`** — the pure drop resolver behind the header drag (DOM-free, the same module contract, colocated tests): the named constants `DRAG_THRESHOLD_PX = 4` (movement before a header press becomes a drag), `ROOT_EDGE_PX = 18` (the layout-edge bands — the outer 18 px of the layout box, four at any tile count, WINNING over tile bands), `EDGE_BAND_FRACTION = 0.25` / `EDGE_BAND_MIN_PX = 28` / `EDGE_BAND_MAX_PX = 110` (a tile-edge band is `clamp(25% of the axis, 28, 110)` px), and `MIN_TILE_W = 150` / `MIN_TILE_H = 100` (the per-viewport size floor gating drops AND every other growth offer — `addSurface`, template rows, the surface toggle, `Tile: Show`/`Tile: Bring`); `zoneAt(rect, point)` (per-side bands, a point in no band is `center`, a corner goes to the DEEPEST edge — the smallest distance/band ratio), `rootZoneAt(box, point)`, `hitTest(rects, box, point, dragged)` → `DropHit` (`root` / `center` / `edge` / `self`; `null` outside the box or in a gutter — a gutter point cancels), and `resolveDrop(tree, sizes, dragged, hit, box)` → `DropResult`: `move` (the canonical result tree + its pre-order `LayoutSizes` + `destId`, the dragged leaf's id at its new position), `noop` (the serialized result equals the input — a size-only rebalance still reads "no change"), `too-small` (some result leaf rect breaks the floor; never offered), `cancel` (null/self hit). Both take `dragged` as an id (an internal drag) OR a full `LayoutLeaf` — an EXTERNAL leaf not yet in the tree (a sidebar-row borrow), which runs the insert half only (wrap the target with the new leaf at 50/50, normalise — with no removal following, a same-axis root merge already leaves the new leaf at half the axis), treats the center zone as a `noop`, and can never hit `self` (its address is no rect key). Edge and root hits run ONE generic edit on the sizes-attached tree: wrap the target (path `[]` for a layout edge — the dragged tile takes 50 % of that axis, no 1/N special case) in a split on the side's axis with a CLONE of the dragged leaf at 50/50 inside the wrap (the wrap takes the target's share), remove the original dragged leaf (its share redistributes to its siblings in proportion — insert MUST precede remove, since removing first can collapse the target's parent), normalise; the clone carries the whole leaf (a foreign leaf keeps its home) and is tracked by identity through the pipeline (`pathOfNode`/`leafIndexAtPath`), so no sentinel value ever escapes the resolver. A center hit is `swapLeaves`, sizes staying with positions. `zoneRegion(hit, rects, box)` is the overlay's region for a `noop`/`too-small` hit (center = the target rect, an edge = that half of the target, a root edge = that third of the layout). The tests cover the band clamps, corner tie-breaks, root-band precedence, self/gutter hits, sizes carried through a drop, and the canonical/leaf-multiset/sizes invariants exhaustively for N ≤ 4 over the 4/36/528-placement enumeration (a center drop is an involution). (260925-c1ql-surface-drag-snap, 260925-jbz0-surface-cross-tab-tiles)

### `app.tsx` integration — derivation, optimistic write, one-shot translation

One layout-state block (§ Window Views → app.tsx integration): `baseLayout = useMemo(() => effectiveLayout(effectiveWindow, gui), [effectiveWindow, gui])` — the layout is DERIVED from the payload plus the host-global `useGui()` signal, never a `useState` copy and never a localStorage/URL read. The render layout additionally prunes dead foreign leaves at READ time — `pruneDeadLeaves` against the route server's window map (a killed home's tiles never render), the bare-`tty` fallback keeping a layout from rendering empty; the pruned tree is persisted only by the next layout write — there is no background writer (§ Tiles from other tabs). **`applyLayout(next)` is the ONE mutation path** — `setWindowOptions(server, windowParam, { "@rk_win_layout": serializeLayoutTree(next) })` through the unified `/options` seam, with no navigation and no localStorage write; tile verbs, the header drag's `onApplyLayout` commit (the drop's resolved result tree — the viewer's sizes are already stored under the new structure signature when it fires, § Tile renderer → Header drag), the surface toggles, the ▦ chip, and the palette `View:` actions all funnel through it. Beside it, `borrowInto(address, tree)` is the cross-tab bring path: an unheld or self-held address writes through `applyLayout`, a third-window-held one POSTs `borrowLayout` (the server unholds it there and writes this window's tree in one chained invocation) — both arms mirror the optimistic overlay and its rejection posture (revert + toast); `sendHome(from, leaf)` POSTs `returnLayout` for the ↩ / bring-back / `Tile: Send Back` verbs (the server recomputes both trees, so the client sends no tree). The render is OPTIMISTIC: a `pendingLayout { key, value }` state (key = `` `${server}:${windowId}` ``) is set before the POST, and the rendered layout is `effectiveLayout({ ...effectiveWindow, layout: pendingLayout.value }, gui)` while the key matches — cleared when the SSE payload's `layout` equals the pending value, on POST rejection (revert to the payload value; the mutation-feedback path surfaces the failure), or on a route change. Degradation still applies to the pending value, so the tiles reorder immediately and never flicker back when the tick confirms. Internal navigation (sidebar, ▾ switcher, palette) targets the bare route (§ Window Views → Internal navigation targets the bare route); layout consumers read the derived layout, never parse `location.search`. `togglePanel(surface)` is the toggle behind the top-bar group, the overflow Tiles rows, and the palette `Tile: Show/Hide` entries: an open surface — one with a BARE leaf — closes its first bare leaf (`closeSurface`; a foreign tile is never the close target), a closed one (a kind present only as foreign leaves reads as closed) grows via `addSurface` — the FOCUSED tile splits along its longer axis, floor-gated, the leaves' real rects on desktop and the viewer's stored sizes threaded so the check matches the rendered sizes — then `applyLayout` (a `null` mutation — closing the last tile, or no split fitting the floor — is a no-op; the boolean return reports whether the mutation applied). The focused-tile reports arriving through `onFocusedKindChange`/`onFocusedLeafChange` are stamped with the current `` `${server}:${windowParam}` `` key and count only while it matches — a window switch invalidates the mirror by key mismatch, not a clearing effect (a child effect's fresh report lands BEFORE a parent's same-render clear and would be wiped) — and until the component reports, slot A is the fallback (§ Design Decisions → Focused leaf owned by SurfaceLayout, kind mirrored to app.tsx).

**One-shot legacy translation (one release)** — a single effect per `(server, windowParam)` arrival, gated on `effectiveWindow !== null`, folds every retired layout carrier into the option: compute `carried = search.layout ?? translateLegacyParams(search.view, search.panel)`, the retired `rk-layout:{server}:{@N}` localStorage value, and `translateLegacyParams(readStoredView(…), readStoredPanel(…))`; when `win.layout` is empty, `legacyTranslationDecision` picks the winner (URL params > `rk-layout:` key > legacy view/panel keys) and exactly one POST writes it, always re-serialized in the tree form (a per-key in-flight ref suppresses a second fire while the payload still reads empty); when `win.layout` is set, nothing is written — the shared layout wins over any carried param. Both arms then navigate `replace: true` to the bare route when the URL carried any of the three params and delete exactly the four matching keys (`rk-layout:`, `runkit-window-view:`, `runkit-window-panel:`, `runkit-code-folder:` for this window only — never a prefix sweep); the code-folder arm POSTs `@rk_win_code_root` from a surviving `runkit-code-folder:` value when `win.codeRoot` is empty (§ Code Surface → The code root). Nothing in the frontend writes `?layout=` anymore; history entries are bare routes. (iip5)

### Present expansion — the layout write is the expand

`rk present` has NO client-side reaction: it is sugar over `rk tab web add <target> --show` — the CLI writes the web-tab slot AND the shared layout itself (the `webAddShow` helper: `WebAdd`, then `@rk_win_layout` gaining `web` when absent — grown through the shared `layoutspec.Add` verb (the last leaf splits along its longer axis — with no tile cap, Add serves every layout) — then `WebSelect(n)`). An agent or any external writer can equally expand by hand — `tmux set-option -w @rk_win_layout …`, `rk tab layout --add web`, or `rk tab web select <n>`. Every mounted viewer of the window repaints on the next option tick with no client-side state machine — the frontend renders `@rk_win_layout`, so any writer's expansion IS the render. Closing the revealed web tile POSTs the layout without it, which every viewer renders too. The frontend's own open-a-URL-in-tile sequence is the help-topic path (§ Help-topic open path below): it issues the same tab-add, layout, and select writes through the HTTP API, and every viewer repaints from the options exactly as after a CLI present. (iip5, 260829-c143)

- **e2e — `present-auto-expand.spec.ts`** — real tmux on the port-3020 rig (the `_tmux.ts` `windowOption` pattern), gated on the status bar's Connected readiness signal: a `tmux set-option -w @rk_win_layout split-h:tty,web` issued while a viewer is mounted repaints the web tile within the option tick (a hand-written legacy value — the permanent legacy parse — and a second external write repaints again); an invalid hand-written option value renders the bare `tty` leaf at render with the option itself left untouched.

### Help-topic open path — the frontend counterpart of `rk present`

The chevron menu's `Help topics` rows ([top-bar](/run-kit/ui/top-bar.md) § Help topics disclosure) and the six `Help: <topic>` palette actions ([keyboard-and-palette](/run-kit/ui/keyboard-and-palette.md) § Command Palette Actions) open a curated `https://shll.ai/…` documentation page in the CURRENT window's web tile through `openHelpTopic(topic)` in `lib/help-topics.ts` — a pure, React-free module with colocated tests that owns the `HELP_TOPICS` registry, the `helpTopicActionId`/`helpTopicPaletteLabel` derivations, the `HELP_TOPIC_EVENT` name (`rk:help-topic`), and the `isHelpTopic` detail guard. Every registered URL is framable (shll.ai is served by GitHub Pages and sends neither `X-Frame-Options` nor a frame-ancestors CSP), which is what lets the web tile's iframe render it.

`openHelpTopic` dispatches a cancelable `rk:help-topic` CustomEvent on `window` with the topic as `detail`. When `dispatchEvent` returns `false` (a listener cancelled it) the topic was taken in-tile and the dispatcher does nothing more; otherwise it falls back to `window.open(topic.url, "_blank", "noopener,noreferrer")` — the same call the `Help: Documentation` entry makes, so external navigation never unloads the live dashboard. `AppShell` (`app.tsx`) is the sole listener, registered only while `windowParam` is set (a terminal route with a current window) and removed on unmount or when the window clears, so on board, server, and host routes nobody cancels and the dispatcher opens a browser tab itself. The handler is `webAddShow` re-expressed client-side over existing pieces: it calls `event.preventDefault()` SYNCHRONOUSLY (the dispatcher decides on the return value, so an async cancel would arrive too late) and, still synchronously, decides the no-room case — when `web` is absent from the layout and `addSurface(layout, "web")` is `null` (no split fits the size floor) it opens `topic.url` through the same `window.open` fallback BEFORE any request is awaited, because `window.open` rides the click's transient user activation, which browsers revoke once the handler yields (a post-request open is what popup blockers return `null` for). Then (1) `addWebTab(server, windowParam, topic.url)` — `POST /api/windows/{id}/web`; the server dedupes an identical stored address and answers with the existing index and `existed: true`, so a re-open never grows the family — after which the no-room case stops: the tab stays added for later, and NO layout write and no select happen (§ Design Decisions → The help-topic path does not yield a slot when the layout has no room); otherwise (2) ensures a web surface: on mobile (`isMobile`) it calls `switchToTile("web")` (the same add mutation plus the per-viewer zoom key, so the phone shows the tile) and on desktop `applyLayout(addSurface(layout, "web"))` when `web` was absent — and (3) `selectWebTab(server, windowParam, index)` — `POST /api/windows/{id}/web/{n}/select` — so the topic is the active tab even when it already existed. Any rejection (the family-cap 409's `error` text included) surfaces via `useToast()` as `addToast(err.message || "Failed to open help topic", "error")` and leaves the layout unchanged. The writes land in the same tmux options `rk present` writes (`@rk_win_web_<n>`, `@rk_win_layout`, `@rk_win_web_active`), so every mounted viewer of the window repaints on the option tick with no client-side state machine (§ Present expansion above). (260910-58al)

- **e2e — `help-topics.spec.ts`** — real tmux on the derived e2e rig, `https://shll.ai/**` route-stubbed with a static page and `window.open` replaced by a recorder (`window.__openedUrls`): on a terminal route the menu row stores the URL as `@rk_win_web_1`, grows `tty` to `h(tty,web)`, sets `@rk_win_web_active` to `1`, shows one selected strip tab titled `shll.ai/run-kit/cron-schedule-kinds/`, and opens no browser tab; the `Help: Boards` palette twin takes the same path and a second selection leaves one tab with `@rk_win_web_2` unset; on the Host route every row carries `↗`, the fab-kit rows carry the `fab-kit` tag, and a click records exactly the topic URL on `window.open`.

### Tile renderer — `components/surface-layout.tsx`

Presentational by contract: the layout tree lives in `app.tsx` and arrives as the `layout` prop; the component owns only transient interaction state — zoom, the focused leaf, the in-flight drags (divider/intersection and the tile-header drag), the mount-once bookkeeping, and the cross-window code-frame LRU (§ Code Surface → Frame retention). `app.tsx` keys it by `server` (a same-server window switch keeps the grid mounted and re-renders it with a new `windowId` prop; only a server change remounts). Per-window transient state resets in ONE effect keyed on `[server, windowId]`, guarded against first mount (the `useState` initializers already seed the first window's values): the hide-never-unmount set (re-seeded from the new window's leaf ids), zoom (re-derived from the new window's stored zoom key), the focused leaf (back to the first leaf in reading order, the kind re-reported through `onFocusedKindChange`), the web page title, tty progress (idle, pending rAF commit cancelled), the find state (closed and cleared, decorations dropped, no focus grab), and an armed or in-flight tile drag (ended through the normal end path — capture released, never a commit); sizes and the web-tab override keep their own keyed effects. The code-frame record list is NOT among the resets — it is the cross-window state; a switch touches it only through the show bookkeeping, which creates or bumps the incoming window's record to most-recently-shown. The `web`/`gui` tiles carry `windowId` in their React key and remount per window; the tty tile's key is window-independent so its `TerminalClient` (xterm instance + relay stream) survives a same-session switch — [terminal](/run-kit/ui/terminal.md) § Terminal Relay; the `code` tile's key is likewise window-independent, keyed by its frame record (`code:<windowId>:<creation src>`; `code:pending` before the src resolves), so a same-server switch re-renders the mounted frame instead of unmounting it (§ Code Surface → Frame retention). Tile keys carry the TILE's window id (`${kind}${suffix}:${tileWindowId}`) — a foreign leaf's home — so two tiles showing different windows' terminals are distinct mounts. (260910-0awz, 260916-az44-persistent-code-frames-lru, 260925-jbz0-surface-cross-tab-tiles)

- **Tiles** — every leaf renders in ONE flat sibling list of absolutely positioned tiles, keyed by leaf id and placed from `layoutRects(tree, containerBox, sizes, 6px)` over a ResizeObserver-measured container (before the first measure, the nominal-box proportions render as percentage styles), so a restructure never re-parents a surviving tile's DOM node — a re-parented iframe reloads; each tile mounts the existing renderer unchanged: `TerminalClient` (tty), `IframeWindow` (web — fed the family `tabs={webTabs ?? []}` + `active={webActive}` plus the strip verbs: onboarding state on an empty family, one iframe per tab + the tab strip otherwise; § Web Tile → Web tab strip), `CodeSurface` (code; host reachability selects CONTENT, never availability), `GuiSurface` (gui — lazy-loaded behind `Suspense`; the host signal selects CONTENT — live canvas vs the enabled-but-unreachable empty state — and never mounts when `!enabled`, § GUI Surface). On desktop the tiles float as cards on the Shell stage's `bg-bg-inset` ground, the 6px gutters coming from the rect computation's gap at every leaf count including one (the outer 6px ground inset and the ground itself are the STAGE's, so the tiles and the sidebar card share ONE continuous ground with 6px from every stage edge) and each tile `border rounded-md`; the REST border is the dimmed 55% `rk-card-border` (`color-mix(in srgb, var(--color-border) 55%, transparent)` — the gap does the separating, the border only defines the card edge), while the focused tile keeps the full `border-accent-green` frame (suppressed at one leaf). (260812-wfic, 260814-011r, 260814-ldbs, 260925-ww92-surface-layout-tree)
- **Tile chrome (header + verbs)** — each tile carries a 35px header (`h-[35px]` = 32px content + a 3px `border-b-[3px] border-border` rule, 11px) painted on the tile's own `bg-bg-primary` surface — the rule separates header from content, not a second surface color, so header, content and compose strip read as one well inside the chrome frame; verb hovers and the meta chip use `bg-bg-card`, the step above that surface with: a leading agent-state `StatusDot` on **tty** tiles only (each tile resolves its OWN window's record from the server window map `app.tsx` passes — a foreign tile's dot reads its home window — the sidebar row's dot vocabulary; non-tty kinds and a null window render no dot), the kind's `SURFACE_GLYPH` glyph (`>_` tty, `://` web, `{}` code, `[]` gui) before the surface label, and the meta text (the code root's basename for code — § Code Surface → The code root — the web address's kind-specific display form for web, derived via `lib/web-url.ts` and never throwing, so relative `/present/…`/`/proxy/…` addresses get header meta too, and `wm · display` for gui — from the `GuiSignal`, degrading to the display alone when `wm` is empty, and swapping to `keys → desktop` while the keyboard-capture latch is set (taking `bg-accent-green/15` + `text-accent-green`, NO well — a label announcing state, not a control; § GUI Surface → Chord gate)) as a truncating inset chip (`bg-bg-inset rounded px-1.5`, 10px). **The web tile's header swaps the label+chip for a kind badge + page title**: a badge derived purely from the active web address via `classifyAddress` (`present` green / `:{port} proxy` amber / `external` blue — the design-study hues on the existing accent vocabulary; the `relative` kind renders NO badge and keeps the plain label + meta chip) plus the same-origin page title reported up from `IframeWindow`'s `onPageMeta` seam as the active engine's reported title changes (held as `SurfaceLayout` state, reset to `null` by the per-window reset effect on a window switch), falling back to the display form when cross-origin, pre-load, or empty (260819-v6y4). **The ONBOARDING web tile (empty active web tab) renders the plain form** — `://` glyph + `Web` label only, no badge, no page title, no meta chip; the derivation yields it on empty input without throwing (§ Web Tile → Onboarding state; 260821-zqlq). Verb buttons are fixed-size boxed buttons (24×24 — the WCAG 2.2 SC 2.5.8 target minimum — `coarse:` 26×26 on touch), **visible at rest at full opacity** in `text-text-secondary` (contrast-passing over `bg-bg-card` in both themes; no rest-state alpha dim — [visual-design](/run-kit/ui/visual-design.md) § Design Decisions → No rest-state alpha dim), hover giving `bg-bg-inset` + `text-text-primary`. Every verb glyph is a 14px SVG from the `top-bar-icons.tsx` ControlGlyph register with a kebab-case `data-icon` test seam ([top-bar](/run-kit/ui/top-bar.md) § Shared top-bar control glyphs). Header verbs come in TWO families. **Layout verbs** (generic — identical on every tile kind, count-gated): **zoom** (`data-icon="zoom"`, maximize corner brackets — one glyph serves zoom and unzoom; state is the accent-green + aria-label) and **✕ close-tile** (`tile-close`, bare crossed lines) — ✕'s hover turns `text-signal-red`, separated from zoom by a 1px hairline rule. Layout verbs render only above one leaf (zooming one tile is meaningless, closing the last tile is disallowed); rearrangement rides the header drag (§ Header drag (drop to snap) below) and the palette's `Layout: Promote` / `Tile: Swap <Dir>` verbs. A FOREIGN tile additionally carries **↩ send-home** in the same cluster (gated on the leaf's home ≠ the route window, not on the count; DISABLED while the home window is dead) invoking the return write (§ Tiles from other tabs), and its header identifies the home tab — the surface label plus the home window's name. **Content verbs** (per-kind): **gui** fills the header's `flex-1` spring with the session-toolbar fold cluster and renders a `⤢` fullscreen rail verb (ringed-toggle latch while the tile is fullscreen, at any tile count — the find-button precedent; § GUI Surface → The header fold cluster), with the layout-verb block suppressed while the gui tile is fullscreen; **code** renders up to two verbs before the layout-verb cluster on the ACTIVE window's visible tile (`kind === "code"` on a VISIBLE tile — a retained frame's hidden wrapper renders no header), in the order Follow terminal (conditional) · Reload editor · hairline (when the layout verbs render) · layout verbs — the cluster is right-aligned, so the conditional verb's appearance never shifts Reload editor or the layout verbs: **Follow terminal** (`aria-label="Follow terminal"`, Tip `Follow terminal — reopen the editor at <basename>`, the register's `FollowTerminalGlyph`) renders exactly while `codeRootFollowTarget(win)` is non-null — the verb's presence IS the drift indicator (§ The code root → Drift) — and disables from click until the follow promise settles (`codeFollowInFlight`); **Reload editor** (`aria-label="Reload editor"`, Tip `Reload editor — reboots this tab's workbench`, the shared `RefreshGlyph`) renders while the active window's frame record exists and the host is reachable (`codeReachable && codeFrames.some(r => r.windowId === windowId)` — a pending or unreachable tile has no frame to reload). Both use `VERB_BUTTON_CLASS` + `Tip` + `hover:text-text-primary` (not the destructive red), and both are palette-registered as `Code: Follow Terminal` / `Code: Reload Editor` through the `codeCommandsRef` seam — `SurfaceLayout` fills the optional `codeCommandsRef?: MutableRefObject<CodeTileCommands | null>` prop with `{ followTerminal, reload }` bound to the SAME internal functions the header buttons call, refilled after every render and cleared while no code tile is visible (the `guiCommandsRef` precedent; Constitution V — [keyboard-and-palette](/run-kit/ui/keyboard-and-palette.md) § Command Palette Actions) (c5w2); **tty** carries a bordered **pane segment** (`border border-border rounded`, 26px tall so the 24px buttons fit inside its border — reads as one control about the pane) holding **Split H · Split V · Close Pane**, placed right-aligned immediately LEFT of the layout cluster with the same hairline pattern between them. The segment renders at ANY leaf count including the bare `tty` leaf (the count gate applies to layout verbs only — splitting the only tile is the primary use case) and stays visible while zoomed (pane ops stay valid on a zoomed tile); at one leaf the layout cluster is absent, so the header shows only the pane segment on the right. Both duplicate tty tiles render it, each segment acting on ITS tile's window (a foreign tty tile's home) and that window's active pane. Split H/V are two separate one-click buttons reusing `SplitHorizontalGlyph` / `SplitVerticalGlyph`. **Close-distinction contract** (the two destructive closes in one header): Close Pane is the boxed ⊠ `ClosePaneBoxedGlyph` INSIDE the segment with hover `text-signal-red` and a consequence-stating Tip (`Close pane — kills the tmux pane`), firing `closePane` immediately (no confirm); the tile-close ✕ stays the bare glyph isolated far right behind the hairline — distinction by shape, grouping, and ~5-button spatial separation. Ahead of the pane segment (after the `flex-1` spacer, left of Split H/V), tty tiles carry the **export button** (`data-icon="export"`, arrow-down-to-line; `Tip`-wrapped, `aria-label="Export terminal output"`), opening a two-section fixed-position menu (`role="menu"`, `.rk-popup-elev`, monospace; closes on pick, outside click, and Escape — the top-bar-overflow-menu dismissal contract): **This view — client buffer** (Download snapshot `.html · colors kept`, Download transcript `.txt · buffer text`, Copy visible screen) over **Full history — server capture** (Download pane history `.txt · capture-pane -S -` — **disabled with a hint on alt-screen windows**: when the window's SSE `altScreen` flag is true (agent TUIs — the active pane is on tmux's alternate screen, so tmux holds no scrollback for it), the row renders `disabled` + `aria-disabled` with `disabled:opacity-60`/`disabled:cursor-not-allowed` dimming and hover suppressed, its subtitle replaced by `agent TUI on alternate screen — tmux holds no scrollback`; no click fires, no fetch. The section header and the three client rows are unchanged). The split is load-bearing: the xterm client buffer holds only what streamed since attach, so for normal-screen panes tmux owns the scrollback and the honest full-history artifact comes from the server capture — for alt-screen panes the client buffer (scrollback-sized — [terminal](/run-kit/ui/terminal.md) § Terminal Scrollback) is the only transcript that exists; the menu sections name which artifact each row produces. Mechanics: [terminal](/run-kit/ui/terminal.md) § Terminal Export. (260819-shqo, 260820-4le0) Mobile renders no header chrome at all. Verb clicks call parent callbacks: layout verbs run the pure mutation + the `applyLayout` option POST; content verbs go through the `onSplitPane(horizontal, tileWindowId)` / `onClosePane(tileWindowId)` props — the second argument is the TILE's own window id (a foreign tty tile's home) — which `app.tsx` wires to its `executeSplit` / `executeClosePane` optimistic actions (split cwd = the tile window's worktree path) — `SurfaceLayout` stays presentational with no API imports. (260812-wfic, 260813-w1lf, 260925-jbz0-surface-cross-tab-tiles)
- **Tty task progress (line + chip)** — passthrough-wrapped OSC 9;4 from the pane renders as tile chrome (design study state 03). `SurfaceLayout` consumes `TerminalClient`'s `onProgressChange` seam on EVERY tty mount into a slot keyed by the tile's OWN window (the `webPageTitle` slot precedent, per window — a foreign tty tile feeds its HOME window's slot, so its progress renders on its own tile and never the route window's; same-window duplicates fire idempotently and the signal survives a hidden primary), folded by the pure `lib/tty-progress.ts` reducer (colocated tests): 0 → idle, 1 → determinate (clamped 0–100), 2 → error at last-known value, 3 → indeterminate, 4 → paused at last-known value; unknown codes ignored. Events reduce immediately (retention needs event order) but commit at most ONE state update per animation frame (latest wins, rAF cancelled on unmount) so bursty emitters cannot re-render storm the grid. Renders, tty tiles only: a **2px progress line** overlaying the content's top edge — a zero-height `.rk-tty-progress` wrapper with an absolute `pointer-events: none` bar, deliberately NEVER in-flow (an in-flow strip would resize the terminal container → fit → PTY resize churn) — green determinate fill at value% (`role="progressbar"` + `aria-valuenow`), red at last-known width on error, amber on pause, and an indeterminate sweep reusing the web tile's `rk-web-progress-sweep` keyframes (reduced-motion: zeroed to a static dim fill, gated in `globals.css`); plus a **`{value}%` bordered chip** beside the `StatusDot` in the tty tile header (states 1/2/4 only — indeterminate has no percentage; green/red/amber via the web-kind-badge chip idiom). The header is desktop-only chrome, so the chip is too; the line renders on mobile as the glanceable signal. **Per-viewer ephemeral stream state**: component state only (no backend, no SSE, no persistence), reset to idle by the per-window reset effect on a window switch (a pending rAF commit cancelled); a stale value with no updates is left as-is — the emitter owns lifecycle via state 0. Only tmux-passthrough-wrapped emitters (`\ePtmux;…\e\\`, inner ESCs doubled) can feed it — raw OSC 9;4 is swallowed by tmux (spike-verified). Board pane cards mount `TerminalClient` without the callback, so terminal-route tiles are the only progress surface. e2e `tty-progress.spec.ts` prints a wrapped sequence into a real pane — the passthrough-transport regression guard; unit coverage in `tty-progress.test.ts` + the `surface-layout.test.tsx` progress block. (260819-1vxq)
- **tty find bar (⌕ / ⌘F)** — the PRIMARY tty tile only (the FIRST tty leaf in reading order, the `wsRef`/`focusRef` primary-only precedent — duplicate tty tiles get no find affordance) carries a find header button (`data-icon="find"`, the register's search SVG; left of the pane segment, `aria-label="Find in terminal"`, `aria-pressed` + accent-green while open — the web tile's URL bar renders the same `FindGlyph`, one find identity across surfaces) and, while open, a find-bar row below the header (the web tile's below-URL-row pattern, `data-testid="terminal-find-bar"`) mounting the shared `FindBar` (§ Web Tile → Find bar) with two terminal-native toggles the web consumer lacks — `Aa` (match case) and `.*` (match regex), `aria-pressed` buttons lit accent-green when on — plus the muted buffer-scope note once a search has run. `SurfaceLayout` owns the find state (open, query, toggles, counter) and drives the passive `SearchAddon` through a stable proxy `searchAddonRef` whose setter mirrors the instance into state — TerminalClient fills the ref asynchronously at init, the subscription and search effects key on the mirrored state, and the proxy's identity is constant because it sits in TerminalClient's init-effect deps. Closing the bar (✕, Escape, the find-button toggle) clears decorations and query and returns focus to the pane. Entry points share one seam: the find button toggles locally; the chord and palette dispatch `terminal-find:open`, which the mounted layout listens for (§ Design Decisions → The find-bar open seam is a document CustomEvent). Search mechanics (options mapping, decorations, the overview ruler): [terminal](/run-kit/ui/terminal.md) § Terminal Addons → Find-in-terminal. (260819-zqf9)
- **Focused tile (accent state)** — exactly one tile is the FOCUSED tile (the one that last received pointer/keyboard interaction): its border and kind glyph turn `accent-green` (the tmux active-pane metaphor), suppressed at one leaf. The focused LEAF is `SurfaceLayout` transient component state keyed by leaf id (default the first leaf in reading order; falls back there when the focused tile closes or leaves the layout; the per-window reset effect returns it there on a window switch and clears the dedupe ref so the kind re-reports — the zoom precedent), reported upward as the focused KIND via `onFocusedKindChange` (and as the leaf id via `onFocusedLeafChange`); `app.tsx` mirrors the kind for the `ttyOnly` shortcut gate and the palette (§ Keyboard Shortcuts → Dispatch seams) and sets focus by kind through a registered `focusTileRef` seam (the `zoomToggleRef` pattern, first leaf of that kind). Focus-assignment seams: `pointerdown` (capture) and `focusin` on the tile wrapper hear parent-DOM interaction ONLY — no parent-document event fires for interaction inside an iframe — so both iframe renderers report in-frame interaction through their optional `onInteract` callbacks: `CodeSurface` from its contentDocument keydown/pointerdown listeners, `IframeWindow` from the same contentDocument pattern inside its per-tab engine plus a window-blur/activeElement fallback for cross-origin content (§ Web Tile → Interaction + reclaim seam); the gui canvas sits in rk's own document, so `GuiSurface` reports from its wrapper's own pointerdown/keydown capture handlers (§ GUI Surface). the seams report the kind SYNCHRONOUSLY (a chord pressed immediately after a click must see the new gate), with the reporting effect kept only for the mount default and the close fallback. The same seams also write the per-window FOCUS MEMORY (`lib/focus-memory.ts`): `tty` records on the wrapper's `onPointerDownCapture` (and the palette `focusTileRef` seam) — deliberately NOT in `focusLeaf` or the wrapper's `onFocus`, where focusin's target-first bubbling would let it clobber the in-tile strip's later `compose` write — with a `data-compose-strip` carve-out skipping presses inside the docked strip; `code` records ONLY via `onInteract`, never on mere focusin (the anti-steal asymmetry); and the code tile's wrapper `onFocus` consults the steal guard (`isGuardArmed` + `recallFocus` ≠ `code`) before `focusLeaf`, skipping the flip while an armed grab contradicts the remembered kind — the guard's no-flip half ([focus-ownership](/run-kit/ui/focus-ownership.md)). (260812-wfic, 260815-41t9, 260815-ltz7)
- **Zoom** — component state PERSISTED per viewer: one tile full-center, the others hidden at display level; renders only above one leaf. `zoomedLeafId` initializes from `readStoredZoom(server, windowId)` (the stored LEAF ID — a bare kind doubles as its first leaf's id; an exact id whose kind is still hosted resolves to that kind's first leaf in reading order, `null` when absent or unhosted) and every flip writes `writeStoredZoom` (the zoomed leaf's id, `null` on unzoom) — a zoom survives reload for that browser while never touching the shared layout or another viewer (§ The model → Zoom). The clear-when-the-layout-cannot-host effect also clears the key when the zoomed leaf's kind leaves the tree. The palette's `Layout: Expand`/`Restore` entries (ids `layout-zoom`/`layout-unzoom` and every internal name keep the zoom vocabulary — only user-facing labels say Expand/Restore, § Design Decisions → Content zoom owns "Zoom") and zen mode's enter/exit body observe/trigger it through an app-level ref seam that toggles the FOCUSED leaf (state not lifted — the per-window reset effect re-derives the zoom from the new window's stored key on a window switch): zen entering with more than one leaf zooms the focused tile when not already zoomed (riding the same key — zen is a viewer posture), and exit unzooms ONLY a zen-initiated zoom — a pre-existing user zoom survives ([keyboard-and-palette](/run-kit/ui/keyboard-and-palette.md) § Stateful surface chords). The zoomed tile's header shows the zoom glyph in `accent-green`; ✕ stays, and a tty tile's pane segment stays visible too (content verbs remain valid on a zoomed tile). (260812-wfic, 260813-w1lf, 260823-cwvv, 260820-o8cr, iip5, 260925-jbz0-surface-cross-tab-tiles)
- **Hide-never-unmount (P3)** — a surface opened earlier this route visit stays mounted (`hidden` class) when closed or zoomed away, so iframe/terminal state survives; bookkeeping is keyed by LEAF ID and is per-window (the reset effect re-seeds the set from the new window's leaf ids on a window switch). For the tty tile the hidden state also reaches `TerminalClient` as its `hidden` prop, driving xterm's render pause ([terminal](/run-kit/ui/terminal.md) § Terminal Addons). For `code` the mechanism extends ACROSS same-server window switches: a bounded per-server LRU of live frames (`CODE_FRAME_CAP_DESKTOP = 3` / `CODE_FRAME_CAP_MOBILE = 1`, the cap counting the active window's frame) with eviction on overflow, the window leaving `liveWindowIds`, non-follow root divergence, or a `codeReachable` `true→false` flip, an immediate evict-down on a cap decrease, and a closed code tile staying retained and counted — § Code Surface → Frame retention.
- **Dividers** — one divider per adjacent sibling pair of every split, derived by `layoutDividers` and rendered in the 6px gutter; a drag edits those TWO siblings' fractions only (their combined share constant — never the tree), clamped by the two-sibling `clampSiblingFraction` (`lib/right-panel.ts`, composing `clampRatio`'s `MIN_PANEL_WIDTH_PX` 280px floor per side on the divider's own axis), and persists under the current structure signature on RELEASE; tiles stay LIVE mid-drag (no IntersectionObserver suspension unmount — the board pane-resize bug class, § Boards View → Desktop Relay-Connection Suspension); tile content gets `pointer-events: none` mid-drag so iframes cannot swallow `pointermove`. The drag posture crosses to the native web engine as a React context: `SurfaceLayout` wraps its tile grid in `<TileDragContext.Provider>` (`lib/tile-drag-context.ts`, a `TileDragPosture = "idle" | "resize" | "move"`, default `"idle"`, read via `useTileDragPosture()`): `"resize"` while a divider or intersection drag runs (the native engine live-resizes its guest — § Web Tile → The `native` engine), `"move"` while a tile-header drag runs (the guest hides — § Header drag (drop to snap) below), `"idle"` on the mobile return (the mobile branch drags nothing); the mid-drag `pointer-events-none` class covers every posture. (q2xk, 260925-c1ql-surface-drag-snap) The chrome is the three-state gap-seam treatment (`rk-divider`/`rk-sash`/`rk-grips` in `globals.css`, § visual-design.md → Hover-Animation Vocabulary): rest = 3 centered ~2.5px grip dots (border-colored, `pointer-events-none`, axis-stacked) in the gutter; hover = a 5px rounded accent-green sash pill filling the seam, inset 10px from both ends so it never touches the tile radii, after a ~150ms anti-flicker `transition-delay` (dots invert to the ground color while lit); drag = the same pill, zero delay (`rk-sash-lit` — also applied to BOTH dividers while the intersection zone is hot/dragged). Hit zones are 14px (`w-3.5`/`h-3.5`, same `-translate-*-1/2` centering); drag mechanics (pointer capture, the 280px-floor two-sibling clamp, persist-on-release, `role="separator"` + `aria-valuenow`) are unchanged, and dividers still render only when not zoomed and never on a bare leaf. Under `prefers-reduced-motion` the sash delay/fades zero out (the states themselves remain). (260812-ab5v, 260814-011r)
- **Intersection zone (perpendicular divider meetings)** — a ~20px `surface-divider-intersection` zone centered on every point where a divider's end meets a perpendicular divider (geometry single-sourced from `layoutDividers`; a tree with no such meeting renders none), z-ordered above both dividers so it wins the junction hit-test, `cursor: move` (single-axis dividers keep `cursor-col-resize`/`cursor-row-resize`). Hover lights BOTH sashes (same 150ms delay semantics; mid-seam hover lights only that seam); drag moves BOTH fraction pairs at once — pointer x/y map to the two dividers, each pair edited as its own two-sibling drag and clamped by the shared `clampSiblingFraction` on its own axis with no term from the other pair (280px floor both sides), both persisted on release via `writeStoredSizes` with pointer capture and `touchAction: "none"`. (260814-011r, ek6j, 260925-ww92-surface-layout-tree)
- **Header drag (drop to snap)** — a primary-button `pointerdown` on a tile header's BACKGROUND arms a drag (a `closest("button, [role='menu'], [data-no-tile-drag]")` guard keeps buttons, menus, the pane segment, code verbs, and the meta chip on their own press behavior); the drag starts only past `DRAG_THRESHOLD_PX` (4 px) — below it the press stays the focus click the pointerdown-capture seam already delivered. On start the header `setPointerCapture`s and the drag snapshots the tree, the effective sizes, the layout box, and the leaf rects (`layoutRects` over the ResizeObserver-measured container); `hitTest` + `resolveDrop` (§ Pure layout model → `lib/layout-drop.ts`) run against that snapshot, the resolution cached per zone (keyed by hit kind + target + side — one resolver pass per zone change, not per pointermove). Mid-drag routing follows the divider drag's hardening: window-level `pointermove`/`pointerup`/`pointercancel` gated on the captured pointerId (engines can drop element capture over iframe content), plus a window CAPTURE `keydown` for Escape (`preventDefault` + `stopPropagation` — the terminal never sees it). While the drag runs the `TileDragContext` posture is `move` (the native web guest hides so the overlay can paint over its tile), tile content goes `pointer-events-none`, the dragged tile dims (`opacity-50`), and the header shows `cursor-grab` when a drag can arm / `cursor-grabbing` mid-drag. The overlay (`data-testid="tile-drop-overlay"`, z-30, `pointer-events-none`, drawn in the layout container's coordinate space) previews the RESULT: a `move` draws every leaf rect of the result tree at the result sizes (glyph + `SURFACE_LABEL`; the dragged tile's destination `tile-drop-dest` filled accent-green, the others outlined); a `noop` marks the hovered zone's region (`zoneRegion`) neutral "No change"; a `too-small` drop is refused with a red "Too small" region, never offered; `cancel` draws nothing. Release on a cached `move` commits exactly one write: `writeStoredSizes` under `structureSig(result.tree)` FIRST (the first render under the new signature reads them), then `onApplyLayout(result.tree)` → the parent's `applyLayout` (the ONE `@rk_win_layout` mutation path — § `app.tsx` integration), focus landing on the dragged tile's new position (`destId`). Every other ending cancels with no write: Escape, release outside the layout or over the dragged tile itself, a `noop`/`too-small` release, `pointercancel`, unmount or window switch (the per-window reset routes through the normal end path, capture released), and a mid-drag `layout` prop change (another viewer's write made the snapshot stale — compared by SERIALIZED form, so a same-structure SSE tick never disturbs a live drag). The drag never arms on a coarse pointer, a zoomed render, a single-leaf layout, or the mobile branch (`canDragTiles = !isMobile && !coarsePointer && !zoomed && arity > 1`). (260925-c1ql-surface-drag-snap)
- **Mobile (R13)** — below `isMobileViewport()` only ONE leaf renders (`mobileActiveSlot`, a reading-order index, default 0 — out-of-range clamps to 0): no dividers, no verb chrome, no multi-tile grid (§ Mobile slot-A + the top-bar switch group below).

### Tiles from other tabs — foreign leaves, the away placeholder, borrow/return

A leaf may address ANOTHER tab's surface on the same server (`@N/<kind>` — § The model above): a surface is **live in exactly one place**, and borrowing moves it (§ Design Decisions → A surface is live in one place; away is derived). (260925-jbz0-surface-cross-tab-tiles)

- **Away derivation** — the backend derives `WindowInfo.awayIn` (surface kind → holder window id, `omitempty`) once per `FetchSessions` pass across ALL of the server's windows (`tmux.DeriveAwayIn` — a foreign leaf `@A/<kind>` in window W's parsed layout sets `A.awayIn[kind] = W`; dead homes and self-naming leaves are ignored, duplicate holders resolve first-wins in list order; nothing stored, Constitution II). The frontend mirrors the field on `types.ts`'s `WindowInfo`. ([api-and-sockets](/run-kit/api-and-sockets.md) § API Layer)
- **The away placeholder** (`components/surface-placeholder.tsx`) — in tab A, a BARE leaf `<kind>` whose `A.awayIn[kind]` names a live holder renders `SurfacePlaceholder` (`data-testid="surface-placeholder"`, dashed border, monospace) INSTEAD of mounting the surface — the mount is gated at the caller, so an away tty opens NO relay stream and no second code-server host boots. It shows "<Surface> is in tab <holder name>", a **bring back** button (the return below, from = the holder), a **go to <holder>** button (navigates to the holder's route), the surface's **status dot** (tty only — the home window's dot exactly as the sidebar row shows it), and **✕**, which closes the slot (`closeSurface` + `applyLayout`, the remaining tiles filling the space) and is HIDDEN when the placeholder is the only leaf (the fallback would render the same placeholder). A sole-leaf placeholder fills the tab; mobile's single slot renders it likewise. The top-bar toggle re-adds a dismissed slot — rendering as the placeholder while away — and shows the away marker (§ Surface Toggles; [top-bar](/run-kit/ui/top-bar.md) § Surface toggles).
- **↩ send home** — a FOREIGN tile's header carries the ↩ verb in the Expand/Close layout cluster, shown only when the leaf's home ≠ the route window and DISABLED while the home window is dead (the read-time prune drops the leaf on the next payload anyway); the header identifies the home tab (a name chip beside the surface label). The palette twin is `Tile: Send Back to <home window>` for the focused foreign tile, plus `Tile: Bring Back <Surface>` on the home tab per away bare leaf (§ ▦ Layout chip + palette + chords).
- **The return write** — `POST /api/layout/return` `{from, leaf}` recomputes both trees server-side from current tmux state: the holder's tree minus the leaf (bare-`tty` fallback when it empties) and — only when the home's layout no longer carries a bare `<kind>` slot (✕ dismissed it) — the home's tree with `<kind>` re-added by `layoutspec.Add` (nominal geometry), written in ONE chained tmux invocation (only the holder when the home keeps its slot; a dead home just removes the leaf) ([api-and-sockets](/run-kit/api-and-sockets.md) § API Layer; § Design Decisions → Two-tab writes go through server-recomputed endpoints).
- **Borrow** — the keyboard route is the palette's `Tile: Bring <window> <Surface> here` (per other window on the route server × its `tty`/`code`/`web` surfaces not already in this layout, never `gui`, floor-gated by the add rule); the pointer route is the **sidebar row drag** (§ Design Decisions → Row borrow reuses the HTML5 window-drag): while a `WINDOW_DRAG_MIME` window-drag is in flight, `SurfaceLayout` mounts a transparent **drop-catcher overlay** above all tiles (window-level `dragstart`/`dragend` listeners — the payload is readable at dragstart — tiles go `pointer-events-none` and the native web guest hides, the header drag's mid-drag seam), hit-tests the snapshot leaf rects with `zoneAt`/`rootZoneAt`, and shows the same result-preview overlay (including red "too small" and neutral "no change"). Edge zones insert `@<dragged>/tty` through the external-leaf mode of `resolveDrop` (§ Pure layout model → `lib/layout-drop.ts`); the center zone is a no-op, as are the refused drops (the route window's own row, a `@<dragged>/tty` already in the layout, a cross-server drag) — they preview "no change" and write nothing. The row drag's other consumers (reorder, move-to-session, board pin) are untouched ([sidebar](/run-kit/ui/sidebar.md) § Window drag-and-drop reorder). The write itself is `app.tsx`'s `borrowInto`: a plain `applyLayout` when the address is unheld or held by this window, `POST /api/layout/borrow` `{to, leaf, tree}` otherwise — the server locates the current holder and writes both tabs in one chained invocation.
- **Live in one place on every write** — a generic layout write (the `/options` handler, `rk tab layout`) that introduces a foreign leaf already held by a third tab is REJECTED (409 naming the holder, pointing at borrow — `tmux.CheckLiveInOnePlace`); reject, never silently steal.
- **Dead addresses** — the client prunes foreign leaves whose window is not in the route server's window set before rendering (`pruneDeadLeaves` → normalise → bare-`tty` fallback; § `app.tsx` integration); the pruned tree is persisted only by the next layout write, and the backend derivation ignores dead leaves.
- **Follow the tile's own window** — every per-tile read/write resolves the tile's OWN window (a foreign leaf's home, the route window for a bare leaf): a foreign tty opens its relay stream with the home window id and `isolate: true` (the `_rk-iso-*` attach — [terminal](/run-kit/ui/terminal.md) § Terminal Relay (frontend), [tmux-sessions](/run-kit/tmux-sessions.md) § Iso Sessions); the FOCUSED tty tile (bare or foreign) registers as the shell's focused terminal with its own server/session/window/wsRef, so the compose strip's send target, the bottom-bar keys, and focus memory follow it ([compose-and-bottom-bar](/run-kit/ui/compose-and-bottom-bar.md) § Docked Compose Strip), while the first BARE tty leaf keeps the shared `wsRef`/`focusRef`/`searchAddonRef` holder (a foreign tty never takes it) and holds the focused slot while a non-tty tile is focused; the tty tile's Split/Close Pane verbs target the tile's window and its worktree path; progress slots are keyed PER TILE WINDOW — a foreign tty's chip/line render on its own tile, never the route window's (§ Design Decisions → Progress slots are keyed per tile window); a foreign code tile resolves its src, code root, frame record, and code-root seed/follow writes from the home window's `WindowInfo` (a borrowed editor reuses the home's retained LRU frame when one exists); a foreign web tile reads and writes the home window's `@rk_win_web_<n>` family, the optimistic `webOverride` keyed `entryKey(server, home)`. Tile keys (`${kind}${suffix}:${windowId}`) use the home window id for foreign leaves; sizes stay keyed by route window + structure signature.

### Surface popout — the per-viewer popped set + the chrome-less `?pop=` window

Any tile can pop out into its own browser window showing that one surface; the opener hides the popped leaf for THIS viewer only and reflows over the rest — the shared `@rk_win_layout` is never written by pop-out or pop-in (§ Design Decisions → Popout is a viewer posture, never a layout write). (260925-3v9l-surface-popout)

- **The popped set (`lib/popout.ts` + `hooks/use-popout.ts`)** — the pure halves live in `lib/popout.ts` (the `window-view.ts`/`layout-tree.ts` module contract — DOM-free, type narrowing over `as`, colocated `popout.test.ts`), the channel/timer wiring in `hooks/use-popout.ts` (the hooks-folder split, colocated `use-popout.test.ts` over a stubbed BroadcastChannel + fake timers). Storage: `rk-layout-popped:{server}:{@N}` → a JSON array of leaf ids (the same leaf-id vocabulary as zoom — `tty`, `tty#2`, `code`, `@12/tty`; § Design Decisions → Popped leaves are keyed by leaf id) via `poppedKey`/`readPopped`/`writePopped`, try/catch-noop, a corrupt or absent value reading as the empty set and the empty set removing the key. The same-origin `BroadcastChannel("rk-popout")` (constants `POPOUT_CHANNEL`, `POPOUT_HEARTBEAT_MS = 2000`, `POPOUT_STALE_MS = 6000` — three heartbeat intervals of liveness margin — `POPOUT_FALLBACK_WIDTH/HEIGHT = 1200×800`) coordinates the windows with `{type, server, window, leaf}` messages (`isPopoutMessage` shape-validates; a malformed message or one naming another server/window is ignored): `opened` (popout → openers, on mount and in reply to `ping`), `alive` (the heartbeat), `closed` (popout → openers, on `pagehide` and before a `pop-in`-driven `window.close()`), `pop-in` (opener → popout: close yourself), `ping` (opener → popouts on mount, so live popouts re-announce immediately instead of the opener waiting a heartbeat). Channel and storage scope are per-origin per-browser-profile, so every opener tab of `@N` in the same browser hides the popped leaf — "this viewer" = this browser profile, the `rk-layout-sizes:*` precedent. A missing BroadcastChannel degrades the hooks to storage-only: marks still apply and clear, only liveness (heartbeat/stale sweep) and remote pop-in go silent.
- **Opener render reduction** — `usePoppedSet(server, windowId, enabled, treeLeafIds)` returns `{ popped, popOut, popIn }`: persisted marks apply immediately on mount (no flash of the popped tile) and settle against the popouts' liveness — `ping` on mount, `opened`/`alive` refreshing a mark's last-seen, `closed` clearing it, and a sweep interval dropping any mark silent for `POPOUT_STALE_MS` (`sweepStale` gives a never-sighted mark one full stale window from mount, covering the mount → ping → `opened` round trip; the sweep covers a crashed popout process where `pagehide` never fired). `treeLeafIds` is the FULL shared tree's ids: a mark whose leaf left the layout (another viewer closed the tile) is pruned from storage, while the popout itself keeps running — it addresses the surface, not the layout. `popOut(leafId, rect?)` writes the mark optimistically BEFORE `window.open(popoutUrl(server, @N, leafId), popoutWindowName(...), popoutFeatures(rect))` — the name `rk-pop:{server}:{@N}:{leaf-id}` makes a repeat Pop out focus/reuse the existing popout window, and the features size the popup from the tile's rendered rect (1200×800 fallback) — and rolls the mark back with a "Pop-out blocked by the browser" toast when `window.open` returns `null`; `popIn(leafId)` posts `pop-in` and clears the mark. `app.tsx` renders `reducePopped(layout, popped)` (`removeLeaf` of each popped id present in the tree, absent ids ignored, `tree: null` when every leaf is popped) as the RENDER tree, while every shared-layout mutation keeps computing from the full `layout`; a popped zoomed leaf renders unzoomed (the persisted zoom key clears through the ordinary unzoom path) and focus falls back to the first leaf in reading order. When the reduction empties the tree, the opener renders `PoppedOutPlaceholder` (`components/popout-states.tsx`, `data-testid="popped-out-placeholder"` — one row per popped leaf, "<Surface> is popped out" + a Pop back in button, foreign leaves named by their home window) with the tiles still mounted-hidden behind it so their streams survive — a layout never renders empty (the away-placeholder precedent).
- **The popout window** — the terminal route with `?pop=<leaf-id>` renders the popout posture ([routes-and-shell](/run-kit/ui/routes-and-shell.md) § URL Structure): `AppShell` validates the value via `parsePopLeaf(raw, routeWindow)` (bare kinds, `tty#<n>` occurrences, foreign addresses via `parseLeafAddress`; a foreign `gui`, a malformed value, or — once the sessions payload has arrived — a surface window known-absent degrade to the ordinary terminal render, never a route error) and mounts `SurfaceLayout` with a ONE-LEAF tree keyed to the URL, never the shared layout — the popout keeps rendering its surface while the surface's window lives even if the leaf leaves `@rk_win_layout`. The `popoutLeafId` prop puts the component in its popout posture: chrome-less via the published slot-context channel (top bar, sidebar, bottom bar/compose strip, quake drawer/tongue, and the command palette all drop — [routes-and-shell](/run-kit/ui/routes-and-shell.md) § Chrome Architecture), the tile header keeping its per-kind content verbs (pane segment, code Follow/Reload, gui toolbar fold, find) while the whole layout-verb cluster (zoom/↩/✕/Pop out) is replaced by a single **Pop back in** verb (`aria-label "Pop <Surface> back in"`, the `SendHomeGlyph`), and the mobile branch forced off (`isMobile={false}` — the popup is sized from the tile's rect, often under the 640px mobile breakpoint, and the mobile branch renders no tile header, the popout's only Pop back in affordance). The document title is `<Surface> · <window name>` — the leaf's HOME window for a foreign leaf, `SURFACE_LABEL` shared so the labels never drift — through `useBrowserTitle`'s override parameter. Every navigation effect is gated off in the posture (no mount-time alignment — selecting would yank every other viewer of the server — no URL writeback, no kill-redirect, no `onSessionNotFound` navigation): when the surface's window leaves the payload the popout renders `PopoutEnded` ("Window closed", `data-testid="popout-ended"`, the surface unmounted) and never navigates. Presence wiring is `usePopoutPresence(server, windowId, leafId, enabled)` — `opened` on mount and in reply to `ping`, the `alive` heartbeat, `closed` on `pagehide`, and a `pop-in` naming this leaf posting `closed` then `window.close()` (legal for a script-opened window); the popout's own Pop back in verb posts `closed` and closes directly, the opener clearing its mark on the `closed` message — closing the popout by any means (✕, ⌘W) is equivalent.
- **Per-kind behaviour** — **tty**: the popout tile's relay stream opens with `isolate: true` on the leaf's home window (`leaf.home ?? @N` — the `_rk-iso-*` attach, [tmux-sessions](/run-kit/tmux-sessions.md) § Iso Sessions), so the popout keeps its window while the opener's session switches to a sibling tab; the opener's hidden tty tile keeps the hide-never-unmount posture. **code**: the opener EVICTS its retained frame for the popped leaf's window (the popout boots its own extension host — one, not two, the ~250–320 MB-per-frame reason for the LRU; § Code Surface → Frame retention) and the popped code leaf renders no hidden tile, while the code seed/workspace hooks read the RENDERED tree (a popped code leaf neither seeds nor holds a frame in the opener; the popout's one-leaf tree drives its own seed + derivation) — popping back in re-mounts fresh, a workbench boot accepted as the cost of the return. **web**: the popout mounts the iframe engine and the page reloads in the new window (no state transfer; the native engine exists only in the desktop shell, which offers no popout). **gui**: the popout is a second RFB client (the Xvnc backend runs `-AlwaysShared`), the opener's hidden gui tile disconnects via the existing visibility gate, and `gui.geometry: auto` authority rules are unchanged.
- **Eligibility + the drag/template gate** — the header **Pop out** verb (content-verb family, ahead of the layout-verb cluster, hairline-separated; the new `PopOutGlyph` in `top-bar-icons.tsx` — a box with an arrow leaving its top-right corner, the box anchoring the source tile where `OpenExternalGlyph` is a bare arrow; Tip/aria-label `Pop out <Surface>`) and the palette rows (§ ▦ Layout chip + palette + chords) are offered only when the REDUCED render keeps ≥2 tiles (a single remaining tile gains nothing — the tab's own URL is the answer), on fine pointers, outside the desktop shell (the caller omits `onPopOut` when `isShell()` — the shell's `window.open` goes to the system browser, which shares neither localStorage nor the BroadcastChannel with the opener, so the mark would go stale while the popout still runs; native shell popout windows are the follow-up `desktop-popout-windows` change's), and for LIVE tiles only — never the away placeholder, a dead-home foreign tile, or the popped-out placeholder. While this viewer has any leaf of the layout popped, header drag-to-snap, the sidebar row-drag borrow, and the ▦ template cycle (chip + `layout-cycle` chord + `Layout: <Template>` palette rows) are ALL disabled — a drop or template resolved on the reduced render would drop the popped leaf from the SHARED layout for every viewer (§ Design Decisions → Drag and templates pause while a tile is popped); palette verbs and every other shared-layout mutation (add, close, promote, swap, send home, borrow, toggles) still operate on the full shared tree.
- **e2e — `surface-popout.spec.ts`** — real tmux on the port-3020 rig, the popup captured via `context.waitForEvent("page")`: header Pop out opens a chrome-less popout titled `<Surface> · <window>` while the opener reflows for this viewer only; closing the popout returns the tile; palette `Tile: Pop Back In` closes the popout and restores the tile; a tty popout keeps its window while the opener switches to a sibling tab and ends ("Window closed") when its window dies; a foreign `@N/tty` leaf pops out titled with its home window; and the route-entry translation keeps `?pop=` while folding the retired params. Unit coverage: `lib/popout.test.ts` (popped-set parse/serialize incl. corrupt storage, `reducePopped` over bare/duplicate-tty/foreign leaves, the all-popped → `null` case, absent ids pruned, the stale sweep at the 6 s boundary, message validation incl. wrong server/window), `router-url.test.ts` (`pop` pass-through + the empty-string drop), `palette/layout.test.ts` (the Pop rows' eligibility — arity 1, shell, placeholder, dead home, popped), `components/popout-states.test.tsx`, and the `surface-layout.test.tsx` popout block (the header verb's presence rules, the popout posture rendering only Pop back in, the placeholder, drag disarmed while popped).

### ▦ Layout chip + palette + chords (Constitution V)

- **▦ chip** (`components/layout-chip.tsx`) — terminal-route L1 right-cluster entry (`id: "layout"`, `menuGroup: "view"`; § Chrome (Top Bar) → Right cluster): click opens a popover listing `templatesFor(n)` for the current tile count, each row leading with a `LayoutTreeGlyph` mini tree glyph rendered from that template's tree (`components/top-bar-icons.tsx`), the current template marked (trailing ✓ + `aria-checked`, the macOS menu pattern); a `single` or `custom` current tree renders a disabled marked row first, so the chip reads `custom` when `templateOf` matches no template. Clicking a template applies it via `applyTemplate` → `applyLayout` (a rebuild from the current slot order — lossy for a custom tree). The popover follows the `SplitControl` direction-menu pattern (outside-mousedown closes, Escape closes + refocuses the trigger). Overflowed, its menu form is one `Layout: <Template>` `menuitemradio` row per template (`LayoutMenuRows`, current template marked).
