While this viewer has a tile of the layout popped out, the chip and its menu rows render DISABLED (Tip `Layout — unavailable while a tile is popped out`; an open popover closes on the transition, the chord hint suppressed) — a template rebuilds from the current slot order, and resolving one over the reduced render would strand the popped leaf (§ Surface popout; § Design Decisions → Drag and templates pause while a tile is popped).
- **Cycle chord** — `layout-cycle`, `⌘;` (`code: "Semicolon"`, `cmd` tier, terminal scope — free in every claimed set, not browser-reserved): the next template in `templatesFor(n)`, wrapping — a `custom` tree cycles to the first template, one tile is a no-op. The handler reuses the `Layout: Cycle Template` palette body (`fromPalette("layout-cycle")`), so the body is the Constitution V parity and `withShortcutHints` decorates the entry with the effective combo. The chip's Tip advertises the same registry-derived combo.
- **Zoom** — the transient zoom toggle (one tile full-center, the others display-hidden; above one leaf only) is driven through the app-level `layoutZoomToggleRef` seam, which toggles the FOCUSED leaf: the palette's `Layout: Expand`/`Restore` entries (exactly one form renders, the id flipping `layout-zoom`/`layout-unzoom` with the observed zoom state), the tile-header zoom verb (Tip/aria-label `Expand <label>`/`Restore <label>`), and zen mode's enter/exit body all ride it. The **⇧⌘⏎ / ⇧Ctrl+⏎ `zen-toggle` chord** (shifted tier on both platforms, `ignoreInputs`, desktop-only) toggles ZEN MODE at ANY tile count — the transient top-bar + sidebar hide (compose strip + status bar stay visible) — riding this seam only for its multi-tile focused-tile zoom ([keyboard-and-palette](/run-kit/ui/keyboard-and-palette.md) § Stateful surface chords). The remaining per-verb actions (promote/swap/close/focus) carry no direct chords; promote/swap are palette-only — the mouse rearrangement path is the header drag (§ Tile renderer → Header drag (drop to snap)) — while ✕ close keeps its header button (§ Design Decisions → Layout verbs are palette-reachable).
- **Palette** — the pure builder `buildLayoutActions(layout, availableSurfaces, opts)` (`lib/palette/layout.ts`, the `buildViewActions` precedent, colocated tests): `Tile: Show <Surface>` per available surface not open as a BARE leaf (omitted when no split of the current tree fits the size floor — `addSurface`'s refusal is the gate; a kind present only as foreign tiles still gets its row, the add landing the bare slot), `Tile: Hide <Surface>` per open bare kind (omitted at one leaf), the cross-tab rows `Tile: Bring <window> <Surface> here` (per other window on the route server × its lendable surfaces — never `gui` — floor-gated like Show, the write riding the caller's `onBring` seam — a plain apply or a borrow by held state), `Tile: Send Back to <home window>` (for a focused FOREIGN tile, disabled when the home is dead), and `Tile: Bring Back <Surface>` (the home tab's half of the return — one row per bare leaf whose kind is away to a live holder) (§ Tiles from other tabs), `Layout: Expand`/`Restore` (exactly one form renders, the id flipping `layout-zoom`/`layout-unzoom` with the observed zoom state; no shortcut hint — the entry id is not the `zen-toggle` actionId), `Layout: Promote <Surface>` per open non-slot-A kind (swaps the leaf with slot A), the popout pair `Tile: Pop Out <Surface>` per open, not-popped, LIVE leaf while the reduced render keeps ≥2 tiles (ids `tile-pop-out-*`; foreign leaves labelled with the home window's name, the `Tile: Bring … here` convention; an away bare kind and a dead-home foreign leaf have nothing to pop; the caller omits `onPopOut` on mobile/coarse pointers and in the desktop shell, so no rows render there) and `Tile: Pop Back In <Surface>` per popped leaf still in the shared tree (ids `tile-pop-in-*`; posts `pop-in` over the `rk-popout` channel and clears the viewer's mark — § Surface popout), `Layout: <Template>` jumps for the current tile count (ids `layout-template-<name>`, one per `templatesFor(n)` whose result fits the floor) and `Layout: Cycle Template` — both OMITTED while this viewer has a leaf popped (a template resolved on the reduced render would drop the popped leaf from the shared layout; omitting the cycle entry disarms the ⌘; chord for free, its handler resolving through the palette body) — and the directional `Tile: Swap Left` / `Right` / `Up` / `Down` (ids `tile-swap-left|right|up|down`) for the FOCUSED tile, each present only when a geometric neighbour exists in that direction (desktop multi-tile only — the builder needs the focused leaf id and the live leaf rects) — the `Layout:` prefix holds only true ARRANGEMENT verbs; showing/hiding/focusing one tile's renderer are `Tile:` verbs (ids `tile-show-*` / `tile-hide-*` / `tile-focus-*` — "Show/Hide" states what happens: a renderer is revealed, nothing is destroyed, and "Tile" matches the existing UI vocabulary — the overflow menu's Tiles section, the `<Label> tile` aria-labels). `Tile: Focus <Surface>` entries (one per open NON-focused kind, desktop multi-tile only — hidden at one leaf and on mobile) invoke a focus-by-kind callback that `app.tsx` routes through the `focusTileRef` seam (260812-wfic). Toggle-chord hints ride the `toggleTarget`/`toggleShortcut` pair plus the generalized `toggleHints?: Partial<Record<SurfaceKind, string>>` map — `app.tsx` feeds the effective per-kind combos (code's ⌘2, gui's ⌘4) so each surface's Show/Hide entry advertises its chord (enabled-else-`undefined`). Beside it, the pure `buildZenActions(active, opts)` (`lib/palette/zen.ts`, the same builder precedent, colocated tests) emits exactly one of `View: Enter Zen Mode` / `View: Exit Zen Mode` (ids `view-zen-enter`/`view-zen-exit`) keyed on live zen state — the `zen-toggle` chord's palette parity, offered at ANY tile count on the desktop terminal route and carrying the ⇧⌘⏎ hint via the explicit `shortcut` option (the id is not the `zen-toggle` actionId, so the hint attaches explicitly; the parity invariant's equivalence map documents the pair). The mobile `Tile: Switch to <Surface>` entries live in § Mobile slot-A + the top-bar switch group. (260816-ox16, 260820-o8cr, 260925-3v9l-surface-popout)

### Mobile slot-A + the top-bar switch group (R13)

Below `isMobileViewport()` the layout manager renders only ONE tile full-width (all surfaces stay mounted-hidden, so switching loses no state). The visible tile is `mobileActiveTile = storedZoom && leaves(layout).includes(storedZoom) ? storedZoom : leaves(layout)[0]` — the per-viewer zoom key read live (re-read after each zoom write via a small epoch state), no per-route state and no reset effect (the key is per-window already). The switch affordance is the top-bar `surface-toggles` group forked to **switch mode** (§ Surface Toggles — one ordered-registry entry, `pinned` at mobile): one button per available surface, rendered only when ≥2 surfaces exist, with RADIO semantics — the visible tile pressed, tapping the pressed button a no-op — and the same per-surface corner dots as the desktop toggles (web's dot means "has content", § Surface Toggles). Every button runs the **switch-to-tile** verb — `switchToTile(surface)` in `app.tsx` — with two arms: a target ALREADY OPEN in the tree writes ONLY the zoom key (`writeStoredZoom` — never `@rk_win_layout`), so a phone reading `web` leaves every desktop viewer's arrangement alone; a target AVAILABLE-BUT-NOT-OPEN goes through the shared growth verb `addSurface(layout, kind)` → `applyLayout` (one `@rk_win_layout` POST — the desktop grows a tile too) AND writes the zoom key to that kind so the phone shows it; when `addSurface` returns `null` (no split fits the nominal-box size floor) the switch is a no-op and the button renders disabled. The palette mirrors the group with `Tile: Switch to <Surface>` entries (Constitution V) — one per available, non-visible surface from the pure `buildTileSwitchActions(available, visible, onSwitch)` (`lib/palette/layout.ts`, colocated tests) — which take the place of the `View: <X>` entries at mobile width (`buildViewActions` output is desktop-only; desktop `View:` actions are untouched). A switch NEVER navigates and never writes localStorage layout state. (260816-ox16, iip5)

### e2e — `surface-layout.spec.ts`

Real-tmux e2e on the isolated port-3020 server (the `web-view-lens.spec.ts` `_tmux.ts` pattern): every layout assertion reads tmux — a `windowOption(id, key)` helper (`execFileSync` argument arrays, no shell; `""` on unset) via `expect.poll(() => windowOption(id, "@rk_win_layout"))` — never the URL or localStorage, and window setup stamps `@rk_win_web_1` + `@rk_win_web_active 1` (`stampWebTab`). Covers: the one-shot inbound translation (a legacy `?view=code&panel=web` deep link produces exactly one `@rk_win_layout` write and the URL is replaced with the bare route; the same link on a window whose `@rk_win_layout` is already set writes nothing and still drops the params — the shared layout wins); building a 3-tile `h(tty,v(web,code))` layout via the top-bar surface toggles (1→2 `h(tty,web)`, then 2→3 — the add splits the focused tile along its longer axis) + tile verbs and rearranging it (palette `Layout: Promote Code` and a directional `Tile: Swap Up` on the focused tile, header ✕ close — closing `web` lifts the nested split to `h(code,tty)` — and the header drag via `page.mouse` on the same mount: a center drop swaps, a tile-edge drop splits beside, a layout-edge drop spans that side at 50 %, and an Escape mid-hold cancels with no write — each commit landing exactly one option write, the URL bare throughout), with the gap-seam intersection folded into that same test — mid-seam hover lights only that sash, the junction hover lights BOTH sashes, and a diagonal drag on the `surface-divider-intersection` zone moves both `aria-valuenow`s and persists both fraction arrays (layout option untouched); a top-bar toggle POSTs the shared `@rk_win_layout` and a reload of the BARE route restores the tile set (persistence comes from tmux); internal navigation (sidebar) targets the bare route and every history entry is a bare route (layout mutations never navigate); A→B→A window-switch round-trips restore each window's own shared layout; divider-size persistence keyed per (window, structure signature) — `rk-layout-sizes:{server}:{@N}:h(0,v(1,2))` — with the sash states folded in (rest shows 3 grip dots and no fill, hover lights the pill after the ~150ms delay, the pill stays lit through the drag); the focused-tile border case asserts the dimmed `rk-card-border` on unfocused tiles; two-context sharing — a tile toggle in browser context A appears in an already-mounted context B with no interaction (the option tick repaints), while zoom in A never zooms B (the zoom key is per-viewer localStorage); the 375px mobile slot-A + switch-group branch (no dividers, no intersection zone — the chrome is desktop-only; a 3-tile window renders slot A with the radio switch group, a tap on an OPEN surface swaps the visible tile via the zoom key with NO `/options` POST and the desktop keeps both tiles, and no `mobile-surfaces-chip` testid anywhere). **Perf budget (binding)**: the plaintext e2e origin is HTTP/1.1 with a 6-slot connection pool (spec § Performance note; the board-route postmortem class) — only ONE test mounts 3 tiles, every other flow stays at ≤2 tiles. The other panel/view suites (`right-panel.spec.ts`, `code-surface.spec.ts`, `web-view-lens.spec.ts`, and companions) exercise the layout model, and `present-auto-expand.spec.ts` covers the external-`set-option` repaint end-to-end (§ Present expansion). `cross-tab-tiles.spec.ts` covers the borrow cycle on the same rig: a sidebar-row drag onto a tile edge borrows a tab's terminal (typing lands in the home window), the home tab renders the placeholder (bring back / go to / status dot / ✕), ✕ dismisses the slot and the toggle re-adds it with the away marker, ↩ sends the surface home, and killing the home window prunes the tile (§ Surface Layout → Tiles from other tabs) (constitution: every `.spec.ts` carries intent comments). (iip5, 260925-jbz0-surface-cross-tab-tiles)

## Surface Toggles (Open-Tile)

The open-tile toggles live in the TOP BAR's right cluster as ONE ordered-registry entry — `id: "surface-toggles"`, `menuGroup: "tiles"` (`components/top-bar.tsx:606`), terminal-route only (`mode === "terminal" && currentWindow`), rendered as a bordered sub-group with a divider (`data-testid="surface-toggles"`, top-bar.tsx:378) sitting LEFT of the remaining terminal chips. The slot prop carries a **mode discriminant** (`top-bar-slot-context.tsx`): desktop registers `mode: "toggle"` (the open tiles + the shared `togglePanel` mutation; the entry sits at the registry head, the first fit candidate to drop into the overflow menu, and the menu's **Tiles** section — `components/top-bar-overflow-menu.tsx`, first in `MENU_SECTIONS`; `MenuGroup = "tiles" | "view" | "window" | "app"` — carries one `role="menuitemcheckbox"` row per shown surface: leading `SURFACE_GLYPH` glyph, checked = a BARE tile open, the floor gate honored, a muted `away` suffix when the surface's slot is live in another tab); mobile registers `mode: "switch"` (the VISIBLE tile + the switch-to-tile verb — § Mobile slot-A + the top-bar switch group — gated on ≥2 available surfaces), where the entry is **`pinned`** in-bar (exempt from the probe/fit/overflow pipeline, its width reserved from the fit budget) and emits NO menu rows. The surfaces themselves render as layout tiles (§ Surface Layout). (260815-19me, 260816-ox16)

- **Open-tile toggles (TOGGLE mode, desktop)** — one focusable `Tip`-wrapped button per available surface, `tty` first. Buttons are LIT (`aria-pressed`, accent-green border/text/10% bg) for every tile open as a BARE leaf (a kind present only as foreign tiles reads as not open — its signal is the **away marker**: an amber ↩ at the button's top-left when the caller's `away` predicate — the route window's server-derived `awayIn` — says the kind's slot is live in another tab, the Tip reading `<Label> — in another tab`; toggling it on re-adds the bare slot, which renders as the away placeholder while borrowed, § Surface Layout → Tiles from other tabs), and every button's corner dot is PER-SURFACE via the caller's `showDot` predicate (`app.tsx`: web's dot means "has content" and renders only when the web-tab family is non-empty; gui's dot is VNC health — the live RFB connection state reported by the tile via `onGuiConnection`, falling back to the signal's `reachable` when no tile is open (R6); every other surface's dot is always-on; the overflow Tiles rows carry no dot) (260821-zqlq). Clicking an unlit button ADDS the surface (`addSurface` — splits the FOCUSED tile along its longer axis, gated by the per-viewport size floor with the viewer's stored sizes threaded); clicking a lit one CLOSES its first BARE tile (`closeSurface` — remove + normalise, the neighbours absorbing the size; a foreign tile is never the close target). The caller (`app.tsx`'s `togglePanel`) runs the pure mutation + the `applyLayout` option POST; a disallowed mutation (closing the last tile, or no split fitting the floor) is a `null` no-op there. While the caller's floor-derived `canAdd` is false the remaining unlit buttons render DISABLED with a "No room for another tile" tooltip (the constraint must be visible, never a silent no-op); the `Tip` wraps a span so the disabled button's tooltip still fires (disabled buttons swallow pointer events). (260925-jbz0-surface-cross-tab-tiles)
- **Switch group (SWITCH mode, mobile)** — the same component and button grammar forked by the slot prop's `mode` discriminant: lit = the VISIBLE tile (exactly one — radio semantics), tapping an unlit button runs the switch-to-tile verb (§ Mobile slot-A + the top-bar switch group), tapping the lit one is a no-op, and the disabled state rides the caller's per-surface predicate (a not-open surface whose growth breaks the size floor). The group renders only when ≥2 surfaces survive the hidden filter and is `pinned` in-bar (never overflows; no Tiles menu rows on mobile). (260816-ox16-mobile-tile-switch-vocab, 260925-jbz0-surface-cross-tab-tiles)
- **Icon glyphs, labels in tooltips** — `>_` tty, `://` web, `{}` code, `[]` gui (the shared `SURFACE_GLYPH` map in `surface-layout.ts`); the text labels live in the `Tip` tooltips and the `<Label> tile` aria-labels (e2e hooks).
- **Availability + attention dots** (right-panel P4): a collapsed or absent tile may hide content, never state that wants a human.
- **`tty` toggle** — the tty surface's button, present on every window (tty is always available).
- **`lib/right-panel.ts`** keeps the SHARED surface registry — `type SurfaceName = SurfaceKind` and `availableSurfaces(win, host?)` (`tty` first, then `code`/`web`, then `gui` when the threaded host signal's `enabled` holds, per the `window-view.ts` capability predicates) — so the toggles, the layout, and the palette read one availability source, plus the two-sibling `clampSiblingFraction` (composing `clampRatio`/`MIN_PANEL_WIDTH_PX`) for the tile dividers and `readStoredPanel`/`panelStorageKey` — the READ-only legacy `runkit-window-panel` input to the one-shot route-entry translation (nothing writes that key; absent simply means no legacy state; removed by the follow-up cleanup change). (260814-6b0j, iip5)
- **⌘1/⌘2/⌘3/⌘4 / ⇧Ctrl+1/2/3/4 (`tty-toggle` / `code-toggle` / `web-toggle` / `gui-toggle`)** — the stateful tile chords, one per surface kind in tile order ([keyboard-and-palette](/run-kit/ui/keyboard-and-palette.md) § Stateful surface chords): hidden → open via `togglePanel(kind)` + focus once the tile lands (the per-kind landing flag); visible-unfocused → focus via the `focusTileRef` seam; focused → hide via `togglePanel(kind)` + `restoreFocus` (a no-op at one leaf, mirroring the palette's `Tile: Hide` omission on single-leaf layouts). Gated on a desktop window route with the surface available — for `web` that is ALWAYS (the surface is always tileable, § Window Views), so ⌘3 mounts on every desktop window route; for the other surfaces a window without the surface mounts no handler and the chord falls through untouched (with the gui switch off, ⌘4 is inert — § Design Decisions → Stateful digit chords toggle the surface tiles; `` ⌃` `` hops tty↔code focus).
- **``⌃` `` / ``⇧Ctrl+` `` (`focus-hop`)** — hops focus between the tty and code tiles through the same focus-by-kind seam as `Tile: Focus <Surface>`, gated on a desktop window route with the code surface available; a closed-but-available code tile opens first (open-then-focus — [focus-ownership](/run-kit/ui/focus-ownership.md)).

### e2e — `right-panel.spec.ts` / `code-surface.spec.ts`

Both suites exercise the layout model on the same real-tmux port-3020 rig, driving the toggles through the `surface-toggles` testid and asserting shared state through `_tmux.ts`'s `windowOption(id, "@rk_win_layout")` rather than the URL or localStorage: toggle-button gating by capability, icon glyphs with label tooltips, toggle add/close (the add splitting the focused tile along its longer axis — each landing as one `/options` POST on `@rk_win_layout` in the tree form), disabled+tooltip when the size floor leaves no room for another tile, the two one-shot translation arms for legacy `?panel=web`/`?view=` deep links (unset option → one write + bare-route replace; set option → no write, params dropped), tile hide-never-unmount, divider drag with the same xterm instance surviving, and the 375px mobile switch group — radio semantics over a 2-tile window (slot A pressed, a tap on the open web tile switching it in via the zoom key with NO `/options` POST) and no `mobile-surfaces-chip` testid anywhere in the DOM. `code-surface.spec.ts` keeps the **stub HTTP server** on the preset port (`scripts/test-e2e.sh` exports `RK_CODE_SERVER_PORT`, default 3939, to both the dev backend and the playwright run — the preset wins over the `RK_PORT+2` convention, so the backend's `/code/*` forwards to the stub; code-server itself is not installable in the test env): the surface toggle + `View: Code` menu row appear for a `/tmp`-cwd window too — the code-server folder resolves to the raw cwd (`_tmux.ts`'s `newWindow` takes a `cwd` option for that); the code tile iframe's `src` is the stable relative `/code/?workspace=<path>` shape — `<path>` read from `GET /api/windows/<id>/code-workspace` in the test (the per-run temp `XDG_STATE_HOME` makes the absolute path run-specific), with the workspace file asserted on disk carrying the expected `folders[0].path` and `rk.tab`/`rk.server` values, the `code-surface-pending` state preceding the iframe on first open, and a deleted file regenerating on remount; the bare `/code` request 308-redirects to `/code/`; the not-running empty state renders when the stub is down; `?view=code` and `?panel=code` deep links both fold through the one-shot translation; and the keyboard-spike case — a registry chord (Ctrl+K) pressed with focus INSIDE the same-origin iframe reaches the parent and opens the palette. (iip5)

## Design Decisions

### Host signal is an argument, not a store read
**Decision**: `availableTiles(win, host?)` / `effectiveLayout(win, host?)` / `availableViews(win, host?)` / `availableSurfaces(win, host?)` take the gui host signal as an optional second argument; `app.tsx` reads `useGui()` once and threads it down.
**Why**: keeps `lib/window-view.ts` / `lib/surface-layout.ts` pure and DOM-free so the colocated unit tests stay drift-free; `ViewWindow` stays per-window because gui availability is per-host.
**Rejected**: a module-level store or context read inside the pure helpers (untestable without React); a synthetic `gui` field on `ViewWindow` (lies about ownership).
*Introduced by*: 260909-o2sp-gui-surface-tile

### Visibility, not focus, gates the RFB connection
**Decision**: the gui tile disconnects after 15 s of not-visible (zoomed away or document hidden) and reconnects on the next visible; focus loss alone never disconnects.
**Why**: noVNC has no pause API; disconnecting when hidden stops framebuffer traffic and releases the relay `viewers` count so the backend probe resumes; focus loss is constant in a multi-tile layout and must not flap the connection.
**Rejected**: disconnect on blur (flaps); keep connected while hidden (wastes bandwidth on phones, keeps the probe suppressed).
*Introduced by*: 260909-o2sp-gui-surface-tile

### The gui chord gate is same-document capture, no steal guard
**Decision**: a capture-phase keydown on the canvas wrapper stops rk chords before noVNC's canvas handler and re-dispatches them on the parent document; no `onProgrammaticFocus` seam is wired.
**Why**: the canvas is in rk's own document, so the iframe re-dispatch dance's cross-document half is unnecessary (the re-dispatch still matters — the dispatcher listens in the bubble phase); noVNC only takes focus on click (`focusOnClick`), so there is no load-time grab to guard.
**Rejected**: reusing the iframe contentDocument reclaim verbatim (no frame here); disabling noVNC's keyboard entirely (breaks the guest).
*Introduced by*: 260909-o2sp-gui-surface-tile

### A permanent pinned member reserves up front; the `⚙` keeps the two-pass reserve
**Decision**: when the capture palette row exists (fine pointers), the gui header's pinned block renders permanently and the fold call subtracts the probed capture-block width (divider + verb) from the fit budget in both layout modes, while `pinnedWidth` keeps covering only the conditional `⚙`; `lib/gui-toolbar-fold.ts`'s API is untouched.
**Why**: the capture verb is the chord gate's mouse exit — the one release path that survives a remapped-or-forgotten chord — so it must be visible at every width, folded or not; meanwhile the `⚙` stays conditional on an actual fold, and its reserve must never be what causes the fold that justifies it. Subtracting the permanent member's width up front honors both rules: the latch flip also cannot shift the measured width because the ringed latch arm paints its ring inset.
**Rejected**: folding the capture verb into the two-pass `pinnedWidth` (the block would render only while something is folded, hiding the verb exactly at full width); one unconditional reserve for the whole block (re-introduces the reserve-causes-the-fold circularity for `⚙`).
*Introduced by*: 260912-31eg-gui-keyboard-capture

### The bare-WM strip and launch rows hide on the screen-sharing backend
**Decision**: the gui tile's bare-WM strip and the palette's `GUI: Open …` rows require `backend !== "screen-sharing"` in addition to `enabled && reachable` (and `wm === ""` for the strip).
**Why**: the macOS mirror backend stamps no window manager, so `wm` is `""` there by construction and every macOS viewer would otherwise see a false "no window manager" strip; the mirror is view-only with no X display, so a launch can only miss.
**Rejected**: keying on `wm_hint` presence alone (the backend also fills it on darwin, so it does not discriminate); a backend change to stamp a sentinel (the backend's macOS branch stays untouched).
*Introduced by*: 260910-vu4p-gui-desktop-tile-strip-and-palette

### The strip's install line rides the status document, fetched once per bare transition
**Decision**: the tile fetches `GET /api/gui/host` once when the bare condition becomes true and reads `wm_hint`; the strip renders without the line (and without Copy) while it is unknown.
**Why**: the stream entry carries `wm` but not the hint; the hint is package-manager-aware wording built server-side, so the frontend must not hardcode `sudo apt …`; the once-per-transition never-poll rule is the tile's existing grammar for the unreachable reason.
**Rejected**: adding `wm_hint` to the stream entry (a backend change, and per-tick repetition of a static string); hardcoding the apt line in the frontend (wrong on dnf/pacman hosts).
*Introduced by*: 260910-vu4p-gui-desktop-tile-strip-and-palette

### The bare-WM strip is a flex sibling above the noVNC host, never an overlay
**Decision**: the strip renders as a flex-row child before the `flex-1 min-h-0` host div inside the existing `flex-col` canvas wrapper.
**Why**: noVNC observes the host div for `scaleViewport`/`resizeSession`, so the flex layout subtracts the strip from the fit and SetDesktopSize sees the reduced height without any measurement code; an overlay would hide desktop pixels behind text.
**Rejected**: an absolute-positioned banner (covers the taskbar area of the desktop); measuring the strip and passing an offset to noVNC (unnecessary — the layout already does it).
*Introduced by*: 260910-vu4p-gui-desktop-tile-strip-and-palette

### The sized host drives gui zoom; `scaleViewport` stays on at every zoom
**Decision**: a percentage zoom `z` sizes the noVNC host div to `fb.w·z/100` × `fb.h·z/100` CSS px (the framebuffer from the gui stream entry's `width`/`height`) while `scaleViewport` stays true and `clipViewport` stays false (public API only), so `Display.autoscale` — `min(w/fb.w, h/fb.h)`, no 1.0 cap — yields exactly `z/100` and `_display.scale` equals the canvas's visual scale by construction, keeping `absX`/`absY` pointer mapping exact on every step. The tile wrapper is `overflow: hidden` and pans by clamped `scrollLeft`/`scrollTop`. `resizeSession` keeps its formula at `fit` but is held false while zoomed: noVNC's ResizeObserver watches that screen div, and `_requestRemoteResize` would request the deliberately-larger scaled size as the remote desktop size — the gui-signal echo would then grow the framebuffer that feeds the host size, a runaway feedback loop under `auto`.
**Why**: `RFB._updateScale` → `Display.autoscale(_screenSize())` recomputes the same `z/100` on every container resize, so mid-zoom browser/tile resizes are self-healing with zero re-application code; `_rescale` writes only the canvas CSS `width`/`height` strings, so visual scale == `_display.scale` always (the pointer-correctness invariant). noVNC's `dragViewport` pans via `Display.viewportChangePos`, a no-op unless the display CLIPS, and `_updateClip` force-disables clipping whenever `scaleViewport` is on — under this mechanism noVNC's native drag pan moves nothing, so the tile drives its own scroll (fine pointer: a capture-swallowed mouse drag with sub-threshold click replay; coarse touch mode: a passive observing touch tracker while `dragViewport` stays on so noVNC swallows the one-finger drag as a viewport gesture instead of sending the guest a left-drag).
**Rejected**: driving `_display.scale` directly — `RFB._updateScale` writes `_display.scale = 1.0` (scaleViewport off) or re-autoscales on every noVNC resize handling inside its own requestAnimationFrame, so a directly-driven scale is reverted on each resize and must race noVNC's rAF to be re-applied; it also needs the `_display` private, which `types/novnc.d.ts` deliberately avoids. A CSS `transform: scale()` on the wrapper — the transform is invisible to `_display.scale` but visible to `getBoundingClientRect`, so `clientToElement` positions land off by the zoom factor and every mouse/touch position would need compensation before noVNC sees it — a second interception layer for fine pointers as well.
*Introduced by*: 260910-0aur-gui-zoom-and-touch-pointer

### Trackpad input reaches noVNC as synthetic DOM events, never private calls
**Decision**: the trackpad layer intercepts touches in the capture phase on the tile wrapper and dispatches synthetic `MouseEvent`/`WheelEvent`s on noVNC's canvas; it never calls `_handleMouseMove`/`_handleMouseButton`/`_sendMouse`.
**Why**: noVNC's DOM listener surface (canvas mouse/wheel events, `_convertButtonMask(ev.buttons)`, `WHEEL_STEP` accumulation) is the behavior the fine-pointer path already depends on, so the translated input is indistinguishable from a mouse and survives noVNC minors; capture-phase interception is the one seam that keeps `GestureHandler` from double-handling the same touches.
**Rejected**: calling RFB privates (`_handleMouseButton` etc.) — brittle across versions and bypasses the cursor/throttle logic; monkey-patching `_handleGesture` — same brittleness plus it still lets noVNC own tap classification.
*Introduced by*: 260910-0aur-gui-zoom-and-touch-pointer

### Pinch and rk zoom are trackpad-mode gestures; touch mode stays noVNC-native
**Decision**: pinch-to-rk-zoom and cursor-follow pan exist in `trackpad` mode; `touch` mode is a verbatim noVNC passthrough (native pinch = Ctrl+wheel to the guest, native one-finger drag pan when zoomed, native two-finger scroll).
**Why**: two gesture systems over the same touches would fight (the gui ergonomics plan's Risk 3); the coarse default is `trackpad`, which is where the acceptance ("pinch reaches 200% and pans") is measured, and keeping `touch` as an untouched passthrough is the only way it can be the guaranteed escape hatch.
**Rejected**: intercepting only pinch in touch mode — capture-phase interception is all-or-nothing per event, so swallowing the second finger desynchronizes noVNC's gesture state machine.
*Introduced by*: 260910-0aur-gui-zoom-and-touch-pointer

### The stats seam consumes noVNC's public raw-channel constructor
**Decision**: `GuiSurface` constructs the `/ws/gui/host` WebSocket and passes the object (not the URL) to `new RFB(...)`, counting bytes through its own `message` listener; fps wraps `drawImage` on the tile canvas's 2D context instance.
**Why**: both hooks are public surfaces rk already owns (its socket, its DOM node) — no global `WebSocket` monkey-patch in product code and no reach into `_sock`/`_display` privates that a noVNC upgrade can rename.
**Rejected**: reusing the perf spec's prototype-level wraps in the product — they instrument every canvas and socket on the page.
*Introduced by*: 260910-5psw-gui-quality-presets-and-stats

### Stats collection is opt-in and idle when hidden
**Decision**: the sample interval, the `drawImage` wrap, and the RTT ping exist only while `statsVisible && connected`; the `rk-gui-stats-visible` posture defaults off.
**Why**: the ergonomics plan's V-D11 — a viewer who never opens the overlay pays nothing, and the tile's default behavior is unchanged.
**Rejected**: always-on counters feeding a hidden overlay — a 5 s ping from every idle viewer for a number nobody is reading.
*Introduced by*: 260910-5psw-gui-quality-presets-and-stats

### HiDPI sizes the host div by devicePixelRatio; it never touches the framebuffer
**Decision**: `rk-gui-hidpi` divides the percentage-zoom host CSS size by `devicePixelRatio`, so `100%` maps one framebuffer pixel to one device pixel; `fit` and every server-facing value are untouched.
**Why**: noVNC owns the canvas backing store (`canvas.width` = framebuffer width) and resets it on every resize, so a fight over backing pixels is unwinnable; device-pixel-exact CSS sizing is the one client-only lever that yields crisp text at 1:1 on a 2× display.
**Rejected**: `image-rendering: pixelated` (blocky, not crisp); requesting `tile × dpr` as the desktop under `auto` (changes the server-side size).
*Introduced by*: 260910-t2lv-gui-toolbar-keybar-hidpi-sendkey

### The header fold cluster is presentational; app.tsx owns every action
**Decision**: `GuiToolbar` receives the built `GUI:` action list itself — app.tsx's memoized `buildGuiActions` output (the zen-fallback description patch included) threaded through `SurfaceLayout` as the `guiActions` prop (the cluster renders in SurfaceLayout's gui header branch; the mobile `⚙` block receives the same list through the top-bar slot's `guiToolbar` payload) — plus the state props its labels and presence need; every chip and menu row fires the `onSelect` of the row selected by stable id via `pickGuiActions`, so app.tsx still owns every action body. The component owns the fold's DOM measurement (one `ResizeObserver` + the hidden probe row); the pure `lib/gui-toolbar-fold.ts` owns the decision.
**Why**: Constitution V — the palette is the complete action registry; a chip that invokes the row's own callback object cannot drift from its row, and threading the built list (rather than the builder's callback inputs) is what lets the cluster mirror any present row with no toolbar-side logic. The pure-module/component split (the `top-bar-overflow.ts` / `crumb-collapse.ts` precedent) keeps the ladder unit-testable without a layout engine.
**Rejected**: per-chip callback props (drift risk — eight props duplicating the palette's gating); a toolbar-only copy of any row logic (two implementations to keep in step).
*Introduced by*: 260910-t2lv-gui-toolbar-keybar-hidpi-sendkey; built-list threading: 260911-abna-gui-toolbar-for-all-viewers; header-fold form: 260912-lut4-gui-toolbar-header-fold

### Fullscreen targets the gui tile; the header serves it
**Decision**: `guiFullscreen` resolves its target as the gui TILE element (query the canvas/empty node, then `.closest('[data-testid^="surface-tile-gui"]')`), so the tile header travels into fullscreen; `GuiSurface` tracks `fullscreenchange` by containment against a root ref attached to both the canvas and empty-state roots and reports flips through `onFullscreenChange`; `SurfaceLayout` holds `guiTileFullscreen`, latching the header's `⤢` and suppressing the layout-verb block while set.
**Why**: one surface and one vocabulary across every form factor — the header serves windowed, fullscreen, and (through the top-bar `⚙` block) mobile; the listener already lives in `GuiSurface`, and the header (a sibling rendered by `SurfaceLayout`) needs the state, so the callback prop is the smallest seam. The wrapper stays a flex child of the fullscreened tile, so the fit math and the `keyboardLock()` chaining are untouched.
**Rejected**: a separate fullscreen-only toolbar surface (two vocabularies, and a second hide-timer machine for one case); a second `fullscreenchange` listener in `SurfaceLayout` matching by `data-testid` (two sources of truth for one DOM fact).
*Introduced by*: 260912-lut4-gui-toolbar-header-fold

### A divider drag edits exactly two sibling fractions
**Decision**: divider geometry derives from the tree via `layoutDividers` — one divider per adjacent sibling pair of every split, plus the intersection points where a divider's end meets a perpendicular divider. A drag changes only its two adjacent siblings' fractions (their combined share constant), clamped by the two-sibling `clampSiblingFraction` in `lib/right-panel.ts` (composing `clampRatio`'s `MIN_PANEL_WIDTH_PX` 280px floor per side on the divider's own axis); an intersection drag moves both adjacent pairs, each clamped on its own axis with no term from the other.
**Why**: sizes live per split, so no cross-split coupling exists — a sibling pair's boundary is bounded only by its own floor, and the floor math stays in one directly unit-testable pure helper beside `clampRatio`. Persisting sizes per (window, structure signature) keeps them with positions across leaf swaps.
**Rejected**: chaining neighbor fractions as bounds across a split (couples axes on nested trees — a column split's floor would pin a row seam); per-call-site ad-hoc clamps (splits one invariant across files).
*Introduced by*: 260925-ww92-surface-layout-tree

### Pointer capture, not HTML5 drag-and-drop
**Decision**: The header drag uses pointer events with `setPointerCapture` and window-level move/up listeners gated on the pointer id.
**Why**: Moves keep arriving over iframes (HTML5 DnD does not deliver them), engines can drop element capture over iframe content, and Playwright drives `page.mouse` reliably.
**Rejected**: HTML5 drag-and-drop — iframe dead zones and unreliable Playwright simulation. (The sidebar row-drag borrow is the deliberate exception — the row's drag already IS HTML5, so the drop-catcher overlay consumes it rather than fighting it; § Row borrow reuses the HTML5 window-drag.)
*Introduced by*: 260925-c1ql-surface-drag-snap

### The overlay previews the result
**Decision**: The drag overlay draws the whole resolved tree at the viewer's sizes, not a half-tile highlight.
**Why**: A drop can merge into a parent split or collapse the dragged tile's old parent, reshaping siblings; only the result is honest.
**Rejected**: VS-Code-style half-tile highlight — lies whenever siblings reshape.
*Introduced by*: 260925-c1ql-surface-drag-snap

### Draft tabs are viewer-local, not a stored sentinel
**Decision**: The empty-tab flow is client-side draft state that materializes into a real slot on first Enter; nothing lands in tmux until an address exists.
**Why**: A stored blank fights three spec decisions at once — identity-is-the-URL (two blanks collide with idempotent add), "absent and empty read alike as unset" (an empty option value cannot exist), and "declared only". The One Rule classes drafts as viewer preferences (the compose-draft class).
**Rejected**: An `about:blank`/`/newtab` sentinel URL — needs an idempotence exemption and makes blanks shared state with no shared content.
*Introduced by*: 260901-s36e-web-tab-strip-drafts-reorder

### Progress line is an absolute overlay, not an in-flow strip
**Decision**: The tty tile's 2px progress line renders inside a zero-height `relative` wrapper with an `absolute`, `pointer-events: none` 2px bar overlaying the content's top edge.
**Why**: The tile layout must not shift when progress appears, and — stronger — an in-flow strip would change the terminal container's height, firing `ResizeObserver → fit → PTY resize` churn on every task start/stop, which the window-switch machinery treats as a hard constraint (transform-only, no layout change).
**Rejected**: The web tile's in-flow `.rk-web-progress` form (fine over an iframe whose element's presence IS the loading state, resize-churn over xterm); reserving a permanent 2px row (burns 2px on every idle tile).
*Introduced by*: 260819-1vxq-terminal-tile-progress

### Progress slots are keyed per tile window
**Decision**: `SurfaceLayout` holds one progress slot PER WINDOW — the tile's own window, a foreign tty tile's home — each fed by `onProgressChange` on every tty mount of that window; the chip and line render on that window's tile.
**Why**: a borrowed terminal is a different window — folding its progress into the route window's slot would misattribute it; wiring every mount keeps the signal alive when a primary tile is hidden (hide-never-unmount keeps its stream open), and same-window duplicate firings fold idempotently under the rAF coalescing.
**Rejected**: one shared route-window slot (misattributes a borrowed terminal's progress); per-tile state for same-window tiles (duplication with no benefit).
*Introduced by*: 260819-1vxq-terminal-tile-progress; per-window keying: 260925-jbz0-surface-cross-tab-tiles

### Heading identifies the window, not the lens
**Decision**: the terminal-route center heading is a static `Tab: <name>` in every lens (tty/web/code/gui); lens identity is carried by the tile content and the top bar's lit tile toggles, not the heading.
**Why**: the heading identifies the WINDOW (the substrate), per window-views.md "rows are substrates, views are lenses". Keeping the prefix constant also stops the name's anchor from jumping on a lens switch (the prefix width does not change with the lens).
**Rejected**: a lens-following heading ("the center page heading follows the lens") — the prefix would flip on every lens switch and misidentify what the heading names.
*Introduced by*: 260714-uco1-topbar-heading-anchor-nav; static `Tab:` in every lens: 260820-lfla

### Two verb families on tile headers
**Decision**: Tile-header verbs split into layout verbs (generic, count-gated — zoom and ✕ close; rearrangement rides the header drag, § Tile renderer → Header drag (drop to snap)) and content verbs (per-kind, any count, zoom-visible) — the tty tile carries a bordered pane segment holding Split H / Split V / Close Pane.
**Why**: Pane operations act on the tmux pane inside the tty tile; proximity is the disambiguation. The tile header is already per-kind on its left side (StatusDot, meta chips), so per-kind verbs on the right are an evolution, not a break.
**Rejected**: Keeping pane verbs in the top bar (target invisible on multi-tile layouts); moving only the splits; a confirm-on-close variant — the glyph+grouping distinction below was chosen.
*Introduced by*: 260813-w1lf-terminal-tile-content-verbs

### Page title crosses the component seam via a callback, kind stays derived
**Decision**: `IframeWindow` reports `{ title }` through an optional `onPageMeta` prop, fed by the ACTIVE engine's reported `FrameChromeState.title`; `SurfaceLayout` holds it as state for the header. The kind badge is derived purely from the active web tab's address via `lib/web-url.ts` with no seam.
**Why**: the header is SurfaceLayout's render but only the engine can read its content's title; a narrow optional callback matches the existing `onInteract`/`onFolderNavigated` seam shapes, and deriving the badge purely keeps it testable and SSE-consistent.
**Rejected**: rendering a second header inside `IframeWindow` (duplicates the tile-header contract) or lifting the engine's element ref up (breaks the presentational-component boundary — the engine handle deliberately exposes no element).
*Introduced by*: 260819-v6y4-web-tile-browser-chrome

### Dead-port detection is a frontend same-origin fetch, not a proxy ErrorHandler
**Decision**: a dead proxied port is detected by the iframe engine `fetch`ing the proxied path and reading the reverse proxy's 502, reported as `tileError` and rendered by the chrome.
**Why**: the proxy already answers 502 on connect failure (the default `ReverseProxy` error path); a same-origin fetch reads it with zero backend change and keeps `frame-check` scoped to absolute external URLs only.
**Rejected**: a custom `ErrorHandler` serving a styled error page from `proxy.go` — touches the proxy layer and couples error UI to the Go layer.
*Introduced by*: 260819-v6y4-web-tile-browser-chrome

### Error states render over a hidden, still-mounted iframe
**Decision**: the chrome's error surfaces render in place of the ACTIVE frame's VISIBLE area while the engine keeps its iframe mounted under the `hidden` class.
**Why**: unmounting would strand the attach seam's mount-once listeners, forcing a remount dance on Retry; `hidden` satisfies "replace the iframe render" visually with zero seam churn.
**Rejected**: conditional unmount of the iframe — breaks the mount-once attach effect and complicates Retry.
*Introduced by*: 260819-v6y4-web-tile-browser-chrome

### `SurfaceLayout` owns zoom/drag/mount-once; `app.tsx` owns layout state
**Decision**: the tile renderer receives the derived layout as a prop and owns only per-viewer interaction state (zoom — persisted via the `rk-layout-zoom:` key, the in-flight divider drag, the ever-opened mount bookkeeping, and the per-server code-frame retention LRU); layout derivation from the payload, the optimistic `pendingLayout` overlay, and the `@rk_win_layout` write live in `app.tsx` behind the single `applyLayout` mutation path.
**Why**: layout state is route-integrated (the payload feed, the one-shot translation effect, navigation seams) and belongs beside the lens machinery; zoom and drag are self-contained viewer postures with no tmux footprint. Prop-in/callback-out composition keeps the renderer free of the router/storage import graph — tile chrome, verbs, and hide-never-unmount are unit-testable in jsdom — and new surface kinds slot in without touching the shell.
**Rejected**: the renderer importing storage/router helpers and owning layout derivation — two sources of truth for the layout (component + route) and untestable without mocking the router.
*Introduced by*: 260811-2r1w-right-panel-shell-web-surface (as the panel shell's contract); generalized to the tile renderer: 260812-ab5v-surface-layout-core; option-driven derivation: 260828-iip5-ui-state-frontend-layout-code-root

### Zoom key stores a leaf id
**Decision**: `rk-layout-zoom:{server}:{@N}` holds a leaf id — a bare unique kind (`tty`, `web`, …), a duplicate-tty occurrence id (`tty#2`), or a foreign leaf's address (`@12/tty`). Desktop zoom targets that leaf, an exact id that left the tree resolving to its kind's first leaf in reading order; the mobile single-tile switch reads the same key — a bare kind doubles as its first leaf's id, so the kind-addressed switch group's writes stay valid under this shape.
**Why**: with foreign leaves a kind no longer names a tile (two windows' `tty` tiles coexist); the leaf id is the tile's identity everywhere else (focus memory, tile keys). A phone's tile choice and a desktop's zoom are the same viewer posture at different widths, so one key serves both.
**Rejected**: a positional index (survives a reorder pointing at the wrong tile); a second address-shaped key (two zoom stores to reconcile).
*Introduced by*: 260828-iip5-ui-state-frontend-layout-code-root (kind-keyed); leaf-id form: 260925-jbz0-surface-cross-tab-tiles

### Mobile switch to a not-open surface grows the shared layout
**Decision**: `switchToTile(kind)` for a kind not in the layout runs `addSurface` through `applyLayout` (one `@rk_win_layout` POST) and sets the local zoom key; `null` growth (no split fits the nominal-box size floor) is a disabled no-op. Switching to an already-open surface writes only the zoom key.
**Why**: the spec names the shared `--add` mutation for this case, and the one-rule demands a phone posture never destroy shared tab state — a not-open switch must grow the arrangement, not collapse it.
**Rejected**: the bare-leaf collapse — it rewrote every desktop viewer's arrangement from a phone; a transient swap for the not-open arm — it breaks code-root seeding (keyed on `leaves(layout).includes("code")`) and drops the persistence an explicit pick implies.
*Introduced by*: 260828-iip5-ui-state-frontend-layout-code-root

### Code-surface availability gates on stable signals; reachability gates content
**Decision**: `hasCode` — the availability gate for the `code` lens/tile — is stable signals only: `(win?.codeRoot || win?.gitRoot)` non-empty — the shared `@rk_win_code_root` OR a non-empty live derivation (the git toplevel when found, else the pane's raw cwd) (the code-server port is always resolvable by convention — preset `RK_CODE_SERVER_PORT` else `RK_PORT+2` — so a configured-port leg would be vacuous). The TTL-cached reachability probe never gates availability; it selects the surface's CONTENT (live iframe when up, the `code-server not running — check rk doctor` empty state when down).
**Why**: reachability fluctuates with code-server restarts — gating the surface toggle / switcher segment on it would strobe the affordances, and the not-running state could never render (the affordance would vanish exactly when its message is needed). The live derivation fluctuates too, with the ACTIVE PANE's cwd: a window whose editor is open would lose its surface toggle, switcher segment, and deep-link validity the moment an agent `cd`s out of the repo. The seeded `@rk_win_code_root` is what makes the signal genuinely stable per window; content states may fluctuate.
**Rejected**: availability = configured ∧ reachable (internally contradictory with the not-running state); availability = gitRoot ∧ port-configured (vacuous once the port resolves by convention); derivation-only availability (strobes with the terminal's cwd).
*Introduced by*: 260811-k3vp-right-panel-code-lens; gitRoot-only gate: 260811-a2bo-daemon-code-server-stable-route; seeded code root: 260828-iip5-ui-state-frontend-layout-code-root

### Web availability is unconditional; `hasWebUrl` selects the content
**Decision**: the `web` lens/tile is ALWAYS available (like `tty`) on every window; `hasWebUrl` selects the tile's CONTENT — an empty web-tab family renders the onboarding state (reduced live URL bar + the fill-path panel), a non-empty active tab renders the live iframe — exactly as code splits availability from reachability.
**Why**: a single `hasWebUrl` availability predicate gated six consumer surfaces (the ⌘3 chord, the top-bar toggle, palette `View:`/`Tile:` entries, the mobile switch group, deep-link degradation), making the tile undiscoverable before initialization; the availability-vs-content split is the codebase's named model for "the lens exists but has nothing to show", and the onboarding state doubles as the discoverability surface (its live address bar is itself an init path).
**Rejected**: a ⌘3-only carve-out (a chord opening a tile the availability model says doesn't exist — the layout resolver would drop the tile anyway, so the resolver, toggles, and palette would each need contradictory special-cases); a modal/tooltip explainer (no live address bar, no automatic flip).
*Introduced by*: 260821-zqlq-web-tile-always-tileable-onboarding

### The web toggle dot means "has content"
**Decision**: the web toggle button always renders (the surface is always available); its corner dot is driven by a per-surface `showDot` predicate (`app.tsx`: `surface !== "web" || hasWebUrl(win)`) — web's dot means "has content"; every other surface's dot stays always-on; the overflow Tiles rows carry no dot.
**Why**: the button's constant presence is the discoverability affordance; the dot carries the at-a-glance "something to see" signal that a conditionally-present button would otherwise convey.
**Rejected**: a dotless always-on web button (loses the content signal); hiding the button until content exists (recreates the discoverability hole).
*Introduced by*: 260821-zqlq-web-tile-always-tileable-onboarding

### The code surface is a lean `CodeSurface`, not an `IframeWindow` reuse
**Decision**: the `code` lens/surface renders a minimal component — an iframe of the derived `/code/?workspace=<path>` URL (the `?folder=` form survives only as the degrade path and the editor's own File > Open Folder navigation) plus the not-running empty and pending states — while `web` uses `IframeWindow` with its URL bar and refresh chrome.
**Why**: the code-server URL is fully derived (the stable `/code/` path plus the code root — no port, no editable address), so the URL bar (the web tab's substrate state — a shared, POSTed content address) is meaningless; reusing `IframeWindow` would drag inapplicable chrome and substrate semantics into a surface whose content address nobody edits. The relative-path discipline (`toProxySrc`'s convention — never compose an absolute origin) is the one piece reused.
**Rejected**: `IframeWindow` with a hidden URL bar (dead chrome, plus web-tab-address semantics that do not apply to a derived URL).
*Introduced by*: 260811-k3vp-right-panel-code-lens; stable `/code/` src: 260811-a2bo-daemon-code-server-stable-route

### Chord-reclaim via a capture-phase iframe `contentDocument` listener
**Decision**: Both iframe renderers (`CodeSurface`, and the web tile's `iframe` engine `WebFrameIframe`) attach a capture-phase `keydown` listener to the same-origin iframe's `contentDocument` after every load (re-attached on each navigation, removed on cleanup, `contentDocument` access try/catch-guarded), reclaim exactly the events matching an injected `shouldReclaimChord` predicate — one curried factory in `app.tsx` (`reclaimChordForKind`) binds the registry's pure, kind-aware `hasReclaimableMatch` per surface kind; it carves out keydowns whose only matches are `ttyOnly` bindings and gates `webOnly` matches to the web kind — and re-dispatch a synthetic bubbling `KeyboardEvent` on the parent DOCUMENT (the web engine through the contract module's shared `redispatchChord` helper, which exists so every engine re-dispatches identically).
**Why**: the `/proxy/` embed is same-origin by design, so a focused iframe's key events are reachable before the embedded app's keybinding service sees them; dispatching on the parent document reaches both document-level listeners (the palette's chord) and window-level ones (the keybinding dispatcher) in one event. Injecting the predicate keeps both components free of the registry import graph, one factory keeps the two kinds reading one registry, and reclaiming only REGISTRY chords leaves the embedded/framed app's own Ctrl/⌘ bindings working.
**Rejected**: dispatching on `window` (misses document-level listeners); reclaiming every chord (breaks VS Code's own bindings); shipping without reclaim (click-out-only escape leaves ⌘K dead whenever the editor has focus).
*Introduced by*: 260811-k3vp-right-panel-code-lens; shared with the web tile + kind-aware predicate: 260819-ie2i-web-tile-keyboard-find

### The find-bar open seam is a document CustomEvent
**Decision**: each find consumer's chord handler and palette body dispatch ONE document CustomEvent — `web-find:open` (`WEB_FIND_OPEN_EVENT`), received by the mounted `IframeWindow`, and `terminal-find:open` (`TERMINAL_FIND_OPEN_EVENT`, `lib/terminal-find.ts`), received by the mounted `SurfaceLayout` (the `window-heading:rename` / `theme-selector:open` precedent). The ⌕ buttons toggle locally.
**Why**: `IframeWindow` is presentational and the web find bar's state belongs inside it, and the tty bar's state belongs to the tile layer — a CustomEvent avoids threading an imperative ref through `SurfaceLayout` (or deeper) for one action; only one web tile exists per layout (kinds don't repeat except tty) and at most one terminal route's `SurfaceLayout` is mounted, so neither receiver is ambiguous — the ONE `IframeWindow` stays the single receiver however many tab frames it hosts, acting on the ACTIVE frame.
**Rejected**: a `findBarRef`/`openFindRef` threaded app.tsx → SurfaceLayout → consumer (touched signatures for one opener); lifting find state into app.tsx (drags search state into the shell for no consumer).
*Introduced by*: 260819-ie2i-web-tile-keyboard-find; tty seam: 260819-zqf9-terminal-tile-find

### Highlight styling enters the frame as a `<style>` element, not injected script
**Decision**: the engine registers Highlight objects on the frame window's `CSS.highlights` and appends one `<style>` with `::highlight(rk-find)` / `::highlight(rk-find-active)` rules to the frame's head, removed/replaced on reset.
**Why**: `::highlight()` pseudo-elements must be styled in the document that owns the ranges; a style element is inert DOM (no execution), which honors the no-script-injection constraint while keeping the search fully parent-driven.
**Rejected**: `window.find()` as the primary (no match count, engine-inconsistent); wrapping matches in `<mark>` elements (mutates the framed page's DOM structure — breaks pages that read their own DOM, and unwinding on reset is error-prone).
*Introduced by*: 260819-ie2i-web-tile-keyboard-find

### Stateful digit chords toggle the surface tiles; `` ⌃` `` hops tty↔code focus
**Decision**: the tile chords are stateful three-state handlers (show+focus / focus / hide+restore) keyed on the CURRENT render layout and focused kind — one positional digit per surface kind (⌘1 tty / ⌘2 code / ⌘3 web / ⌘4 gui, ⇧Ctrl+digit on Win/Linux) riding `togglePanel`, the per-kind landing flag, the `focusTileRef` seam, and the restore router, with the hide arm a no-op at one leaf; each chord-target surface's `Tile: Show/Hide <Surface>` palette entry carries its chord's hint (the `toggleTarget`/`toggleShortcut` pair generalized by the `toggleHints` seam) so it stays discoverable. The sibling `focus-hop` chord hops focus between the tty and code tiles through the `Tile: Focus <Surface>` focus-by-kind seam, opening a closed-but-available code tile first (open-then-focus — VS Code's ⌃`-opens-the-hidden-panel analog).
**Why**: the JetBrains tool-window model gives every surface both verbs on ONE chord — a pure toggle cannot express "put my cursor there", and a kind-fixed positional digit gives each surface a stable identity (toggling needs a stable target). Statefulness lives in the chord handlers only — the palette entries keep explicit single-verb semantics ([keyboard-and-palette](/run-kit/ui/keyboard-and-palette.md) § Design Decisions → Statefulness lives in chord handlers, not palette bodies).
**Rejected**: a generic first-non-tty toggle (a surface picker with no stable target across capability sets, and no chord for the hop); separate toggle and focus chord layers (double the keycap spend); making `togglePanel` itself stateful (it backs the top-bar toggles and menu checkboxes, whose semantics must stay pure toggle); no-op when the code tile is closed (deads the hop in the shipped bare-`tty` default layout — VS Code's ⌃` opens the hidden panel, so the borrowed gesture includes the open half).
*Introduced by*: 260811-k3vp-right-panel-code-lens; retargeted to tiles: 260812-ab5v-surface-layout-core; stateful digit family: 260819-qwr7-surface-focus-chords

### Surface toggles are one ordered-registry group in the top bar
**Decision**: The open-tile toggles live in the top bar's right cluster as the single `surface-toggles` ordered-registry entry (`menuGroup: "tiles"`, terminal-route only) — a bordered sub-group with a divider, left of the remaining terminal chips, registered at the head so it is the first fit candidate to drop into the overflow menu; the menu's Tiles section mirrors the group as one `menuitemcheckbox` row per shown surface. Click routing runs through the caller's `togglePanel` semantics unchanged.
**Why**: A dedicated side column forks the stage grid template for one route's worth of four small buttons; as a registry entry the toggles inherit the top bar's probe/fit/overflow machinery for free, and the Tiles section keeps every toggle reachable at narrow widths. Grouping them as ONE entry (not four independent chips) preserves their internal order and divider treatment through the fit pipeline.
**Rejected**: A dedicated collapsible rail column in the stage grid (a whole grid track, a persisted collapse pref, and a top-bar toggle chip to serve four small buttons); `menuOnly` toggles (the primary add/close affordance hidden behind the overflow menu at every width).
*Introduced by*: 260815-19me-composed-frame-unification

### The layout is a canonical tree; presets are templates
**Decision**: `@rk_win_layout` stores a canonical split tree (≥2 children per split, alternating directions). The six templates (`row`, `col`, `main-left|right|top|bottom`) generate trees from slot order, and legacy preset strings parse into their trees permanently.
**Why**: arrangements grow 2/6/22/90/394 for N = 2…6, so presets cannot model N > 3, and the generic drop edit (drag to snap) needs a tree. Canonical form gives exactly one encoding per arrangement.
**Rejected**: extending the preset list (correct only to N = 3); an unconstrained tree (unary nodes, stored sizes, many encodings per arrangement).
*Introduced by*: 260925-ww92-surface-layout-tree

### Leaves render flat, positioned from rects
**Decision**: tiles are one flat keyed list of absolutely positioned elements placed from `layoutRects`, not nested split containers.
**Why**: re-parenting an iframe reloads it, so a nested DOM would reload code/web tiles on every restructure.
**Rejected**: a recursive nested flex/grid DOM that mirrors the tree.
*Introduced by*: 260925-ww92-surface-layout-tree

### Add splits the focused tile, gated by the size floor
**Decision**: add splits the FOCUSED tile along its longer axis (ties → horizontal; no focus ⇒ the last leaf in reading order), the new leaf landing after it, using real rects on desktop and the nominal 1600×1000 box on mobile and in Go; when the result breaks the 150×100 px per-tile floor in the caller's box it retries the remaining tiles largest-first and refuses only when no split fits. The floor gates OFFERS only (add, drop, template, toggle, palette rows) — a stored tree under the floor renders as-is.
**Why**: the focused tile is where the user's attention is; a tile-count cap cannot see viewport size, while the floor is the actual usability constraint, so growth past three tiles is an ordinary state. Go's `layoutspec.Add` keeps the last-leaf nominal-box rule (no focus, no viewport), keeping CLI growth deterministic.
**Rejected**: a fixed tile cap (blind to viewport size); per-viewer degradation of stored trees (a wider viewer's tree must never shrink for a narrower one); split-focused-then-refocus (TS and Go diverge).
*Introduced by*: 260925-ww92-surface-layout-tree; focused split + floor gate: 260925-jbz0-surface-cross-tab-tiles

### A surface is live in one place; away is derived
**Decision**: borrowing writes only the holder's layout (the `@A/<kind>` leaf); the home keeps its slot, rendered as a placeholder while a server-derived `awayIn` (kind → holder window id, computed from every window's stored layout per fetch) names a holder.
**Why**: one live instance avoids relay-pointer fights and duplicate ~300 MB code-server extension hosts; deriving away state from layouts keeps Constitution II — nothing new is stored.
**Rejected**: tmux `join-pane` (kills a single-pane home, changes what plain-tmux users see, needs a stored home); mirroring the surface live in both tabs.
*Introduced by*: 260925-jbz0-surface-cross-tab-tiles

### Row borrow reuses the HTML5 window-drag
**Decision**: the sidebar row's existing HTML5 drag drives the borrow onto a layout tile via a drop-catcher overlay reusing `zoneAt`/`rootZoneAt`/`resolveDrop` (external-leaf mode) and the result preview; the tile-header drag stays pointer-based.
**Why**: rows already carry `WINDOW_DRAG_MIME` with the server and window id; a second pointer-based drag on the row would fight the native `draggable` and its reorder / session-move / board-pin consumers.
**Rejected**: a pointer-captured row drag (the handoff with native DnD is fragile).
*Introduced by*: 260925-jbz0-surface-cross-tab-tiles

### Two-tab writes go through server-recomputed endpoints
**Decision**: re-borrow and send-home are `POST /api/layout/borrow` / `POST /api/layout/return`, which recompute the other tab's tree from current tmux state and chain both `set-option`s in one invocation; plain layout writes reject an already-held foreign leaf (409 naming the holder).
**Why**: a single chained invocation means no viewer observes a surface live in two tabs or none; server recompute prevents a stale client from clobbering the other tab's tree.
**Rejected**: client-computed two-tree writes (a stale client clobbers); silent steal on plain writes.
*Introduced by*: 260925-jbz0-surface-cross-tab-tiles

### Writers always emit the tree form
**Decision**: every writer serializes the tree form, and there is no one-release preset-string fallback.
**Why**: frontend and backend ship in one binary; a downgrade renders a tree value as `tty` without rewriting the option, so nothing is corrupted.
**Rejected**: writing the preset string when a tree matches one, which adds a second serializer path to remove later.
*Introduced by*: 260925-ww92-surface-layout-tree

### Layout helpers live in pure modules; the panel module keeps the divider clamp
**Decision**: `lib/layout-tree.ts` owns the tree model (parse/serialize, canonical validation, normalise/remove/insert/swap, rects and divider geometry, the template registry) as a pure DOM-free module with colocated tests and the shared TS↔Go fixture table; `lib/surface-layout.ts` owns `effectiveLayout` + degradation, the legacy-param mapper and translation decision, per-viewer sizes/zoom storage, and the verbs, re-exporting the tree API; `lib/right-panel.ts` keeps the divider clamp and the shared registry (`tty` first).
**Why**: mirrors the shipped `window-view.ts` pure-helper pattern (unit-testable, drift-free single source) and minimizes churn in shipped, tested code; one registry (`SurfaceName = SurfaceKind`) keeps the toggles, the layout, and the palette availability from drifting; the tree/verbs split keeps the N-generic model free of app concerns (storage, degradation, availability).
**Rejected**: rewriting `right-panel.ts` wholesale into `surface-layout.ts` — bigger diff, no behavioral gain.
*Introduced by*: 260812-ab5v-surface-layout-core; tree split: 260925-ww92-surface-layout-tree

### Layout verbs are palette-reachable; zoom and the template cycle carry the direct chords
**Decision**: promote and swap ship palette-only (the mouse path is the header drag — § Tile renderer → Header drag (drop to snap)); ✕ close and zoom ship as the tile header's boxed, rest-visible layout buttons; none carries a direct keybinding. The direct-bound layout chords are `layout-cycle` ⌘; (the next template for the tile count) and `zen-toggle` ⇧⌘⏎ (the full zen toggle — a transient top-bar + sidebar hide at ANY tile count, riding the `layoutZoomToggleRef` seam only for its multi-tile focused-tile zoom, desktop-only). Further per-verb direct bindings remain open to a later phase if palette latency proves irritating.
**Why**: a small chord set plus palette rows covers keyboard-first (Constitution V — every action is keyboard-reachable) with far less chord-surface to defend against collisions and to learn; the cycle chord earns its binding because it is the tmux `next-layout` muscle memory, and zen earns its own because ⇧⌘⏎ is the iTerm2 maximize-pane habit ([keyboard-and-palette](/run-kit/ui/keyboard-and-palette.md) § Design Decisions → Zen is a transient render-time override, bound as a shifted-tier Enter chord). `` Ctrl+` `` stays the `focus-hop` keycap (and collides with code-server's own integrated-terminal toggle inside the code tile), so it was never a zoom candidate.
**Rejected**: direct-binding all four verb families (per-verb + directional-swap chords) — a large chord-surface commitment before the verbs have proven their usage frequency.
*Introduced by*: 260812-ab5v-surface-layout-core; zen chord: 260819-qwr7-surface-focus-chords

### Focused leaf owned by SurfaceLayout, kind mirrored to app.tsx
**Decision**: The focused **leaf id** is `SurfaceLayout` component state; `app.tsx` receives the focused **kind** via `onFocusedKindChange` (and the leaf id via `onFocusedLeafChange`) and triggers focus via a registered ref (`focusTileRef`, the `zoomToggleRef` pattern — first leaf of the kind).
**Why**: the per-window reset lives in the component's `[server, windowId]` reset effect (the same seam that resets zoom); the `ttyOnly` gate and most palette rows only need the kind; duplicate-tty highlight and the directional swaps need the leaf, which only the component knows.
**Rejected**: Lifting the leaf into `app.tsx` — it would need manual reset on window switches and layout mutations, re-deriving what the component's reset effect already guarantees.
*Introduced by*: 260812-wfic-surface-titlebar-redesign-shortcut-scoping; leaf-id keying: 260925-ww92-surface-layout-tree

### Chord scope gates at handler presence, not inside the dispatcher
**Decision**: The `ttyOnly` shortcut gate works by making the handler `undefined` in the `app.tsx` handler map when the tty tile is unfocused, keyed off the registry's `ttyOnly` data flag.
**Why**: "Scope is descriptive; handler presence gates" is the established dispatcher contract — a match with no handler falls through without `preventDefault` for free, so the chord reaches the focused surface (e.g. code-server's own ⌘D).
**Rejected**: Teaching `useKeybindingDispatch` about tile focus — the dispatcher is deliberately generic and route-agnostic.
*Introduced by*: 260812-wfic-surface-titlebar-redesign-shortcut-scoping

### No terminal-route tile-focus cycling on ⌘[/⌘]
**Decision**: Boards cycle pane focus on `board-cycle-prev`/`board-cycle-next` (⌘[/⌘], scope `board`); the terminal route has NO tile-focus cycling chord — it is a backlog item, not shipped behavior.
**Why**: a terminal-scoped ⌘[/⌘] pair shadows macOS history navigation and the usage need is unproven. The focused-slot state and the `Tile: Focus <Surface>` palette entries exist, so the chord is cheap to add on that focus seam once tile focus proves its usage.
**Rejected**: Shipping the chord pair alongside the demotions — the macOS history-nav shadowing tradeoff deserves its own decision once tile focus proves its usage.
*Introduced by*: 260812-0c6o-retire-view-rows-demote-chat

### Derivation seeds the code root once; the editor or an explicit user verb moves it
**Decision**: The code surface's folder is the shared per-window tmux option `@rk_win_code_root`. The backend's live `gitRoot` derivation seeds it exactly once — at the code surface's first actual render for that window — and the only writers afterward are the editor's own navigation (reported back from the iframe's `load` event) and the explicit, drift-gated Follow terminal verb (§ The code root). The terminal never moves the editor on its own.
**Why**: `gitRoot` follows the ACTIVE pane's cwd and agents `cd` constantly, so a live folder makes the terminal's movement move the editor: a pane leaving every repo unmounts the tile, and two panes in two repos swap the workbench between them — either way the editor's in-flight state (open tabs, dirty buffers, undo stacks) is expensive or impossible to recover. That defeats hide-never-unmount (P3), which exists precisely so the iframes keep their in-memory state. A window option makes the folder the tab's choice rather than the terminal's — and every viewer of the tab sees the same folder, a snapshot restore carries it, and no viewer-local store needs migrating (the one-shot route-entry effect folds any surviving per-browser latch into the option once).
**Rejected**: Stabilizing the backend derivation (first-pane-with-a-root wins) — still moves the editor whenever panes `cd`, so it is a mitigation, not a guarantee; re-deriving on tile close/reopen (seed only while open) — reopening after a `cd` is exactly when the editor must come back where it was; liveness probing a stale root — code-server's own "folder does not exist" state plus the explicit follow surfaces (File > Open Folder, the drift-gated verb, `rk tab code set`) are the escape.
*Introduced by*: 260813-if5d-latch-code-surface-folder; shared `@rk_win_code_root`: 260828-iip5-ui-state-frontend-layout-code-root; the Follow-terminal writer: 260921-c5w2-code-tile-follow-reload-verbs

### Iframe src fixed per mount generation, never a parent `src` rewrite
**Decision**: `CodeSurface` computes its iframe `src` once per iframe mount generation — a ref keyed to the `reachable` gate that mounts the iframe — not reactively from the folder prop; a mounted frame is parent-navigated only through the two sanctioned re-navigation mechanisms (the nonce-keyed `followSrc` and `contentWindow.location.reload()` — § Design Decisions → Two sanctioned re-navigation mechanisms, four triggers), neither of which rewrites the `src` ref of a live frame.
**Why**: P3 (hide, never unmount): React re-rendering a changed `src` attribute re-navigates a live frame, even to a URL the frame is already at, which would reload the editor on the very code-root follow write the editor just caused. Keying the computation to mount generation means a reachability `false→true` flip or an eviction + re-creation under the frame LRU picks up the CURRENT workspace URL (fresh workbench, correct folder) while a mounted frame is never parent-navigated outside the sanctioned re-navigations — a window switch alone is not a generation boundary: a retained frame's `reachable`/`src` props don't change on show/hide (§ Design Decisions → Code frames are retained across window switches by an LRU inside `SurfaceLayout`). A ref rather than `useMemo` because a memo cache is a documented performance hint React may discard, and a dropped cache here would re-navigate the live frame — the exact hazard the decision prevents.
**Rejected**: A `src` state set only at first render — goes stale across reachability flips (a remounting iframe would boot into the seed-time folder instead of where the editor last was).
*Introduced by*: 260813-if5d-latch-code-surface-folder; the `followSrc` exception: 260909-kji8-tab-keyed-code-workspace-actions; the rescue `reload()`: 260910-74q0-code-tile-first-boot-rescue-reload

### Two sanctioned re-navigation mechanisms, four triggers
**Decision**: `CodeSurface` may parent-navigate a live frame by exactly two MECHANISMS. (a) The nonce-keyed `followSrc` re-navigation — triggered by the editor's own File > Open Folder (the frame navigates itself to a bare `/code/?folder=<new>`, dropping the workspace identity; `app.tsx` POSTs the `@rk_win_code_root` latch, re-fetches the workspace path for the new root, and re-navigates the live frame to the new `?workspace=` URL via the nonce-keyed `followSrc` prop; a `?folder=` load equal to the current root is not re-navigated) OR by the header's Follow terminal verb (the same latch POST + re-fetch + nonce re-navigation through the shared `requestCodeFollow` wrapper, degrading to `/code/?folder=<gitRoot>` when the re-derivation GET fails). (b) `contentWindow.location.reload()` — triggered by the marker-gated first-boot rescue (a `?workspace=` mount generation whose verdict finds an empty-boot MARKER strictly newer than its baseline — § Code Surface → First-boot rescue) OR by the header's Reload editor verb (carried into `CodeSurface` as the per-window `reloadNonce` prop). Both keep the frame's URL and tab identity; neither writes `src`; nothing else may parent-navigate a live frame.
**Why**: the follow triggers never strand a frame with editor state to lose — the editor-initiated one targets a frame that already navigated itself (and must regain the workspace identity — its `rk.tab`/`rk.server` settings — that the bare `?folder=` form dropped, or the bridge host registers tab-less and the run-kit actions hide), and the verb fires only on an explicit click under visible drift; the rescue targets a frame whose boot loaded zero folders (the bridge extension's marker says nothing was ever edited), and the Reload editor verb reboots a stuck workbench on explicit request. A `reload()` keeps the `?workspace=` URL and tab identity; a `src` write would re-navigate and lose the mount-generation ref discipline.
**Rejected**: leaving a followed frame on `?folder=` (a tab-less host, hidden actions); a `src` rewrite on every code-root change (re-navigates live frames on ordinary latch writes — the mount-generation rule's hazard); a blind rescue reload on every first open (double-boots good boots); inferring the broken boot from the ABSENCE of a new host record (a good boot's record can land later than any fixed window under load — 7 s measured with three concurrent workbenches — so the absence rule false-reloads exactly the boots the rescue exists to spare); degrading a rescued frame to `?folder=` (drops the tab identity the workspace file carries); a blind reload of every retained frame from the verb (destroys state in frames the user did not ask to touch — the `reloadNonce` reaches only the active window's frame).
*Introduced by*: 260909-kji8-tab-keyed-code-workspace-actions; the rescue leg: 260910-74q0-code-tile-first-boot-rescue-reload; marker-gated: 260910-oa3c-code-rescue-positive-signal-record-ownership; the Follow terminal / Reload editor triggers: 260921-c5w2-code-tile-follow-reload-verbs

### Follow terminal is an explicit verb, not a live derivation
**Decision**: The Follow terminal verb is a second WRITER of `@rk_win_code_root` beside File > Open Folder (and `rk tab code set`). It calls the identical follow wrapper (pending-target record → parent callback → latch POST → workspace re-fetch → nonce-keyed `followSrc` re-navigation), so the frame LRU sees a follow, never an eviction, and the `?workspace=` tab identity is preserved. The terminal never moves the editor on its own.
**Why**: Worktree switches (`wt`) leave the tile on the old checkout and File > Open Folder is too buried to be a practical escape. A verb that appears only under drift makes the state visible without moving the editor on ordinary `cd`s.
**Rejected**: A live-derivation follow (moves the editor on every `cd`); a `src` rewrite (P3 hazard — re-navigates on ordinary latch writes); clearing the option so the seed re-fires (the seed fires only on an empty option at first render and races the SSE tick; it also bypasses the LRU's follow bookkeeping).
*Introduced by*: 260921-c5w2-code-tile-follow-reload-verbs

### A shell-initiated follow degrades to `?folder=` on a failed re-derivation
**Decision**: `followFolder(folder, { degradeToFolder: true })` lands the frame on `/code/?folder=<folder>` when the workspace GET fails; the editor-initiated follow keeps the leave-in-place behaviour.
**Why**: The editor-initiated follow targets a frame that already navigated itself to the new folder, so a failed GET costs only tab identity. The verb's frame is still on the OLD folder; without the degrade a failed GET leaves the option moved and the editor stale — a mismatch the latch never had before.
**Rejected**: Rolling the option back on a failed GET (a second write racing the SSE tick, and the terminal's folder is what the user asked for); leaving the mismatch (the verb would appear to do nothing).
*Introduced by*: 260921-c5w2-code-tile-follow-reload-verbs

### At most three point-in-time GETs per mount generation, not a payload field or a watcher
**Decision**: the first-boot rescue's signals are read by point-in-time `GET /api/windows/{windowId}/code-bridge` calls only — a two-stamp baseline at src adoption, a first verdict at the wait's expiry, and exactly one bounded re-check when neither stamp moved: at most three reads per mount generation.
**Why**: the state socket's idle cadence (12 s `safetyPollInterval` + 5 s TTL) cannot observe a record or marker written inside the wait window, so a per-tick payload field would misfire; three requests with no interval and no loop are not polling, and the derivation stays request-time (Constitution II, IX).
