**Rejected**: an `fsnotify` watcher on `cb/` waking the hub (machinery for a once-per-workspace event); widening the wait to ≥ 20 s with a single read (a 20 s empty-editor dwell on every broken boot, and the good-boot confirmation latency doubles for nothing); inferring the verdict from record absence (§ The rescue fires only on a positive empty-boot signal).
*Introduced by*: 260910-74q0-code-tile-first-boot-rescue-reload; the three-read bound: 260910-oa3c-code-rescue-positive-signal-record-ownership

### The rescue fires only on a positive empty-boot signal
**Decision**: the code tile reloads only when the bridge extension itself reports that THIS boot activated with zero folders (an empty-boot marker newer than the mount baseline); the absence of a host record is never a trigger.
**Why**: a good boot's record lands only after the remote extension host activates, which under load takes longer than any fixed window (7 s measured with three concurrent workbenches), so absence cannot distinguish "slow" from "broken" — and a false reload is exactly the blind reload the rescue exists to avoid. The extension provably runs `activate()` on the broken boot, so it can name the failure directly.
**Rejected**: widening the fixed window (every extra second is a longer broken-boot dwell and still no guarantee); polling until a record appears (an interval loop on a UI path); keeping the absence rule with a re-check only (still infers from silence).
*Introduced by*: 260910-oa3c-code-rescue-positive-signal-record-ownership

### One bounded re-check, not a fixed single read
**Decision**: the verdict reads at 10 s and, when neither stamp has moved, once more at 20 s, then settles — at most three reads per mount generation.
**Why**: the marker is written by the same slow extension-host activation as the record, so a single read at 10 s would miss the broken boot under load; one re-check covers the measured latency with margin while keeping the per-generation cost bounded and interval-free.
**Rejected**: an interval until a signal appears (open-ended client polling); a single read at 20 s (doubles the good-boot confirmation latency for nothing).
*Introduced by*: 260910-oa3c-code-rescue-positive-signal-record-ownership

### The waiting-badge click advances, never restarts
**Decision**: `handleWaitingBadgeClick` resolves its destination through `nextWaitingTarget(ordered, server, windowParam)` (`lib/palette/agent-nav.ts`) — the same helper the `Agent: Next waiting` palette action uses — rather than scanning the session's windows itself.
**Why**: the badge means "the next waiting window", relative to where the viewer already is. Picking the session's FIRST waiting window makes the badge a silent no-op whenever the viewer is already on that window, instead of advancing to the next one.
**Rejected**: a local `windows.find(isWaiting)` scan — re-implements the palette's ordering (drift risk) and dead-ends on the first waiting window.
*Introduced by*: 260717-vhvz

### Scale wrapper over guest-document CSS zoom
**Decision**: web-tile content zoom is a transform-scale the iframe engine applies to its own element with compensated width/height (rendered at `100%/s`, scaled back by `s`, origin 0 0), the chrome owning the level and handing it down as the engine's `zoom` prop.
**Why**: works for all four address kinds including cross-origin `external`; reproduces browser-zoom semantics (zooming in shrinks the frame's CSS viewport, so responsive guest layouts adapt); zero reach into guest documents — and an engine without a document element to scale simply applies the level its own way.
**Rejected**: injecting CSS `zoom` into the guest document — same-origin only, and touching proxied content re-opens the proxy-rewrite class of bugs (the stale Content-Length scar).
*Introduced by*: 260823-cwvv-tile-content-zoom-gestures

### Content zoom owns "Zoom"; the tile-maximize verb is Expand/Restore
**Decision**: "Zoom" names content magnification everywhere (the web tile's URL-bar control, the `Web: Zoom *` palette actions); the tile-maximize verb's user-facing strings are Expand/Restore (`Layout: Expand`/`Layout: Restore`, the tile-header Tip/aria-labels, the `zen-toggle` binding description) while its ids (`layout-zoom`/`layout-unzoom`), the `ZoomGlyph`/`data-icon="zoom"` seam, and the `zoomed`/`zoomToggleRef` internals keep the zoom vocabulary.
**Why**: two different "Zoom"s in one tile header/palette is a vocabulary collision, and the browser muscle-memory meaning of zoom is content magnification; ids persist in user macros/overrides, so only labels may move.
**Rejected**: per-surface content-zoom names with the tile-maximize verb keeping "Zoom" — leaves "zoom" meaning maximize, fighting every browser's meaning of the word.
*Introduced by*: 260823-cwvv-tile-content-zoom-gestures

### Gestures zoom continuously; only click/shortcut zoom steps the ladder
**Decision**: web-tile gesture zoom is continuous — `s *= exp(-deltaY * 0.01)` per ctrl/meta wheel event (`applyWheelZoom`), `s = base * e.scale` per Safari `gesturechange` — while buttons and palette actions keep the discrete ladder.
**Why**: a pinch that clicks between ladder stops reads as broken next to Chrome/macOS, where pinch tracks the fingers and only Cmd+± steps; the exponential mapping multiplies equal deltas equally, so in/out feel symmetric and a reversed gesture returns to its start.
**Rejected**: shrinking the step ladder (finer quantization) — still visibly clicks; linear `s += k*deltaY` — asymmetric feel, zoom-out crawls near the low bound.
*Introduced by*: 260824-iafo-web-tile-continuous-gesture-zoom

### Read returns the float; only stepping snaps
**Decision**: `readWebZoom` returns the stored float clamped to the continuous bounds; the ladder snap lives solely in `stepWebZoom`, the click/shortcut path.
**Why**: a reload must restore a gesture-set value exactly; snapping belongs to the one path that needs a ladder index.
**Rejected**: read-time snapping — a 137% pinch would reload as 125%, silently moving the user's zoom.
*Introduced by*: 260824-iafo-web-tile-continuous-gesture-zoom

### Viewer-owned figure zoom, independent of tile zoom
**Decision**: Diagram magnification in the present viewer lives inside the viewer shell as a CSS transform on the SVG within a fitted, clipping, pannable figure; the tile's content zoom is untouched and composes on top.
**Why**: The tile zoom shrinks the frame viewport and scales it back, so a fit-to-width SVG re-fits and never magnifies — at the 300% cap a wide scene is still fully visible, only sharper. A viewer-owned transform gives arbitrary crisp magnification with no re-render, no dependency, no route, no setting.
**Rejected**: Raising `WEB_ZOOM_MAX` (no effect on re-fitting content and changes every page's tile zoom); dropping the SVG `max-width: 100%` (wide scenes overflow at rest and lose the at-a-glance fit); the interactive `@excalidraw/excalidraw` viewer (React plus a large chunk in a deliberately plain-TypeScript shell — held until selection/editing is wanted); tile-injected guest zoom (cross-origin-unsafe).
*Introduced by*: 260909-pdow-viewer-figure-pan-zoom

### The viewer claims ctrl-wheel over figures at window capture; the tile keeps the rest
**Decision**: One viewer-wide `window`-capture wheel/gesture arm handles only events whose target lies inside a `.viewer-figure`, stopping propagation; plain keys (`+ - 0`, arrows) drive keyboard zoom and pan, never modifier chords.
**Why**: DOM capture order is window → document → target and the tile attaches its arm at the frame's `document` on every load, so window capture is the one pre-emption point needing no change in `iframe-window.tsx`. Every tile chord is on a modifier tier, so unmodified keys are never reclaimed from the frame, while `⌘0`/`⌘=`/`⌘−` belong to the browser and the desktop shell.
**Rejected**: A yield hook in `iframe-window.tsx` (couples the tile to guest markup); letting both arms zoom (the tile would zoom on every figure gesture); `⌘`/`Ctrl` key variants (collide with shell and browser zoom).
*Introduced by*: 260909-pdow-viewer-figure-pan-zoom

### One `IframeWindow` chrome, N URL-keyed engines
**Decision**: the web tile is a single `IframeWindow` chrome component that mounts one ENGINE per tab — `<Engine key={`${engineKind}:${tabUrl}`}>` against the `WebFrameEngine` contract, the tab's content hidden when inactive, never unmounted by a selection change — with per-frame state held inside the engine, reported into the chrome's URL-keyed `FrameChromeState` map, and the chrome bound to the ACTIVE frame's entry.
**Why**: P3 (hide, never unmount): dev-server pages, HMR sessions, and half-filled forms keep their in-page state across tab switches, which a single re-`src`d iframe loses on every switch. One chrome keeps exactly one receiver for the `web-find:open` / `web-address:focus` / `web-open-external` / `web-zoom` document CustomEvents and one URL bar / find bar / zoom control DOM set. Keying by URL means a middle close's slot renumber remounts nothing (identity is the URL); index keys would remount every surviving frame on a `WebRemove` shift, and stable client-side ids would be a second identity the spec deliberately avoids.
**Rejected**: N stacked `IframeWindow` instances (duplicated chrome, N receivers for every document CustomEvent — each would need listener gating — and zoom buckets re-seeding per instance); index keys (remount storm on a middle close); lazy frame mount or LRU eviction (P3 defers eviction; the family cap of 16 bounds the eager-mount cost).
*Introduced by*: 260828-9kip-web-tab-strip

### The chrome renders per capability, never per origin
**Decision**: `IframeWindow` gates every content-dependent control on the mounted engine's reported `FrameChromeState.supports.*` (plus `canGoBack`/`canGoForward` and `find`); the word `crossOrigin` exists only inside the iframe engine, where it derives the capability flags and selects the reload strategy.
**Why**: engines answer per feature, not per origin — a renderer that does not embed has no meaningful origin answer, so an origin probe at the chrome level misreports there (an embeddability probe answers an iframe-specific question — X-Frame-Options — and would paint "refuses embedding" over content another renderer shows fine). Capability flags are the one axis every engine can answer.
**Rejected**: `if (engine === …)` branches in the chrome (duplicates every control's logic per engine); keeping `crossOrigin` as a pseudo-capability on the reported state (the wrong axis — it conflates "not same-origin to this parent" with "cannot do history/find/meta").
*Introduced by*: 260916-fxt1-web-frame-engine-seam

### Probes are engine-owned
**Decision**: the frame-check refusal probe, the proxied-port 502 fetch, and the same-origin location/document reads live in `web-frame-iframe.tsx` and nowhere else; the chrome consumes only the reported `tileError`/`trackedLocation`/`title`/`favicon`.
**Why**: each probe answers an iframe-specific question (can this origin be embedded; does the proxied upstream listen; is the document readable); on another engine the question is meaningless or answered by the engine's own events, so a chrome-level probe can only misreport there.
**Rejected**: a chrome-level probe gated per engine kind — the gate is one more per-engine branch in the chrome, and the probe's answer is still engine-shaped.
*Introduced by*: 260916-fxt1-web-frame-engine-seam

### Find is an engine command with a chrome-owned query
**Decision**: the engine handle carries `find(query, { forward, findNext })` / `stopFind()` (the Electron `findInPage` / `stopFindInPage` shape); the engine owns matches, the active index, and highlights and reports `find: { active, total } | null`; the chrome owns only `findOpen` and `findQuery` and resets the query on the active frame's `onLoad`.
**Why**: the iframe engine's Highlight-API machinery reaches into the frame document, which only the engine may touch; an engine with a native find maps onto the same `{ active, total }` report shape, so the find bar never learns which engine serves it.
**Rejected**: leaving match state in the chrome and passing the frame document out through the handle (re-couples the chrome to `contentDocument`).
*Introduced by*: 260916-fxt1-web-frame-engine-seam

### `onLoad` is a callback edge, not a state level
**Decision**: the engine props carry `onLoad(url)` beside `onState`; the chrome uses it to reset its find query on the active frame's loads.
**Why**: "a navigation happened" is an edge the chrome must react to once; encoding it as a state field (a load counter, or `find` flipping to null) would be either a synthetic level or ambiguous with `stopFind`.
**Rejected**: a `loadCount` field on `FrameChromeState`; deriving the reset from `find` becoming `null`.
*Introduced by*: 260916-fxt1-web-frame-engine-seam

### Engine selection is bridge presence × viewer preference
**Decision**: `selectWebEngineKind(canShellWeb(), nativeEnabled)` — the `web` group's own presence gates the native engine, and a per-viewer localStorage boolean (default on) can turn it off.
**Why**: The bridge group's presence is the truthful signal that the shell can host guests (an older shell reports `version`/`platform` but no `web`); a per-viewer flag is Constitution IV's home for viewer state and gives an escape hatch while the engine matures.
**Rejected**: Gating on `isShell()` (a second, less accurate signal); a settings-registry key (a per-instance preference for a per-viewer concern, and a new registry surface).
*Introduced by*: 260916-q2xk-web-frame-native-engine

### Sash drags live-resize the native guest; tile moves hide it
**Decision**: The `TileDragContext` posture selects the engine's mid-drag behavior: a sash or intersection drag (`resize`) sends deduped bounds on every animation frame and never hides the guest; a tile header drag (`move`) hides the guest for the drag's duration (`wantVisible` false), re-measuring and re-showing on the return to `idle`.
**Why**: rAF bounds hold the guest within 1–2 px of the tile edge with no lag, so a hide-then-restore would flash the card on every sash drag for no gain; a tile move's overlay previews the result tree and the guest is composited above the DOM, so the overlay cannot paint over it — hiding is the only way the preview is visible.
**Rejected**: Hide-while-dragging for sash drags (a visible flash per drag); throttling bounds below frame rate (visible lag); one boolean for both postures (either the overlay is occluded or the sash drag loses live resize).
*Introduced by*: 260916-q2xk-web-frame-native-engine; move posture: 260925-c1ql-surface-drag-snap

### The relay subscription is disposed with the engine
**Decision**: `onShellWebEvent` always returns a disposer and the engine's mount-effect cleanup calls it; events are demuxed by `tabKey` before any state update.
**Why**: In the spike a subscription that could not be dropped leaked one listener per engine mount, re-dispatched each chord N times, and toggled the palette shut.
**Rejected**: One module-level singleton listener fanning out over a tabKey map (hides the leak instead of preventing it and outlives the component tree).
*Introduced by*: 260916-q2xk-web-frame-native-engine

### The drag posture crosses to the engine as a context, not an engine prop
**Decision**: `TileDragContext` (a `TileDragPosture` — `"idle" | "resize" | "move"`) provided by `SurfaceLayout`, read by the native engine via `useTileDragPosture()`.
**Why**: The posture is a layout fact only one engine consumes; threading it through `IframeWindow` and `WebFrameEngineProps` would make the iframe engine carry a prop it ignores.
**Rejected**: Widening `WebFrameEngineProps` with a drag field (contract noise for one consumer).
*Introduced by*: 260916-q2xk-web-frame-native-engine; posture: 260925-c1ql-surface-drag-snap

### Capabilities are honest per engine; an undrivable control is hidden, never dead
**Decision**: every engine reports exactly the capabilities it can drive through its handle (`WEB_FRAME_IFRAME_DEFAULT_CAPABILITIES` / `WEB_FRAME_NATIVE_DEFAULT_CAPABILITIES` for the pre-report first paint, plus the iframe engine's same-origin derivation), and the chrome renders per capability — a control the mounted engine cannot drive is hidden or disabled, never rendered as a no-op.
**Why**: capability flags are the one axis every engine can answer (→ The chrome renders per capability, never per origin); a seeded-true flag paints a button whose verb no-ops, which reads as broken chrome rather than an absent feature.
**Rejected**: optimistic capabilities with no-op verbs (a rendered control that does nothing); origin-derived flags reported by an engine that does not embed (no meaningful origin answer).
*Introduced by*: 260916-q2xk-web-frame-native-engine

### The chord table is the reclaim predicate, enumerated
**Decision**: the SPA enumerates `hasReclaimableMatch`'s kind-`web` answer over the binding registry into a per-guest table (`buildWebChordTable`), and main matches `before-input-event` against it with exact modifier equality; the engine re-dispatches without re-consulting the predicate.
**Why**: a guest's keydowns never reach the SPA document, so the predicate cannot run at event time; enumerating it keeps the registry the single authority (a rebind moves both engines) and keeps main dumb and testable.
**Rejected**: a hard-coded chord list in main (drifts from the registry); relaying every keydown to the SPA for the predicate to judge (a round-trip per keystroke, and the page would already have handled it).
*Introduced by*: 260917-w0k7-web-native-parity

### Zoom re-applies on every navigation
**Decision**: the native engine sends `web:zoom` after create, on every `zoom` prop change, and on every `url` relay; `zoom-changed` steps the chrome's bucket through `onZoomStep`.
**Why**: Chromium's per-host zoom store inside the partition persists and leaks across views; the SPA's localStorage buckets are the source of truth for both engines.
**Rejected**: letting Chromium own zoom (fights the bucket across hosts); applying `zoom-changed` main-side (two writers).
*Introduced by*: 260917-w0k7-web-native-parity

### Guest errors map SPA-side from code plus HTTP status
**Decision**: `lib/web-native-errors.ts` maps `did-fail-load` codes and `did-navigate`'s `httpResponseCode` to `TileError` using `classifyAddress`/`proxyPortOf`; the `refused` kind is unreachable on this engine.
**Why**: address kind is SPA knowledge, and a dead proxied port is an HTTP 502 from the Go reverse proxy, not a Chromium load failure — a code-only mapping would miss the common case.
**Rejected**: a main-side mapping (main does not know address kinds); a same-origin 502 probe fetch (Chromium already made the request).
*Introduced by*: 260917-w0k7-web-native-parity

### Client selects after add
**Decision**: after a selected draft's Enter resolves `onAddTab` to the server's `{index}`, the component calls `onSelectTab(index)`.
**Why**: the backend's add verb sets `@rk_win_web_active` only when the family is empty ("add" is not "show" — idempotent adds must not yank an existing view); materializing a selected UI draft carries user intent to look at the resolved tab.
**Rejected**: a server-side `?show=1` flag on the add route (a backend change, out of scope for a frontend change; the verb routes' select-only-on-empty contract is already shipped).
*Introduced by*: 260828-9kip-web-tab-strip

### Address bar replaces URL-shaped input; paths and drafts go through add
**Decision**: Enter on the address bar writes `@rk_win_web_<active>` for URL-shaped input when a declared tab is selected; path/port-shaped input (relative paths, `./`, bare `:NNNN`, dotted single names) ALWAYS submits through the idempotent add verb (append-or-focus), as does a draft's Enter.
**Why**: browser semantics for URLs — typing a URL never silently grows a tab strip — while a pasted repo path is an attach gesture: the add verb's target-identity match makes a repeat focus rather than duplicate, and a path paste never navigates the current tab in place.
**Rejected**: routing every address-bar submit through the add verb (typing a URL would grow the family on every submit — surprising, and idempotent adds of the same URL would make repeats indistinguishable from edits); pre-rejecting path input in the frontend regex (the backend resolver was built for exactly this input — `rk present docs/foo.html` already accepts it).
*Introduced by*: 260828-9kip-web-tab-strip; path/port lanes: 260908-ewud-address-bar-path-targets

### The backend stat decides dotted single names; 400 falls back to https
**Decision**: A dotted single segment (`README.md`, `example.com`) submits to the add verb first; an `ApiError` with status 400 retries once, silently, as `https://{name}` through the write flow. The fallback keys on the HTTP status, never on the error message text.
**Why**: only a stat can tell a repo file from a domain — an eager `https://` rewrite sends `README.md` to Moldova's TLD; the status code is a stable contract while error wording is not.
**Rejected**: substring-matching "does not exist" in the response body (brittle against wording changes); a frontend file-extension allowlist (still guessing what only the filesystem knows).
*Introduced by*: 260908-ewud-address-bar-path-targets

### Detected-port `+ :port` chip is deferred (declared-only)
**Decision**: the web tab strip ships with NO detected-port add chip; every add goes through a declared address (the address-bar draft, the `rk present`-shaped target forms).
**Why**: the chip assumed a per-window `ports` field on the Window payload, which does not exist — the host-global services list has no window attribution (backend pane-pid → process-tree → port join, tracked in `fab/backlog.md`). Declared-only keeps the add path honest instead of guessing at ports.
**Rejected**: consuming the host-global `useHostServices()` list un-attributed (wrong-window chips); shipping a per-window join inside this frontend-only change.
*Introduced by*: 260828-9kip-web-tab-strip

### No chord reaches a lens switch
**Decision**: On desktop the palette `View: Terminal/Web/Code` actions are the only single-tile lens-switch surface; no keyboard chord cycles or selects a lens, and the `View:` entries carry no shortcut hint (`buildViewActions(available, resolved, onSwitch)` emits `shortcut: ""`). `Period` (⌘./Ctrl+.) is deliberately unbound ([keyboard-and-palette](/run-kit/ui/keyboard-and-palette.md) § Design Decisions → `Period` is deliberately unbound).
**Why**: the tile toggles (⌘1/⌘2/⌘3/⌘4) and `View:` entries already cover every lens; a reflex-hit cycle chord added only the footgun of landing users on a lens they did not intend.
**Rejected**: hinting the tile-toggle digits on `View:` entries (toggle semantics ≠ lens switch).
*Introduced by*: 260826-sfsv-remove-cmd-dot-view-cycle

### `--show` grows the layout through the shared `Add` verb
**Decision**: when `web` is absent from the layout, `rk tab web add --show` (and `rk present`, its sugar) grows `@rk_win_layout` through `layoutspec.Add` — the last leaf splits along its longer axis. With no tile cap, Add serves every layout, so there is no full-layout fallback path.
**Why**: the show path must succeed on every layout — `rk present` never fails to open the tile.
**Rejected**: exit 1 on a crowded layout (regresses `present`); replacing the last leaf on a "full" layout (there is no full state — the size floor gates viewer offers only, and the CLI has no viewport).
*Introduced by*: 260829-c143-rk-tab-cli-present-sugar; uncapped Add: 260925-jbz0-surface-cross-tab-tiles

### Root chrome reaches the current window's layout through a cancelable `rk:help-topic` window event
**Decision**: `openHelpTopic` dispatches a cancelable `rk:help-topic` `window` CustomEvent; `AppShell` handles it in-tile while a window is current — cancelling synchronously, then add tab → ensure a web surface → select tab — and the dispatcher falls back to a browser tab whenever nobody cancels.
**Why**: the chevron menu and the global palette hook mount at the root with no access to `layout`, `applyLayout`, `switchToTile`, or the current window; those live in `AppShell`, which exists only on a terminal route. One helper serves both entry points without lifting layout state to the root, and cancel-or-fallback makes board/server/host routes correct with no listener at all. It is the codebase's established boundary-crossing idiom (`rk:quake-terminal`, `palette:open`, `HOST_MENU_OPEN_EVENT`, `WEB_FIND_OPEN_EVENT`).
**Rejected**: duplicating the topic actions in `AppShell`'s own palette list (two providers would emit the same ids in terminal mode); lifting layout state into a context solely for this (a larger refactor than the feature).
*Introduced by*: 260910-58al-help-topics-submenu

### The help-topic path does not yield a slot when the layout has no room
**Decision**: when the current layout cannot grow a `web` tile (no split fits the size floor), the frontend help-topic handler still adds the web tab but performs NO layout write and opens the page in a browser tab (`window.open`, `noopener,noreferrer`).
**Why**: the frontend's no-room signal is `addSurface(layout, "web") === null` — the same predicate that disables the surface toggles and the `Tile: Show Web` entry — and the path reuses it rather than adding a client-side replace-a-tile mutation; the intake requires falling through to the browser-tab fallback rather than silently no-oping; adding the tab first keeps the page one strip-click away once the layout has room.
**Rejected**: a silent no-op after adding the tab (the user clicked and nothing visibly happened); evicting a tile the viewer arranged for a documentation page.
*Introduced by*: 260910-58al-help-topics-submenu; the floor as the no-room signal: 260925-jbz0-surface-cross-tab-tiles

### Grid key is server-only; per-window state resets by effect
**Decision**: `SurfaceLayout` is keyed by `server`; transient per-window state (hide-never-unmount set, zoom, focused leaf, web page title, tty progress, find state) resets in one `[server, windowId]` effect guarded against first mount.
**Why**: the tty tile must survive a same-session switch so the relay's same-session ride and the deferred-reset/clear designs apply ([terminal](/run-kit/ui/terminal.md) § Terminal Write Batching); the sizes reset already follows the effect pattern.
**Rejected**: hoisting `TerminalClient` above the keyed boundary (more prop plumbing, two ownership seams for the same tile) — recorded as the fallback if the reset effect proves too entangled.
*Introduced by*: 260910-0awz-tab-switch-persistent-terminal

### Web/gui tiles remount per window via their key; tty and code keys are window-independent
**Decision**: web/gui tile wrappers carry `windowId` in their React key; the tty tile wrapper's key is window-independent, and the code tile's key is its frame record (`code:<windowId>:<creation src>`) — both survive a same-server window switch.
**Why**: web/gui content identity changes with the window and each has mount-once bookkeeping (per-url iframes, RFB session). The code frame's content identity is its workspace, not the route window, and its persistence is deliberate and bounded — the retention LRU with its own eviction triggers (§ Design Decisions → Code frames are retained across window switches by an LRU inside `SurfaceLayout`); the frame's window id rides in the key because the `?folder=` degrade src is folder-keyed, so src alone is not unique across same-folder windows.
**Rejected**: persisting the web/gui tiles and re-deriving their content by prop — a much larger change for no user-visible gain.
*Introduced by*: 260910-0awz-tab-switch-persistent-terminal; the code tile's persistence: 260916-az44-persistent-code-frames-lru

### The code lens keys on the derived folder — git toplevel when found, else the pane's raw cwd
**Decision**: the per-window availability half is `gitRoot`, derived in `FetchSessions` from the active pane's cwd (else first pane's, else the worktree path) via `config.FindGitRoot` — a filesystem stat-walk, no subprocess — falling back to that resolved cwd itself when no `.git` ancestor is found; the field is empty only when no cwd is resolvable at all.
**Why**: code-server keys browser workspace state on the `?folder=` value, so editor state follows the code — agents `cd` constantly, and two windows on one worktree deliberately share one editor state. Outside any repo there is no shared toplevel to key on, so each window keys on its own raw cwd (two non-git windows on different cwds get distinct editor state; two on the same cwd share) — the same "toplevel, else cwd" resolution the CLI's `rk code` applies per invocation.
**Rejected**: keying by window id (editor state would reset on every window recreation); keying by raw cwd everywhere (state would fragment across subdirectories of one repo — raw cwd is the fallback only where no git toplevel exists); a synthetic shared root for all non-git windows (would conflate unrelated directories' editor state).
*Introduced by*: 260811-k3vp-right-panel-code-lens; cwd fallback: 260907-a482-code-server-outside-git-root

### The code lens widens `deriveGitRoot`'s derivation rather than changing `config.FindGitRoot` or adding a field
**Decision**: the non-git case is a fallback branch inside `deriveGitRoot` — return the resolved cwd when `FindGitRoot` finds no `.git` ancestor — with the `GitRoot`/`gitRoot` field name and JSON key unchanged; `config.FindGitRoot`'s `""`-means-not-in-a-repo semantics are untouched.
**Why**: the frontend-facing `Window.GitRoot` has exactly one consumer (the code lens), so widening its derivation carries no cross-feature risk, while `FindGitRoot` is a shared primitive whose empty return gates unrelated features (`api/fork.go`, `api/closed.go`, `cmd/rk/operator.go`, `cmd/rk/riff.go`, `cmd/rk/tutorial.go`); the CLI's `codeTargetFolder` already ships the same "toplevel, else cwd" pattern, so the fallback mirrors an accepted precedent.
**Rejected**: changing `FindGitRoot`'s return semantics (silently changes every other call site's behavior); introducing a separate `workspaceRoot` field or renaming `gitRoot` (single-consumer field — a rename would ripple across the e2e/unit fixtures that reference the key for no behavioral benefit).
*Introduced by*: 260907-a482-code-server-outside-git-root

### Code frames are retained across window switches by an LRU inside `SurfaceLayout`
**Decision**: the `code` tile drops `windowId` from its React key (identity = the frame record, `code:<windowId>:<creation src>` — the window id rides along because the `?folder=` degrade src is folder-keyed), and `SurfaceLayout` holds a per-server ordered list of `{windowId, src, root}` frame records, most-recently-shown last, rendering non-active ones display-hidden (`hidden` class, never `visibility`/off-screen) through the same flat `allTiles` list and evicting on overflow (`CODE_FRAME_CAP_DESKTOP = 3` / `CODE_FRAME_CAP_MOBILE = 1`, the cap counting the active frame; an `isMobile` cap decrease evicts down immediately), the window leaving `liveWindowIds`, a non-follow root divergence, or a `codeReachable` `true→false` flip (all frames drop). A record's `src` is fixed at creation as its identity; a follow moves only the record's `root` baseline in place. The retention state is in-memory viewer state — nothing is persisted (Constitution IV).
**Why**: without retention a window switch unmounts the iframe — a page unload: VS Code web disconnects gracefully and code-server kills the extension host — so every switch re-boots the workbench (measured 2026-09-16, warm cache, idle box: 1.4 s to `.monaco-workbench`, ~2 s to explorer rows, ~2.9 s to the SCM status item; 2.2/2.5/4.1 s cold; 7 s extension host under load) while a display-hidden frame re-shows in 16 ms (prototype-measured). Each live workbench is one ~250–320 MB extension-host process on the server plus a browser renderer, so retention must be bounded. Mount bookkeeping already lives in the renderer (zoom, `everOpened`), and the grid is keyed by server, so the list is per-server for free. Nothing on the request path was the lever (warm network is 3 KB; rk's derivation GETs are ~2 ms; spawn flags do not affect boot).
**Rejected**: reconnecting a new iframe document to the old code-server connection (the unload disconnect is unconditional; the reconnection token lives only in the dying renderer's memory); one shared frame navigating between workspaces (a workspace switch is a full workbench reload — the same cost as a remount); unbounded retention; cache/proxy tuning; a palette evict verb (eviction stays payload-driven — the shipped `Code: Reload Editor` palette row reloads the ACTIVE window's frame in place through the `reloadNonce` seam and touches no retention record).
*Introduced by*: 260916-az44-persistent-code-frames-lru; the reload-verb note: 260921-c5w2-code-tile-follow-reload-verbs

### The rescue/bridge fetcher is bound per frame
**Decision**: `fetchBridgeStatus` reaches each `CodeSurface` as a fetcher bound to that frame's window id — `app.tsx`'s `fetchBridgeStatusFor(windowId)` factory, threaded through `SurfaceLayout` and resolved per frame record — not the active window's fetcher.
**Why**: `CodeSurface` reads the fetcher ref at verdict time (10 s and 20 s after load); with cross-window retention a frame can outlive its window's active period, so an active-window fetcher would read the wrong tab's bridge status.
**Rejected**: keeping the single active-window fetcher (silent wrong-window verdicts).
*Introduced by*: 260916-az44-persistent-code-frames-lru

### A follow's pending target is recorded synchronously before the latch POST
**Decision**: the frame's `onFolderNavigated` wrapper writes the pending-follow target (`{windowId, root}`) into a ref synchronously with the navigation report, before the parent's `@rk_win_code_root` latch POST; the root-divergence eviction treats a divergence TOWARD the pending target as the follow itself (baseline moves in place, target clears), and the nonce-adoption effect covers the opposite tick ordering. The slot is window-scoped and a stale target can only adopt when the frame already shows that folder.
**Why**: the follow's own option write wakes the SSE hub, so the payload tick carrying the new `codeRoot` can outrun the POST response + re-derivation GET that produce the follow nonce; a nonce-only baseline update loses that ordering and reads the follow as an external root change, evicting the live frame it was meant to keep. A failed latch POST leaves the payload unmoved, so nothing diverges and nothing evicts. Known bound: the target is a single slot, so two editor-initiated follows across a window switch inside one SSE round trip overwrite the first — that frame degrades to an evict + re-boot at the correct root, never a wrong baseline.
**Rejected**: moving the baseline only on nonce adoption (the real event ordering races it — verified in review); a per-window pending-target map (closes the single-slot bound for a case that degrades safely).
*Introduced by*: 260916-az44-persistent-code-frames-lru

### Popout is a viewer posture, never a layout write
**Decision**: the opener hides the popped leaf only in its own render via localStorage `rk-layout-popped:{server}:{@N}`; `@rk_win_layout` is untouched by pop-out and pop-in, and the two windows coordinate over a same-origin BroadcastChannel.
**Why**: popping a tile out on one machine must not rearrange the tab for other viewers or agents reading the shared layout (Constitution II/IV per-viewer state lives in localStorage).
**Rejected**: writing popout into `@rk_win_layout` — hides the tile for every viewer.
*Introduced by*: 260925-3v9l-surface-popout

### Popped leaves are keyed by leaf id
**Decision**: the `?pop=` value and popped-set entries are `leafIds()` ids (`tty`, `tty#2`, `code`, `@12/tty`).
**Why**: the popout keys by leaf address, not kind; duplicate bare tty tiles are legal, and `leafIds` is the existing stable id.
**Rejected**: keying by kind — cannot distinguish a bare `tty` from `@12/tty`.
*Introduced by*: 260925-3v9l-surface-popout

### Drag and templates pause while a tile is popped
**Decision**: header drag-to-snap, the sidebar row-drag borrow, and the ▦ template cycle (chip, chord, and `Layout: <Template>` palette rows) are disabled while this viewer has a leaf of this layout popped.
**Why**: a drop or template resolved on the reduced tree would drop the popped leaf from every viewer's shared layout.
**Rejected**: re-inserting the popped leaf into the drop result (surprising positions, more code); auto pop-in on drag start.
*Introduced by*: 260925-3v9l-surface-popout
